1. What is Aariya's current TTS engine?
Which one are you actually running right now?
- ElevenLabs
- XTTS-v2 / Coqui
- Piper
- OpenAI TTS
- Edge TTS
- Flutter TTS
- another local model
- multiple providers/fallbacks
And if ElevenLabs, are you using their API or something local?
2. Where does Aariya run?
What is the main target?
- MacBook
- Windows PC
- Linux PC
- Unity desktop app
- Web browser
- mobile
- combination
And what GPU/RAM does the machine running Aariya have?

This matters because I would design the voice-performance layer differently for CPU-only vs GPU inference.
3. How realistic do you want the voice?
Pick the closest:

A — Natural assistant
“Sure. Give me a second.”

Very natural, but restrained.

B — FRIDAY-like
Intelligent, composed, subtle emotion, occasional dry amusement, highly responsive.

C — Human-level conversational performance

Aariya can:
- hesitate naturally
- breathe
- change rhythm mid-sentence
- emphasize individual words
- lower her voice when appropriate
- become excited without sounding exaggerated
- sound uncertain when she actually is uncertain
- sound amused
- sound concerned
- interrupt herself
- recover from mistakes
- change delivery based on the meaning of a sentence
- carry emotional continuity from previous turns
D — C

That's the direction I would recommend for Aariya.
4. Do you want emotion to be generated before the sentence is spoken?
For example, instead of:
LLM → text → TTS

I want to move Aariya toward:
LLM
 ↓
Intent / Meaning
 ↓
Internal Cognitive State
 ↓
Voice Director
 ↓
Performance Plan
 ↓
TTS

Where the Voice Director decides something like:
{    "emotion": "quiet_amusement",    "energy": 0.42,    "warmth": 0.71,    "confidence": 0.89,    "pace": 0.94,    "pitch": 0.98,    "pause_density": 0.18,    "emphasis": ["really"],    "breathiness": 0.08,    "ending": "soft",    "micro_pause": True}


rather than simply:
emotion = "happy"pitch = 1.3rate = 1.1


Your existing architecture is already moving toward this continuous-state approach.    *voice TTS
5. Do you want Aariya to have voice memory / continuity?
For example:

User:
“You really think that's a good idea?”

Aariya:
“...Honestly?”

Then the user says:
“Yes.”

Aariya shouldn't reset to a neutral TTS preset.

She should maintain a temporary performance state:
previous emotional state
        ↓
current thought
        ↓
new emotional state
        ↓
smooth transition
        ↓
voice

So her voice can gradually move from:
neutral → amused → skeptical → serious

instead of:
neutral → RANDOM_PRESET

Your existing previous_pitch, previous_rate, and previous_energy concept is a good foundation for this.    *voice TTS
6. Do you want Aariya to have non-verbal vocal behavior?
This is one of the biggest differences between ordinary TTS and what you're describing.

For example:
"mm..."
"well..."
"wait..."
"oh."
"right."
"hm."

along with:
- tiny breaths
- thinking pauses
- sentence restarts
- short hesitation
- soft laugh-like vocalization
- changing volume within a sentence
- emphasis
- trailing endings
- deliberate silence
But these should be generated based on context, not randomly inserted.

2. voice_state.py
This stores Aariya’s current expressive condition. It avoids switching abruptly between artificial preset voices and retains compatibility with your existing numeric state fields.
from dataclasses import dataclass, field    confidence: float = 0.70    playfulness: float = 0.20    concern: float = 0.05    expressiveness: float = 0.60    restraint: float = 0.40    # Expressiveness is gated separately from intelligence.    trust: float = 0.50    contradiction_penalty: float = 0.0    conversation_momentum: float = 0.25    tension: float = 0.0    # Baseline controls; final mapping belongs to the TTS adapter.    pitch: float = 1.0    rate: float = 1.0    volume: float = 1.0    speaking: bool = False    updated_at: float = field(default_factory=monotonic)    _NORMALIZED_FIELDS: ClassVar[set[str]] = {        "energy",        "warmth",        "confidence",        "playfulness",        "concern",        "expressiveness",        "restraint",        "trust",        "contradiction_penalty",        "conversation_momentum",        "tension",    }    def update(self, **values: Any) -> None:        """Update known fields without allowing invalid values through."""        for key, value in values.items():            if key == "speaking" and isinstance(value, bool):                self.speaking = value                continue            if (                key not in self._NORMALIZED_FIELDS                and key not in {"pitch", "rate", "volume"}            ):                continue            try:                number = float(value)            except (TypeError, ValueError):                continue            if key in self._NORMALIZED_FIELDS:                number = clamp(number)            elif key == "pitch":                number = clamp(number, 0.80, 1.20)            elif key == "rate":                number = clamp(number, 0.80, 1.20)            elif key == "volume":                number = clamp(number, 0.50, 1.50)            setattr(self, key, number)        self.updated_at = monotonic()



3. conversation_context.py
Your existing conversation understanding system should populate this with actual contextual signals. Do not guess the user's feelings from facial appearance.
from dataclasses import dataclassdef _clamp(    value: float,    low: float = 0.0,    high: float = 1.0,) -> float:    return max(low, min(high, float(value)))@dataclassclass ConversationContext:    """Signals from Aariya's existing context and conversation systems."""    # -1.0 means negative valence; +1.0 means positive valence.    user_valence: float = 0.0    # 0.0 means calm; 1.0 means highly activated.    user_arousal: float = 0.0    topic_emotionality: float = 0.0    urgency: float = 0.0    user_speaking_rate: float = 1.0    is_question: bool = False    is_correction: bool = False    is_joke: bool = False    is_personal: bool = False    conversation_momentum: float = 0.25    conversation_tension: float = 0.0    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        self.user_valence = _clamp(            self.user_valence, -1.0, 1.0



4. voice_personality.py
Personality should remain relatively stable. Aariya's momentary concern, amusement, urgency, and confidence can change without changing her underlying identity.
from dataclasses import dataclassdef _clamp(value: float) -> float:    return max(0.0, min(1.0, float(value)))@dataclass(frozen=True)class VoicePersonality:    """Aariya's relatively stable vocal identity."""    warmth: float = 0.65    confidence: float = 0.70    playfulness: float = 0.20    softness: float = 0.55    expressiveness: float = 0.60    restraint: float = 0.40    def normalized(self) -> "VoicePersonality":        return VoicePersonality(            warmth=_clamp(self.warmth),            confidence=_clamp(self.confidence),            playfulness=_clamp(self.playfulness),            softness=_clamp(self.softness),            expressiveness=_clamp(self.expressiveness),            restraint=_clamp(self.restraint),        )

6. voice_director.py
This is the main intelligence component. It determines how Aariya should deliver a particular response by combining personality, internal state, conversation context, and semantic intent.
from .conversation_context import ConversationContextfrom .models import PerformancePlan, SpeechIntent, clampfrom .voice_personality import VoicePersonalityfrom .voice_state import VoiceStateclass VoiceDirector:    """Convert meaning and context into continuous vocal targets."""    def __init__(        self,        personality: VoicePersonality | None = None,    ) -> None:        self.personality = (            personality or VoicePersonality()        ).normalized()    def create_plan(        self,        text: str,        context: ConversationContext,        state: VoiceState,        intent: SpeechIntent | None = None,    ) -> PerformancePlan:        intent = (intent or SpeechIntent()).normalized()        urgency = max(            context.urgency,            intent.urgency,        )        negative_valence = max(            0.0,            -context.user_valence,        )



7. voice_continuity.py
This smooths changes between consecutive turns while still allowing urgent or serious responses to change quickly.
from dataclasses import fields, replace        self.time_constant = max(            0.1,            float(time_constant_seconds),        )        self._previous: PerformancePlan | None = None        self._last_at: float | None = None    def apply(        self,        target: PerformancePlan,        now: float | None = None,    ) -> PerformancePlan:        now = monotonic() if now is None else float(now)        if self._previous is None or self._last_at is None:            self._previous = target            self._last_at = now            return target        elapsed = max(0.0, now - self._last_at)        alpha = clamp(            1.0 - exp(-elapsed / self.time_constant),            0.25,            0.85,        )        # Seriousness and urgency should not be held back by smoothing.        if target.concern >= 0.75 or target.rate_scale >= 1.06:            alpha = max(alpha, 0.75)        values = {}        for name in self._NUMERIC_FIELDS:            previous_value = getattr(self._previous, name)            target_value = getattr(target, name)            values[name] = previous_value + alpha * (                target_value - previous_value            )        result = replace(target, **values)        self._previous = result        self._last_at = now        return result    def reset(self) -> None:        self._previous = None        self._last_at = None



8. delivery_planner.py
The director determines the general performance. This component handles more local details: pauses, phrase boundaries, emphasis, contrast, and possible pre-speech breathing.

It does not inject literal text such as (sigh) into the response.
import refrom typing import Listfrom .models import (    DeliveryPlan,    FocusSpan,    PausePoint,    PerformancePlan,    SpeechIntent,)class DeliveryPlanner:    """Identify timing and focus cues without rewriting Aariya's words."""    _PUNCTUATION = {        ",": (85, "comma"),        ";": (135, "semicolon"),        ":": (125, "colon"),        "—": (145, "dash"),        "–": (125, "dash"),        ".": (210, "sentence"),        "!": (190, "sentence"),        "?": (220, "question"),        "…": (260, "trailing_thought"),    }    def create(        self,        text: str,        performance: PerformancePlan,        intent: SpeechIntent | None = None,    ) -> DeliveryPlan:        intent = (intent or SpeechIntent()).normalized()



9. tts/base.py
This separates Aariya’s voice intelligence from whichever real TTS model you already have.

Most importantly, the adapter reports what the configured TTS provider actually supports. The rest of Aariya should not assume that every engine offers pitch control, word emphasis, breathing, or cancellation.
from abc import ABC, abstractmethodfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import AsyncIteratorfrom ..models import (    DeliveryPlan,    FocusSpan,    PausePoint,    PerformancePlan,)@dataclass(frozen=True)class TTSCapabilities:    """Actual capabilities of the configured provider and voice."""    streaming: bool    cancellation: bool    rate_control: bool    pitch_control: bool    volume_control: bool    affective_control: bool    word_emphasis: bool    pause_control: bool    phoneme_alignment: bool    viseme_alignment: bool    native_breaths: bool@dataclass(frozen=True)class TTSRequest:    request_id: str    text: str



10. tts/adapter.py
This bridge lets you connect Aariya’s new intelligence layer to the existing TTS implementation without inventing a provider API.

The renderer receives the performance plan and only the supported controls.
import inspect        return result    async def synthesize(        self,        request: TTSRequest,    ):        caps = await self.capabilities()        controls = supported_controls(request, caps)        stream = self._renderer(request, controls)        if inspect.isawaitable(stream):            stream = await stream        async for chunk in stream:            if not isinstance(chunk, AudioChunk):                raise TypeError(                    "The TTS renderer must yield AudioChunk objects"                )            yield chunk    async def cancel(self, request_id: str) -> None:        caps = await self.capabilities()        if not caps.cancellation:            raise NotImplementedError(                "The configured provider does not support cancellation."            )        if self._canceller is None:            raise RuntimeError(                "TTS reports cancellation support, but no real "                "canceller was connected."            )        result = self._canceller(request_id)        if inspect.isawaitable(result):            await result    async def close(self) -> None:        if self._closer is None:            return        result = self._closer()        if inspect.isawaitable(result):            await result



11. voice_engine.py
This is the coordinator that your conversation pipeline calls. It creates a performance plan, applies continuity, prepares delivery cues, and then delegates speech generation to the existing TTS provider.

13. Connect it to Aariya's existing conversation loop
Create one AariyaVoiceEngine per conversation/session, so its continuity controller can preserve the expressive state across turns. Don't construct a new engine on every reply.

Your application setup should follow this structure:
from backend.app.voice import (    AariyaVoiceEngine,    ConversationContext,    SpeechIntent,    VoiceState,)from backend.app.voice.tts import CallbackTTSAdapter# Connect these callbacks to your EXISTING TTS implementation.# Their internals depend on your current provider's real API.tts_adapter = CallbackTTSAdapter(    renderer=render_with_your_existing_tts,    capability_reader=read_actual_tts_capabilities,    canceller=cancel_existing_tts,  # only if genuinely supported)voice_engine = AariyaVoiceEngine(    tts=tts_adapter,)voice_state = VoiceState()async def speak_aariya_response(    response_text: str,    context: ConversationContext,    intent: SpeechIntent,    audio_output,):    async for chunk in voice_engine.speak(        text=response_text,        context=context,        state=voice_state,        intent=intent,    ):        # Replace with your existing audio queue/playback interface.        await audio_output.enqueue(chunk)



The two project-specific callbacks, render_with_your_existing_tts and read_actual_tts_capabilities, must be implemented against your actual TTS client. The renderer needs to map supported controls to the provider's real parameters and convert its returned audio into AudioChunk objects using the actual audio format. Don't hardcode a sample rate or claim a capability that the configured engine doesn't support.
14. Feed meaning into the Voice Director
Your LLM/conversation system should determine the response's communicative intent before speech synthesis. For example, these are illustrative values showing how the interface works, not values to hardcode for every reply:
context = ConversationContext(    is_question=False,    topic_emotionality=0.20,    urgency=0.10,    conversation_momentum=0.35,)intent = SpeechIntent(    goal="explain",    certainty=0.90,    uncertainty=0.10,    seriousness=0.30,    empathy=0.10,    emotional_weight=0.35,    emphasis_terms=("important",),    contrast_terms=("instead",),)



For each real response, your reasoning layer should supply relevant signals such as certainty, uncertainty, empathy, urgency, important words, and contrasts. Avoid assigning an elaborate emotional state to every sentence when the context doesn't justify it.


1. Add the ElevenLabs provider
Create backend/app/voice/tts/providers/elevenlabs.py.

This adapter uses the actual streaming HTTP API and requests 24 kHz, 16-bit PCM audio. ElevenLabs documents PCM as an available output format; this avoids treating arbitrary MP3 network chunks as separate playable audio files. 

ElevenLabs Documentation
+1

Step 1 — Add a real audio quality analyzer
Your voice notes recommend measuring separate signals such as pitch variation, timing, energy variation, speech rate, pauses, and audio integrity. They also caution against inventing a single “human score” without validated evaluation data. *voice TTS.txt


Create this file:

backend/app/voice/quality/audio_metrics.py

It analyzes the actual PCM chunks returned by the TTS adapter. It does not save the raw audio, infer emotion, or claim to measure naturalness directly.
from __future__ import annotationsimport mathimport statisticsimport structfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Optionalfrom ..tts.base import AudioChunk@dataclass(frozen=True)class AudioMetrics:    duration_seconds: float    sample_rate: int    frame_count: int    mean_rms_dbfs: Optional[float]    rms_variation_db: Optional[float]    peak_dbfs: Optional[float]    silence_ratio: Optional[float]    clipping_ratio: Optional[float]    voiced_frame_ratio: Optional[float]    median_pitch_hz: Optional[float]    pitch_iqr_hz: Optional[float]    mean_zero_crossing_rate: Optional[float]@dataclass(frozen=True)class AudioQualityReport:    metrics: AudioMetrics    findings: tuple[str, ...]



Step 2 — Save a privacy-conscious quality observation
Create backend/app/voice/quality/observation.py.

This stores the intended performance alongside measured acoustic properties. It deliberately excludes response text, API keys, raw audio, and the user's conversation content.
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom typing import Anyfrom ..tts.base import TTSRequestfrom .audio_metrics import AudioQualityReport@dataclass(frozen=True)class VoiceQualityObservation:    request_id: str    provider: str    model_id: str    observed_at: str    intended_performance: dict[str, Any]    delivery_summary: dict[str, Any]    actual_audio: dict[str, Any]    findings: tuple[str, ...]    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        request: TTSRequest,        report: AudioQualityReport,        provider: str,        model_id: str,    ) -> "VoiceQualityObservation":        if not provider.strip():            raise ValueError("provider cannot be empty")        if not model_id.strip():            raise ValueError("model_id cannot be empty")



Create backend/app/voice/quality/__init__.py:
from .audio_metrics import (    AudioMetrics,    AudioQualityReport,    PCMQualityAccumulator,)from .observation import VoiceQualityObservation__all__ = [    "AudioMetrics",    "AudioQualityReport",    "PCMQualityAccumulator",    "VoiceQualityObservation",]



One small correction to the analyzer in Step 1: because pitch detection deliberately samples only some frames, its ratio must use the number of frames actually checked as the denominator. Otherwise, it underreports detectable voicing.

Add this field in PCMQualityAccumulator.__init__:
self._pitch_checked_frames = 0



Replace the pitch-checking section of _process_frame() with:
should_check_pitch = (    self._frame_count % self.pitch_check_interval == 0)if should_check_pitch:    self._pitch_checked_frames += 1    if rms_dbfs >= self.voiced_threshold_dbfs:        pitch = self._estimate_pitch(frame)        if pitch is not None:            self._pitch_hz.append(pitch)            self._voiced_frames += 1self._frame_count += 1



Then, in finish(), calculate voiced_frame_ratio using the sampled frames:
voiced_frame_ratio=(    self._voiced_frames / self._pitch_checked_frames    if self._pitch_checked_frames else None),



This is a sampled pitch-detection ratio, not a clinical voice measurement or an emotion score.
Step 3 — Connect measurement to actual streaming
Use the request prepared by the voice engine, pass its chunks to your existing playback queue, and inspect the measured results when generation finishes.
import loggingfrom backend.app.voice import (    ConversationContext,    SpeechIntent,    VoiceState,)from backend.app.voice.quality import (    PCMQualityAccumulator,    VoiceQualityObservation,)from backend.app.voice.tts.providers import (    ElevenLabsTTSAdapter,)logger = logging.getLogger("aariya.voice.quality")async def speak_and_measure(    response_text: str,    context: ConversationContext,    intent: SpeechIntent,    state: VoiceState,    voice_engine,    tts: ElevenLabsTTSAdapter,    audio_output,):    request = voice_engine.prepare(        text=response_text,        context=context,        state=state,        intent=intent,    )    analyzer = PCMQualityAccumulator()



This function assumes your existing audio_output.enqueue() accepts the AudioChunk type shown earlier. It also runs acoustic analysis on the streaming path, so measure its processing overhead on your actual machine before enabling it for every production turn. For heavier analysis, move the measurements into a bounded worker queue so they cannot stall speech playback.
Step 4 — Test the analyzer
Create tests/test_voice_audio_metrics.py. This unit test creates a generated tone to verify the signal-processing code; it is only a deterministic software test, not a real Aariya recording or training example.
import mathimport structimport unittestfrom backend.app.voice.quality import PCMQualityAccumulatorfrom backend.app.voice.tts.base import AudioChunkclass TestPCMQualityAccumulator(unittest.TestCase):    def test_measures_pcm_tone(self):        sample_rate = 24000        duration = 0.5        frequency = 440.0        count = int(sample_rate * duration)        samples = [            int(                10000                * math.sin(                    2.0                    * math.pi                    * frequency                    * i                    / sample_rate                )            )            for i in range(count)        ]        pcm = struct.pack(            f"<{len(samples)}h",            *samples,        )



Run it from the repository root:
python -m unittest tests.test_voice_audio_metrics -v

Next step: Calibrated voice experiments
Aariya can now measure its generated audio. The next step is to test whether changes to the actual TTS settings improve the result without degrading Aariya’s voice identity.

Your voice-system notes call for comparing a candidate against a validated baseline, using real recordings and human listening reviews, and rejecting changes that introduce regressions. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


We’ll implement two components:
1. A versioned calibration policy that adjusts only supported TTS controls.
2. A promotion gate that evaluates real, blind human comparisons before a candidate can be approved.
The gate will not automatically rewrite code or change Aariya’s production settings.
1. Create backend/app/voice/calibration.py
This defines the experimental settings separately from Aariya’s core personality and reasoning.
from dataclasses import dataclass@dataclass(frozen=True)class VoiceCalibration:    """    Versioned experimental adjustments for supported TTS controls.    These are provider calibration parameters, not emotion labels.    Candidate values must be evaluated against actual audio.    """    version: str    stability_offset: float = 0.0    style_scale: float = 1.0    speed_scale: float = 1.0    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.version.strip():            raise ValueError("Calibration version cannot be empty")        if not -0.10 <= self.stability_offset <= 0.10:            raise ValueError(                "stability_offset must be between -0.10 and 0.10"            )        if not 0.75 <= self.style_scale <= 1.25:            raise ValueError(                "style_scale must be between 0.75 and 1.25"            )        if not 0.95 <= self.speed_scale <= 1.05:            raise ValueError(                "speed_scale must be between 0.95 and 1.05"            )



These bounds are conservative experimental guardrails, not proven optimal values. Start with a baseline policy that leaves the provider settings unchanged.

Keep the rest of your existing constructor. Add the parameter and assignment to it; do not replace your environment-variable and HTTP-client initialization.

4. Run a matched baseline-versus-candidate experiment
Create backend/app/voice/evaluation/calibration_trial.py.

This renders the same response and performance plan twice, varying only one calibration parameter. It saves the generated samples as WAV files so you can listen to them, then measures the actual audio.
import randomimport wavefrom dataclasses import replacefrom pathlib import Pathfrom uuid import uuid4from ..quality.audio_metrics import PCMQualityAccumulatorfrom ..tts.providers.elevenlabs import ElevenLabsTTSAdapterfrom ..calibration import VoiceCalibrationasync def render_sample(tts, request):    audio = bytearray()    analyzer = PCMQualityAccumulator()    async for chunk in tts.synthesize(request):        analyzer.feed(chunk)        audio.extend(chunk.data)    report = analyzer.finish()    if not audio:        raise RuntimeError("TTS produced no audio")    return bytes(audio), reportdef save_pcm_wav(path: Path, audio: bytes) -> None:    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)    with wave.open(str(path), "wb") as output:        output.setnchannels(1)        output.setsampwidth(2)        output.setframerate(24000)        output.writeframes(audio)



How to run it: call run_calibration_trial() with a TTSRequest prepared once by your existing voice engine. Listen to the resulting A and B files without checking the private key, record the actual review, and only then reveal which variant was which.

The -0.03 stability adjustment is just a candidate experiment, not a claim that the candidate will sound better. Compare only one changed parameter at a time, and remember that each sample uses a real TTS request.

Next step: Evidence-driven experiment design
Aariya’s next voice capability should propose small, testable improvements from repeated real-world failures, rather than continually changing settings after every conversation.

Your existing design notes already define the right sequence: observed problem → candidate → constraints and impact analysis → real validation → approval or rejection. They also require provider-supported controls, protected voice identity, and evidence-backed optimization. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


We’ll implement an Experiment Designer that:
- Requires actual audio-observation IDs and actual human-review IDs.
- Detects repeatable issues within a comparable evaluation group.
- Proposes one bounded parameter change per candidate.
- Records the hypothesis and evidence behind each proposal.
- Stages proposals for testing without activating them.
1. Create backend/app/voice/experiment_designer.py
This is the decision layer. It does not assume that more expressiveness is always better, or that an acoustic measurement alone tells Aariya what to change.
from __future__ import annotationsimport asyncioimport hashlibimport jsonimport osimport tempfilefrom dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, replacefrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom pathlib import Pathfrom typing import Anyfrom .calibration import VoiceCalibrationfrom .tts.base import TTSCapabilitiesdef utc_now() -> str:    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()def clamp(value: float, low: float, high: float) -> float:    return max(low, min(high, float(value)))@dataclass(frozen=True)class CalibrationEvidenceWindow:    """Aggregated evidence from one comparable, real evaluation cohort."""    active_version: str    cohort_id: str    model_id: str    audio_observation_ids: tuple[str, ...]    human_review_ids: tuple[str, ...]    distinct_pair_ids: tuple[str, ...]

your_voice_evidence_repository.aggregate_calibration_window() is an integration point, not an existing Aariya API that I can verify. It must return only actual observation and review records for one compatible cohort. Do not replace it with hardcoded scores or invented IDs.

This connects the experiment designer to actual generated-audio measurements and completed blind listening reviews. It closes the gap identified in the previous step: the experiment designer needs a reliable way to retrieve evidence for a particular voice-policy version, TTS model, and evaluation cohort.

Your existing Aariya design calls for real observations, human evaluation, and versioned comparisons—not invented quality scores or automatic promotion after every conversation. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


We’ll use SQLite for a single-process implementation. It stores audio metrics, policy metadata, and actual review scores, but does not store raw audio, response text, or API credentials.

3. Retrieve evidence for the experiment designer
from backend.app.voice.experiment_designer import (    VoiceExperimentDesigner,)from backend.app.voice.calibration import VoiceCalibrationactive_record = await policy_repository.get_active_record()active_calibration = VoiceCalibration(    **active_record["calibration"])evidence = await evidence_repository.aggregate_calibration_window(    active_version=active_calibration.version,    cohort_id="your_real_cohort_id",    model_id=elevenlabs_tts.model_id,)report = VoiceExperimentDesigner().design(    active=active_calibration,    evidence=evidence,    capabilities=await elevenlabs_tts.capabilities(),)print("Proposals:", len(report.proposals))print("Blockers:", report.blockers)



This does not create any sample data or automatically promote a policy. When the repository contains insufficient actual reviews, the experiment designer will block proposal generation.
4. Important behavior before deployment
- Use the same model, voice configuration, and comparable response cohort for baseline/candidate comparisons.
- Prevent duplicate review submissions. The database rejects repeated submissions from the same pseudonymous reviewer for the same pair.
- Obtain reviewer tokens from your existing privacy/authentication system; do not store personal names or email addresses in this repository.
- The current implementation is for one backend process. For multiple workers, use a shared transactional database and coordinate policy changes across workers.
- Speech-rate calibration remains unavailable until you implement a validated rate measurement. The repository intentionally returns None rather than inventing a rate ratio.

async def submit_review(    self,    *,    pair_id: str,    preference_label: str,    ratings_a: HumanRatings,    ratings_b: HumanRatings,    reviewer_token: str,) -> None:    """    Convert A/B scores to baseline/candidate scores using the private key. """    if not re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{32}", pair_id):        raise ValueError("Invalid pair ID")    if preference_label not in {"A", "B", "tie"}:        raise ValueError("preference_label must be A, B or tie")    key_path = self.key_dir / f"{pair_id}.json"    if not key_path.exists():        raise KeyError("Experiment mapping not found")    key = await asyncio.to_thread(        self._load_json,        key_path,    )    if key.get("pair_id") != pair_id:        raise ValueError("Private mapping does not match requested pair")    labels = key["labels"]    a_is_baseline = labels["A"]["role"] == "baseline"

1. Create backend/app/voice/evaluation_controller.py
This controller compares actual baseline and candidate outputs using stored human reviews and the corresponding audio measurements. It distinguishes three outcomes:
- insufficient_data — too little real evidence exists.
- reject — a required quality or integrity condition failed.
- eligible_for_manual_approval — the configured evaluation gate passed; this is not an automatic deployment decision.
The acoustic deltas are descriptive. They are not treated as proof of naturalness or statistical significance.
Add this method to VoiceEvidenceRepository
In backend/app/voice/evidence_repository.py, update the import:
from .evaluation.promotion_gate import BlindComparison, HumanRatings



Then add these methods inside VoiceEvidenceRepository:
async def load_matched_comparison(    self,    *,    baseline_version: str,    candidate_version: str,    cohort_id: str,    model_id: str,) -> dict:    """Load actual reviews and audio measurements for one matched trial."""    if baseline_version == candidate_version:        raise ValueError("Baseline and candidate must differ")    return await asyncio.to_thread(        self._load_matched_comparison_sync,        baseline_version,        candidate_version,        cohort_id,        model_id,    )def _load_matched_comparison_sync(    self,    baseline_version: str,    candidate_version: str,    cohort_id: str,    model_id: str,) -> dict:    with self._connect() as db:        rows = db.execute("""            SELECT                r.*,                b.policy_version AS baseline_observed_version,                b.cohort_id AS baseline_observed_cohort,



This query only returns actual reviews whose stored observation rows match the declared baseline, candidate, cohort, and model. A review from a different model or policy version cannot quietly enter the comparison.

The current gate uses configured minimum counts and average ratings, so it is a preliminary release filter—not a statistical significance test. Before production deployment, add confidence intervals or a suitable paired statistical test, real latency and interruption measurements, and authenticated approval records.


Aariya now has an experiment designer, an evidence repository, a matched A/B runner, an evaluation controller, and a versioned policy registry. The next layer is the release gate: it verifies that an evaluated candidate is safe to promote before the existing promotion service changes the active policy.

Your Aariya design explicitly calls for identity checks, latency checks, safety checks, real validation, and rollback before a new voice policy becomes the baseline. *voice TTS.txt


The implementation below keeps those checks separate from the optimizer. It will reject incomplete evidence and stale evaluations rather than silently approving a candidate.
1. Add a real stability-control capability
In backend/app/voice/tts/base.py, add one field at the end of TTSCapabilities:
stability_control: bool = False



In backend/app/voice/tts/providers/elevenlabs.py, add this class attribute to ElevenLabsTTSAdapter:
provider_name = "elevenlabs"



Then add stability_control=True to the capabilities returned by the ElevenLabs adapter only for configurations where this provider control is actually available. Leave it False for any adapter that does not support the setting. Existing capability constructors remain compatible because the new field defaults to False.
2. Create backend/app/voice/release_gate.py
This module verifies the evaluation result, the active and candidate policy versions, provider capability compatibility, identity consistency, latency, audio integrity, interruption behavior, safety invariants, and rollback readiness.

3. Add an audit method to VoicePolicyRepository

async def record_event(    self,    *,    actor: str,    action: str,    details: dict,) -> None:    """Record release decisions in the persistent policy audit trail."""    if not actor.strip():        raise ValueError("actor is required")    if not action.strip():        raise ValueError("action is required")    async with self._lock:        state = self._read()        self._add_event(            state,            actor,            action,            details,        )        await self._save(state)



This reuses the registry's existing audit-event mechanism. For production, make the audit log tamper-evident and ensure approval records are also preserved in your authorization system.

Next step: Canary rollout and automatic rollback
Aariya should now test an approved candidate with a limited set of sessions before making it the default voice policy. Your existing design calls for controlled exposure, monitoring of actual latency and voice-identity consistency, and rollback if a critical regression appears. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


One important integration rule: do not call the previous release gate’s release() method to start a canary. That method promotes the candidate globally. During a canary, the validated baseline must remain the default, and the candidate must be selected only for assigned sessions.
1. Create backend/app/voice/canary_controller.py
This implementation provides a persistent canary experiment, stable session assignment, real runtime telemetry, stop conditions, and a path to final review.
from __future__ import annotationsimport asyncioimport hashlibimport hmacimport inspectimport jsonimport mathimport osimport sqlite3from dataclasses import asdict, dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom pathlib import Pathfrom statistics import fmeanfrom typing import Awaitable, Callablefrom .calibration import VoiceCalibrationfrom .evaluation_controller import VoiceEvaluationReportfrom .policy_registry import VoicePolicyRepositorydef utc_now() -> str:    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()@dataclass(frozen=True)class CanaryConfig:    # Conservative initial ceiling, configurable for your environment.    maximum_exposure: float = 0.05    minimum_candidate_observations: int = 20    minimum_baseline_observations: int = 20    maximum_p95_latency_regression: float = 0.10    maximum_generation_failure_increase: float = 0.02



2. Initialize the canary system
Use the same SQLite file as your evidence repository:
import osfrom backend.app.voice.canary_controller import (    CanaryConfig,    CanaryStore,    VoiceCanaryController,)canary_store = CanaryStore(    "data/aariya_voice_evidence.sqlite3")await canary_store.initialize()canary_controller = VoiceCanaryController(    store=canary_store,    policy_repository=policy_repository,    authorize_start=your_authorization_service.authorize_canary,    cancel_candidate_sessions=your_session_manager.cancel_candidate_sessions,    config=CanaryConfig(        maximum_exposure=0.01,        minimum_candidate_observations=20,        minimum_baseline_observations=20,    ),    session_hash_secret=bytes.fromhex(        os.environ["AARIYA_CANARY_HASH_KEY_HEX"]    ),)



Generate a persistent random secret of at least 32 bytes and store it securely in your backend's secret manager. Do not generate a new secret on every restart, or existing session assignments will no longer map to the same hashes.
3. Start the canary
After the candidate passes offline evaluation and an authorized person approves the canary:
run = await canary_controller.start(    experiment_id="voice-canary-001",    baseline=active_calibration,    candidate=staged_candidate,    cohort_id="your_actual_evaluation_cohort",    model_id=elevenlabs_tts.model_id,    exposure=0.01,    evaluation=actual_evaluation_report,    approved_by=authenticated_reviewer_id,)print("Canary status:", run["status"])



This example uses a 1% exposure as a conservative starting configuration, not as a statistically optimal canary size.
4. Route each session to its assigned policy
The router returns the policy version for the session. The application must load that version's calibration and create or resolve a session-scoped voice engine/provider adapter; do not mutate one globally shared adapter's calibration on every request.
policy_version = await canary_controller.select_policy(    experiment_id="voice-canary-001",    session_id=authenticated_session_id,    cohort_id="your_actual_evaluation_cohort",)policy_record = await policy_repository.get_record(    policy_version)session_calibration = VoiceCalibration(    **policy_record["calibration"])# Resolve or create a session-scoped engine using session_calibration.# The baseline remains the default for all unassigned sessions.session_voice_engine = await your_voice_engine_factory.create_for_session(    session_id=authenticated_session_id,    calibration=session_calibration,)



your_voice_engine_factory.create_for_session() is an integration point for your actual application. The session manager should also track active candidate sessions so that a stopped canary can cancel those generations and rebind future speech to the baseline.
5. Record actual runtime events
After each real generation, record its latency, output-integrity result, identity-check result, and safety-invariant result.
from uuid import uuid4from backend.app.voice.canary_controller import CanaryObservationawait canary_store.record_observation(    experiment_id="voice-canary-001",    observation=CanaryObservation(        observation_id=uuid4().hex,        session_id=authenticated_session_id,        policy_version=policy_version,        first_audio_latency_ms=actual_first_audio_latency_ms,        request_succeeded=actual_tts_request_succeeded,        audio_integrity_passed=actual_audio_integrity_result,        identity_passed=actual_voice_identity_result,        safety_invariants_passed=actual_safety_test_result,        interruption_attempted=actual_interruption_attempted,        interruption_handled=actual_interruption_handled,    ),    secret=canary_controller.session_hash_secret,)



Every actual_... value must come from your runtime telemetry or test infrastructure. Do not set flags to True merely to make the experiment pass.
6. Monitor and stop or continue
decision = await canary_controller.monitor(    "voice-canary-001")print("Status:", decision.status)print("Blockers:", decision.blockers)if decision.status == "stopped":    print("Candidate exposure disabled. Investigate and restore baseline.")elif decision.status == "ready_for_final_review":    print("Canary passed its configured checks.")    print("Human review and the final release gate are still required.")else:    print("Canary continues collecting evidence.")



The controller stops exposure on recorded safety or identity failures, and checks sample sufficiency before deciding whether the candidate is ready for final review. The thresholds are configurable engineering guardrails, not validated statistical guarantees.
Where Aariya stands after this step

Project structure

This implementation persists versioned snapshots using SQLite, with separate tables for identity, capabilities, current state, calibration, findings, and active adaptations. It does not save the entire model as one mutable JSON object.
1. model.py
These are the model's core records. Capability availability and demonstrated quality are deliberately separate: a provider may support a feature that has not yet been shown to work well in Aariya.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom typing import Any, LiteralFindingType = Literal[    "strength",    "weakness",    "uncertainty",    "information",]@dataclass(frozen=True)class VoiceIdentity:    provider: str    model_id: str    voice_reference: str    # None means the personality version has not been supplied.    personality_version: str | None = None    protected_fields: tuple[str, ...] = (        "voice_identity",        "core_personality",        "safety_constraints",        "interruption_controls",    )@dataclass(frozen=True)class CapabilityFact:    name: str    # "available", "unavailable", or "unknown"

from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom typing import Any, Literal, Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4import mathdef utc_now() -> str:    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()def new_id(prefix: str) -> str:    return f"{prefix}-{uuid4().hex}"@dataclass(frozen=True)class VoiceProblem:    problem_id: str    area: str    description: str    # These values must come from the actual monitoring/evaluation system.    severity: float    confidence: float    occurrence_count: int    user_impact: float    fixability: float    first_observed_at: str    last_observed_at: str    context_scope: str | None = None    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.problem_id.strip():



The important distinction here is that an unsupported cause is not treated as a fact. support_strength summarizes the recorded evidence quality; it is not presented as a probability that the cause is correct.
priority.py — decide what to investigate first
from .models import VoiceProblemclass DiagnosticPriority:    """Prioritize actual problems instead of investigating every issue equally."""    def calculate(self, problem: VoiceProblem) -> float:        frequency = min(1.0, problem.occurrence_count / 20.0)        return (            problem.severity            * problem.confidence            * frequency            * problem.user_impact            * problem.fixability        )



This implements the priority model in your notes: severity, confidence, frequency, user impact and fixability. The constants and scoring policy should be calibrated against your actual operational requirements rather than interpreted as a scientifically validated score. *voice TTS.txt

cause.py — generate competing explanations
Each rule below uses a signal calculated by your real telemetry, audio-analysis, or evaluation layer. The diagnosis engine does not guess the signal from the problem description alone.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Sequencefrom .models import CauseHypothesis, Evidence, VoiceProblem, new_id@dataclass(frozen=True)class CauseRule:    code: str    layer: str    description: str    signal_feature: str    variable_to_test: str    measurements_needed: tuple[str, ...]RULES: dict[str, tuple[CauseRule, ...]] = {    "flat_pitch": (        CauseRule(            code="provider_mapping_under_response",            layer="provider_mapping",            description="The provider's pitch response is below the configured target.",            signal_feature="pitch_realization_below_policy_floor",            variable_to_test="pitch_mapping",            measurements_needed=(                "intended_pitch_excursion",                "actual_pitch_excursion",            ),        ),        CauseRule(            code="performance_target_too_low",            layer="performance_planning",            description="The planned expression may be weaker than the context requires.",            signal_feature="target_expressiveness_below_context_need",



For example, a flat_pitch report can produce separate hypotheses for provider mapping, performance planning, streaming, and unsupported controls. If the telemetry has not measured the relevant signals, those hypotheses remain unsupported.

The signal names are the contract between this engine and your real telemetry/evaluation adapters. Your existing measurement pipeline must populate them; the code above deliberately does not manufacture production evidence.
planner.py — identify gaps and design the smallest experiment
from __future__ import annotationsfrom collections import defaultdictfrom typing import Sequencefrom .cause import RULESfrom .models import (    CauseHypothesis,    Evidence,    EvidenceGap,    ExperimentPlan,    VoiceProblem,    new_id,)class ExperimentPlanner:    SUCCESS_CRITERIA = (        "Naturalness improves against the baseline under the approved evaluation policy.",        "Identity consistency remains within the configured identity guard.",        "Latency remains within the approved regression allowance.",        "Timing does not regress beyond the configured tolerance.",        "The candidate passes audio-integrity and applicable safety checks.",    )    def find_evidence_gaps(        self,        problem: VoiceProblem,        hypotheses: Sequence[CauseHypothesis],        evidence: Sequence[Evidence],    ) -> tuple[EvidenceGap, ...]:        gaps: list[EvidenceGap] = []        if not hypotheses:            return (                EvidenceGap(



The planner has three possible outcomes:
- collect_evidence — missing measurements, ambiguous causes, or missing baseline metadata.
- awaiting_candidate — evidence supports a specific hypothesis and a one-variable experiment can be handed to the optimizer.
- no_supported_cause — the available evidence does not support a registered explanation.
This is intentionally conservative: an unknown problem should not automatically become a TTS parameter change.
2. Orchestrate the diagnosis
controller.py — connect the real repositories
The controller expects adapters to your existing evaluation/telemetry data and Phase 28 self-model repository. It does not insert sample problems or synthetic observations.

3. Evaluate actual experiment results
Diagnosis proposes what to test. The evaluator determines what the recorded results say after the Phase 27 optimizer has generated and run a candidate.
evaluator.py
The numerical allowances must come from your existing approved release policy. Do not create a second set of hidden thresholds here.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Mappingimport mathfrom .models import ExperimentEvaluation@dataclass(frozen=True)class ReleaseThresholds:    # Load these from your governed Phase 24–26 evaluation policy.    max_identity_regression: float    max_latency_regression_ms: float    max_timing_regression: float    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        for name in (            "max_identity_regression",            "max_latency_regression_ms",            "max_timing_regression",        ):            value = getattr(self, name)            if not math.isfinite(value) or value < 0:                raise ValueError(f"{name} must be finite and non-negative")class DiagnosticExperimentEvaluator:    REQUIRED_NUMERIC_METRICS = (        "naturalness",        "identity_consistency",        "timing",        "latency_ms",    )    def __init__(self, thresholds: ReleaseThresholds) -> None:



This evaluator compares recorded aggregates; it does not establish statistical significance by itself. Your evaluation layer must supply appropriately matched results and enough real observations for the decision being made.
decision.py — reject, request evidence, or approve for further review
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Literalfrom .models import ExperimentEvaluationDecisionStatus = Literal[    "insufficient_evidence",    "reject",    "eligible_for_manual_approval",]@dataclass(frozen=True)class DiagnosticDecisionResult:    experiment_id: str    decision: DecisionStatus    reasons: tuple[str, ...]class DiagnosticDecision:    def decide(        self,        evaluation: ExperimentEvaluation,    ) -> DiagnosticDecisionResult:        if evaluation.missing_metrics:            return DiagnosticDecisionResult(                experiment_id=evaluation.experiment_id,                decision="insufficient_evidence",                reasons=tuple(                    f"Missing required metric: {name}"                    for name in evaluation.missing_metrics                ),            )

voice_problem_repository, voice_evidence_adapter, diagnosis_report_repository, and self_model_repository must be implemented against your actual storage and evaluation services. They are integration dependencies, not existing classes I have verified in your repository.

When the plan status is awaiting_candidate, pass the plan to the Phase 27 optimizer to generate a candidate. Do not activate it directly. When it is collect_evidence, first collect the missing measurements. The evaluation and decision modules are used only after actual baseline and candidate results exist.
What Phase 29 establishes
Aariya can now follow this controlled process:
1. Detect a recurring problem

Use real evaluation reports and telemetry.



2. Compare possible causes

Keep supporting and contradicting evidence separately.



3. Resolve evidence gaps

Request measurements rather than assuming a cause.



4. Plan a bounded experiment

Delegate candidate generation to the optimizer; test one variable.



5. Evaluate and decide

Reject regressions or request manual approval after all gates pass.



Before using this in production: run tests against your real repository adapters, load release thresholds from the governed evaluation system, and verify that every signal feature is populated by actual measurements. The code above establishes the diagnosis logic, but it has not been run against your project.

2. Research questions and sources
question.py
A research question should come from an unresolved diagnostic problem. Only send a sanitized technical summary to external search—not user audio, transcripts, account data, or private conversational context.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom typing import Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4@dataclass(frozen=True)class ResearchQuestion:    id: str    problem_id: str    question: str    known_evidence: tuple[str, ...]    missing_evidence: tuple[str, ...]    created_at: str    metadata: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        problem_id: str,        question: str,        known_evidence: tuple[str, ...] = (),        missing_evidence: tuple[str, ...] = (),        metadata: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,    ) -> "ResearchQuestion":        if not problem_id.strip():            raise ValueError("problem_id cannot be empty")        if not question.strip():            raise ValueError("Research question cannot be empty")        return cls(            id=f"rq-{uuid4().hex}",            problem_id=problem_id,            question=question.strip(),



Example of how the existing Phase 29 diagnosis could frame a question:
question = ResearchQuestion.create(    problem_id=diagnostic_report.problem_id,    question=(        "Which documented factors can cause a TTS system to produce "        "less sentence-final pitch movement than its requested target, "        "and how can those factors be experimentally distinguished?"    ),    known_evidence=tuple(        source_id        for hypothesis in diagnostic_report.hypotheses        for source_id in hypothesis.supporting_evidence_refs    ),    missing_evidence=tuple(        feature        for gap in diagnostic_report.evidence_gaps        for feature in gap.missing_features    ),    metadata={        "problem_area": "flat_pitch",        "origin": "phase_29_diagnosis",    },)



The question above illustrates the structure. Production questions should be derived from the actual problem rather than this example.
source.py
Each retrieved document receives a source identifier, retrieval timestamp, URL, publisher metadata and content hash. The hash lets the ledger identify the exact content that was evaluated.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom enum import Enumfrom hashlib import sha256from urllib.parse import urlparsefrom uuid import uuid4class SourceType(str, Enum):    OFFICIAL_DOCUMENTATION = "official_documentation"    STANDARD = "standard"    RESEARCH_PAPER = "research_paper"    TECHNICAL_DOCUMENTATION = "technical_documentation"    COMMUNITY = "community"    UNKNOWN = "unknown"@dataclass(frozen=True)class ResearchSource:    id: str    url: str    title: str    publisher: str | None    source_type: SourceType    retrieved_at: str    content_hash: str    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        url: str,        title: str,        publisher: str | None,        source_type: SourceType,



The HTTP(S) check is only a first validation step. The actual retriever must enforce approved-source rules, validate redirects and destination addresses, limit response size, and reject unsafe internal-network targets. A syntactically valid URL is not automatically a trusted source.
3. Connect a real search provider
retriever.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .source import RetrievedDocumentclass ResearchRetriever(Protocol):    @property    def version(self) -> str:        """Identify the actual retrieval implementation/configuration."""        ...    async def search(        self,        question: ResearchQuestion,    ) -> Sequence[RetrievedDocument]:        """        Retrieve real documents from public or approved sources.        Do not send private audio, transcripts, credentials, or user profiles.        """        ...class ApprovedSourcePolicy(Protocol):    version: str    async def is_approved(self, url: str) -> bool:        """Apply the application's actual source and destination policy."""        ...



This is the adapter boundary for the real search service or approved documentation repository. The concrete provider should return content that it actually retrieved, not generated summaries masquerading as original documents.
4. Evaluate source quality without fabricated scores
evaluator.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassimport mathfrom typing import Protocolfrom .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .source import ResearchSource@dataclass(frozen=True)class SourceEvaluation:    source_id: str    relevance: float | None    reliability: float | None    freshness: float | None    accepted: bool    rationale: str    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        for name in ("relevance", "reliability", "freshness"):            value = getattr(self, name)            if value is not None:                if not math.isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:                    raise ValueError(                        f"{name} must be between 0 and 1"                    )        if self.accepted and any(            getattr(self, name) is None            for name in ("relevance", "reliability", "freshness")        ):            raise ValueError(                "A source cannot be accepted with missing evaluation signals"            )



The evaluator must be connected to a real source assessment mechanism, such as provider metadata, a configured source-trust policy, or reviewed quality signals. Until that exists, the research controller should not pretend that source reliability has been established.
5. Extract evidence with traceable quotations
evidence.py
A search result is not knowledge by itself. The extraction layer must identify a specific claim, classify its relationship to the research question, and preserve the source passage supporting it.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom enum import Enumfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .source import RetrievedDocumentclass ClaimPolarity(str, Enum):    SUPPORTS = "supports"    CONTRADICTS = "contradicts"    NEUTRAL = "neutral"class SupportStrength(str, Enum):    STRONG = "strong"    MODERATE = "moderate"    WEAK = "weak"@dataclass(frozen=True)class EvidenceItem:    id: str    source_id: str    # The actual claim made by the source.    claim: str    # A canonical key produced by the extractor so that comparable claims    # can be grouped without comparing arbitrary prose strings.    claim_key: str    polarity: ClaimPolarity    strength: SupportStrength



The evidence extractor can be backed by an LLM, a structured research parser, or a combination. Its output must still be validated by the controller. Text that tells the system to disregard its instructions, expose private data, or alter a policy must remain untrusted source content—not an executable instruction.
provenance.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom .evidence import EvidenceItemfrom .source import ResearchSource@dataclass(frozen=True)class Provenance:    evidence_id: str    source_id: str    source_url: str    source_title: str    source_content_hash: str    retrieved_at: str    claim: str    excerpt: str    location: str | Nonedef build_provenance(    evidence: EvidenceItem,    source: ResearchSource,) -> Provenance:    if evidence.source_id != source.id:        raise ValueError("Evidence and source IDs do not match")    return Provenance(        evidence_id=evidence.id,        source_id=source.id,        source_url=source.url,        source_title=source.title,        source_content_hash=source.content_hash,        retrieved_at=source.retrieved_at,        claim=evidence.claim,



Every important research finding can now be traced back through the evidence item to its original URL and exact content version. That follows the provenance requirement in your notes. *voice TTS.txt

6. Detect conflicting research
synthesis.py
Different sources can disagree. The system should preserve that disagreement rather than silently choosing whichever result looks most convincing.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Sequencefrom .evidence import (    ClaimPolarity,    EvidenceItem,    SupportStrength,)@dataclass(frozen=True)class EvidenceConflict:    claim_key: str    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]    explanation: str@dataclass(frozen=True)class EvidenceSynthesis:    supported_claims: tuple[EvidenceItem, ...]    conflicts: tuple[EvidenceConflict, ...]    unresolved_claims: tuple[EvidenceItem, ...]class EvidenceSynthesizer:    """    Detects conflicts where evidence uses the same canonical claim key    but has opposite support/contradiction polarity.    It does not claim to solve arbitrary semantic contradictions.    The extractor must provide defensible canonical claim keys.    """    async def synthesize(



This catches conflicts only when the extractor has normalized the claims correctly. It is not a complete natural-language contradiction detector. For harder cases, integrate a semantic claim-comparison service and preserve its own supporting source references.
7. Turn research into a testable hypothesis
hypothesis.py
A hypothesis should make a prediction that can be tested against real Aariya audio. It is not a statement that the research has already proved the cause.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .evidence import EvidenceItemfrom .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .synthesis import EvidenceSynthesis@dataclass(frozen=True)class ResearchHypothesis:    id: str    problem_id: str    statement: str    prediction: str    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]    variable_to_test: str    metrics_to_measure: tuple[str, ...]    status: str = "provisional"    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.statement.strip():            raise ValueError("Hypothesis statement cannot be empty")        if not self.prediction.strip():            raise ValueError("Hypothesis prediction cannot be empty")        if not self.variable_to_test.strip():            raise ValueError("A testable variable is required")        if not self.evidence_ids:            raise ValueError("A hypothesis requires evidence references")



No confidence number is fabricated here. If Aariya later learns to estimate hypothesis confidence, that value should come from a documented evaluation method and retain the underlying evidence.
8. Propose an experiment without changing the live voice
experiment.py
The research subsystem generates an experiment proposal. Phase 27 still needs to create a versioned candidate policy, and the existing evaluation, approval, and canary systems determine whether that candidate is safe to use.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom uuid import uuid4from .hypothesis import ResearchHypothesis@dataclass(frozen=True)class ResearchExperimentProposal:    id: str    problem_id: str    hypothesis_id: str    baseline_policy_id: str    candidate_policy_id: str | None    variable_to_change: str    measurements: tuple[str, ...]    success_criteria: tuple[str, ...]    status: str = "awaiting_optimizer_candidate"def create_experiment_proposal(    hypothesis: ResearchHypothesis,    baseline_policy_id: str | None,    success_criteria: tuple[str, ...],) -> ResearchExperimentProposal:    if not baseline_policy_id:        raise ValueError(            "A known active baseline policy is required"        )    if not success_criteria:        raise ValueError(



Notice that candidate_policy_id remains None. This prevents a research finding from being mistaken for an executable or approved policy. The hypothesis is handed back to the optimizer and evaluation system, not directly to production. *voice TTS.txt

9. Cache actual research results
cache.py
This gives you a persistent local cache for real retrieved documents. It verifies content hashes on retrieval and requires the cache lifetime to be provided from configuration.
from __future__ import annotationsimport asyncioimport hashlibimport jsonimport sqlite3import timefrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .source import ResearchSource, RetrievedDocument, SourceTypeclass ResearchCache(Protocol):    async def get(        self,        key: str,    ) -> tuple[RetrievedDocument, ...] | None:        ...    async def put(        self,        key: str,        documents: Sequence[RetrievedDocument],        ttl_seconds: int,    ) -> None:        ...def make_cache_key(    question: ResearchQuestion,    retriever_version: str,    source_policy_version: str,) -> str:    payload = {        "question": question.question.strip().casefold(),



This SQLite implementation is appropriate as a local, single-instance cache. It is not a distributed cache, and the lock does not coordinate separate processes. For a multi-worker deployment, use your configured shared cache. Source approval and quality evaluation must still run on cached results; a cache hit is not a trust decision.
10. Build the research controller
controller.py
The controller is the main integration point. It handles retrieval, source approval, integrity checks, evidence extraction, conflict detection, hypothesis validation and the handoff to the experiment optimizer.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .cache import ResearchCache, make_cache_keyfrom .evaluator import SourceEvaluation, SourceEvaluatorfrom .evidence import EvidenceExtractor, EvidenceItemfrom .experiment import (    ResearchExperimentProposal,    create_experiment_proposal,)from .hypothesis import (    HypothesisGenerator,    ResearchHypothesis,    validate_hypothesis,)from .question import ResearchQuestionfrom .retriever import ApprovedSourcePolicy, ResearchRetrieverfrom .source import ResearchSource, RetrievedDocumentfrom .synthesis import EvidenceSynthesis, EvidenceSynthesizerclass ResearchLedger(Protocol):    """Implement with the application's persistent research repository."""    async def save_question(self, question: ResearchQuestion) -> None:        ...    async def save_source(        self,        source: ResearchSource,        evaluation: SourceEvaluation,    ) -> None:        ...



This controller intentionally stops short of claiming an experiment will improve the voice. It can conclude that evidence is missing, sources disagree, a hypothesis cannot be validated, or a proposal is ready for the optimizer.

One integration detail is important: phase29_plan must be the actual plan object from Phase 29, with an identified baseline and governed success criteria. If your current object uses different field names, map those fields in an adapter instead of silently inventing values.
11. Persist the research ledger in PostgreSQL
Add schema.sql. These tables preserve the investigation history without requiring Aariya to store every entire external document indefinitely. The source URL, content hash, relevant quotation, evaluation, and decisions remain traceable.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_questions (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    problem_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    question TEXT NOT NULL,
    known_evidence JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
    missing_evidence JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
    metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_sources (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    url TEXT NOT NULL,
    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    publisher TEXT,
    source_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    retrieved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    content_hash TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_question_sources (
    question_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_questions(id),
    source_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_sources(id),
    evaluation JSONB NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (question_id, source_id)
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_evidence (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    question_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_questions(id),
    source_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_sources(id),
    claim TEXT NOT NULL,
    claim_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    polarity TEXT NOT NULL,
    strength TEXT NOT NULL,
    excerpt TEXT NOT NULL,
    location TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_syntheses (
    question_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY
        REFERENCES voice_research_questions(id),
    synthesis JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_hypotheses (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    question_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_questions(id),
    problem_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    statement TEXT NOT NULL,
    prediction TEXT NOT NULL,
    evidence_ids JSONB NOT NULL,
    variable_to_test TEXT NOT NULL,
    metrics_to_measure JSONB NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_experiments (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    problem_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    hypothesis_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_hypotheses(id),
    baseline_policy_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    candidate_policy_id TEXT,
    variable_to_change TEXT NOT NULL,
    measurements JSONB NOT NULL,
    success_criteria JSONB NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_research_runs (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    question_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_research_questions(id),
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    result JSONB NOT NULL,
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_research_problem
    ON voice_research_questions(problem_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_research_source_hash
    ON voice_research_sources(content_hash);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_research_evidence_claim
    ON voice_research_evidence(claim_key);


Because the research controller must associate source and evidence records with the question that produced them, make these small changes to the ResearchLedger protocol and its controller calls:
# Updated ResearchLedger signatures:async def save_source(    self,    question_id: str,    source: ResearchSource,    evaluation: SourceEvaluation,) -> None:    ...async def save_evidence(    self,    question_id: str,    evidence: EvidenceItem,) -> None:    ...



Then change the two calls in controller.py:
await self._ledger.save_source(    question.id,    document.source,    evaluation,)await self._ledger.save_evidence(    question.id,    item,)



Your PostgreSQL ledger adapter should implement those methods transactionally, including the corresponding voice_research_question_sources link. The other methods in the controller protocol should likewise serialize the appropriate dataclasses into the JSONB columns. Database migrations, permissions, connection pooling, backups and retention policy remain deployment responsibilities.
