13. Connect it to Aariya
The wiring should use the concrete providers and repositories your project actually has:
from backend.app.voice.research import (    EvidenceSynthesizer,    SQLiteResearchCache,    VoiceResearchController,)research_controller = VoiceResearchController(    retriever=actual_research_retriever,    source_policy=approved_source_policy,    source_evaluator=actual_source_evaluator,    evidence_extractor=actual_evidence_extractor,    synthesizer=EvidenceSynthesizer(),    hypothesis_generator=actual_hypothesis_generator,    ledger=postgres_research_ledger,    cache=SQLiteResearchCache(settings.voice_research_cache_db),    cache_ttl_seconds=settings.voice_research_cache_ttl_seconds,)# These objects must be based on a real Phase 29 diagnosis.result = await research_controller.investigate(    question=research_question,    phase29_plan=diagnostic_report.plan,)print("Research status:", result.status)print("Accepted sources:", result.accepted_sources)print("Rejected items:", result.rejected_items)if result.synthesis:    print("Conflicts:", result.synthesis.conflicts)if result.hypothesis:    print("Hypothesis:", result.hypothesis.statement)    print("Testable prediction:", result.hypothesis.prediction)if result.experiment:



Here, names beginning with actual_ are integration placeholders. The controller cannot carry out real research until you connect an actual retriever, approved-source policy, source evaluator, evidence extractor, hypothesis generator and PostgreSQL ledger adapter. The local SQLite cache stores real retrieved content; the rest of the research records belong in the persistent ledger.
What Phase 30 accomplishes
External knowledge

Retrieve real technical documentation, standards and research papers through approved providers.


Evidence and provenance

Keep source identifiers, content hashes, exact quotations and evaluation results.


Conflict-aware research

Preserve unresolved disagreements rather than presenting them as settled facts.


Research-to-experiment handoff

Create a falsifiable hypothesis and a one-variable experiment proposal for Phase 27 and Phase 29.



Implementation status: this is the code structure and orchestration logic for Phase 30, not a verified, working integration with your existing repository. The provider adapters, database repository implementation, source-entailment checks, production network security, migrations and automated tests still need to be integrated and tested.


Phase 31 — Autonomous Voice Knowledge & Research Memory
Your notes distinguish this from ordinary conversation memory: Aariya must retain the difference between an observation, externally supported information, an experimentally verified result, a failed approach, and something still unknown. *voice TTS.txt


The main design rule is never promote a conclusion just because it was written into memory. Each stored finding will have evidence references, a lifecycle state, and a record of contradictions. When the provider version, policy, or operating context changes, previously useful knowledge can become stale and require revalidation. *voice TTS.txt


The package will be backend/app/voice/knowledge/, backed by PostgreSQL and connected to the research controller from Phase 30.
1. Create the knowledge package
backend/app/voice/knowledge/
├── __init__.py
├── entities.py
├── provenance.py
├── confidence.py
├── validation.py
├── lifecycle.py
├── contradiction.py
├── repository.py
├── retrieval.py
├── consolidation.py
├── controller.py
└── schema.sql


entities.py — represent what Aariya knows
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom enum import Enumfrom uuid import uuid4from typing import Mappingdef utc_now() -> datetime:    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)class KnowledgeType(str, Enum):    OBSERVATION = "observation"    EXTERNAL_EVIDENCE = "external_evidence"    EXPERIMENT_RESULT = "experiment_result"    PROVIDER_CAPABILITY = "provider_capability"    FAILURE_PATTERN = "failure_pattern"    SUCCESS_PATTERN = "success_pattern"    IDENTITY_CONSTRAINT = "identity_constraint"    UNKNOWN = "unknown"class KnowledgeStatus(str, Enum):    UNVERIFIED = "unverified"    SUPPORTED = "supported"    EXPERIMENTALLY_VERIFIED = "experimentally_verified"    CONTRADICTED = "contradicted"    SUPERSEDED = "superseded"class ClaimStance(str, Enum):    AFFIRMS = "affirms"    DENIES = "denies"    NEUTRAL = "neutral"



The subject_key is important for finding related knowledge. For example, statements about whether a particular pitch mapping improves expressiveness need a shared canonical subject key before the contradiction detector can compare them reliably.

The scope lets Aariya preserve the conditions under which a claim was established, such as provider version, voice model, calibration policy, or response type. Knowledge found in one context should not automatically be assumed true in another.
2. Preserve evidence provenance
provenance.py
Knowledge references must resolve to actual records in the Phase 30 research ledger, audio analysis service, evaluation store, or experiment repository.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetimefrom typing import Mapping, Protocolfrom .entities import EvidenceOrigin, KnowledgeEvidence@dataclass(frozen=True)class ResolvedEvidence:    reference_id: str    origin: EvidenceOrigin    # Set by the source-specific evidence adapter after checking the    # actual referenced record and its integrity.    integrity_verified: bool    # Produced by the real evaluation/provider adapter, not guessed.    quality_score: float | None    supports_claim: bool    independence_key: str | None    observed_at: datetime    experiment_id: str | None = None    experiment_outcome: str | None = None    human_evaluation_id: str | None = None    context: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)class EvidenceResolver(Protocol):    async def resolve(        self,        reference: KnowledgeEvidence,



For an external source, the resolver should verify its Phase 30 source ID and content integrity. For an experiment, it should resolve the actual result and evaluation references. For audio observations, it should query your audio-quality repository.

The resolver must not infer that a missing reference exists. A failed lookup is a failed lookup, not an opportunity to make up supporting evidence.
3. Calculate confidence from real evidence
confidence.py
The score below combines evidence quality, independent corroboration, reproducibility, human evaluation, recency, and contradictory evidence. All weights and evaluation requirements are injected from application configuration.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom math import exp, isfinitefrom typing import Mapping, Sequencefrom .provenance import ResolvedEvidenceMETRICS = (    "evidence_quality",    "source_diversity",    "reproducibility",    "human_evaluation",    "recency",)@dataclass(frozen=True)class ConfidencePolicy:    # Supply weights from a reviewed application configuration.    weights: Mapping[str, float]    target_independent_sources: int    target_experiment_replicates: int    target_human_evaluations: int    recency_half_life_days: float    contradiction_penalty: float    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if set(self.weights) != set(METRICS):            raise ValueError(f"Weights must contain exactly: {METRICS}")        for key, value in self.weights.items():



This score is a configurable evidence-strength ranking—not a calibrated probability of truth. Its usefulness depends on the integrity of the evidence resolver and the quality signals it supplies. Missing measurements remain missing; the engine does not fill them with default scores.
4. Validate knowledge before storing it
validation.py
A knowledge item can be retained as unverified without being treated as an established fact. An item marked experimentally verified must pass stricter requirements than one supported by external documentation alone.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Sequencefrom .confidence import ConfidenceAssessment, EvidenceConfidenceEnginefrom .entities import (    EvidenceOrigin,    KnowledgeStatus,    VoiceKnowledge,)from .provenance import EvidenceResolver, ResolvedEvidence@dataclass(frozen=True)class KnowledgeValidationPolicy:    minimum_supporting_evidence: int    minimum_independent_sources: int    minimum_experiment_replicates: int    minimum_human_evaluations: int    minimum_supported_score: float    minimum_experimentally_verified_score: float    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        for name in (            "minimum_supporting_evidence",            "minimum_independent_sources",            "minimum_experiment_replicates",            "minimum_human_evaluations",        ):            if getattr(self, name) < 1:                raise ValueError(f"{name} must be positive")        for name in (            "minimum_supported_score",



The requirements are deliberately configurable. Load them from a reviewed application policy instead of choosing production thresholds merely to make the pipeline pass.

One limitation matters: this validator treats conflicting evidence as a reason to update the claim's status only when the configured minimum is met. It is not a full semantic reasoning system, and contextual exceptions still need to be represented using scope and the Phase 30/32 contradiction mechanisms.
5. Enforce legal knowledge transitions
lifecycle.py
Knowledge should not move silently from a hypothesis to a verified fact. Each change in status should create a durable lifecycle event.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetimefrom uuid import uuid4from .entities import KnowledgeStatus, VoiceKnowledge, utc_nowALLOWED_TRANSITIONS: dict[KnowledgeStatus, frozenset[KnowledgeStatus]] = {    KnowledgeStatus.UNVERIFIED: frozenset({        KnowledgeStatus.SUPPORTED,        KnowledgeStatus.CONTRADICTED,    }),    KnowledgeStatus.SUPPORTED: frozenset({        KnowledgeStatus.EXPERIMENTALLY_VERIFIED,        KnowledgeStatus.CONTRADICTED,        KnowledgeStatus.SUPERSEDED,    }),    KnowledgeStatus.EXPERIMENTALLY_VERIFIED: frozenset({        KnowledgeStatus.CONTRADICTED,        KnowledgeStatus.SUPERSEDED,    }),    KnowledgeStatus.CONTRADICTED: frozenset({        KnowledgeStatus.SUPPORTED,        KnowledgeStatus.SUPERSEDED,    }),    KnowledgeStatus.SUPERSEDED: frozenset(),}@dataclass(frozen=True)class KnowledgeLifecycleEvent:    event_id: str    knowledge_id: str    previous_status: KnowledgeStatus



A revalidation of contradicted knowledge must supply new supporting evidence before restoring its status. A superseded record remains in the database for historical reconstruction.
6. Detect contradictions without erasing either claim
contradiction.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetimefrom itertools import combinationsfrom typing import Sequencefrom uuid import uuid4from .entities import ClaimStance, VoiceKnowledge, utc_now@dataclass(frozen=True)class KnowledgeContradiction:    id: str    left_id: str    right_id: str    subject_key: str    explanation: str    unresolved: bool    created_at: datetimeclass ContradictionDetector:    """    Detects direct disagreements only when records share the same canonical    subject key and have opposite explicit stances.    General semantic contradictions need a separate, evidence-linked    semantic comparison provider.    """    async def detect(        self,        items: Sequence[VoiceKnowledge],    ) -> tuple[KnowledgeContradiction, ...]:        conflicts: list[KnowledgeContradiction] = []



This direct detector does not infer that two differently worded statements contradict each other. It provides the deterministic baseline for Phase 31; later, Phase 32 can supply graph-based semantic relationships and evidence-backed causal reasoning.
7. Add the persistent PostgreSQL store
schema.sql
Apply this schema to the PostgreSQL database used by Aariya. It stores knowledge, its evidence references, status transitions, and contradictions in separate tables.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_knowledge (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    knowledge_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    statement TEXT NOT NULL,
    subject_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    stance TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    confidence_score DOUBLE PRECISION,
    confidence_profile JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    scope JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    supersedes_id TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    search_vector TSVECTOR GENERATED ALWAYS AS (
        to_tsvector('simple', statement || ' ' || subject_key)
    ) STORED,

    CHECK (
        confidence_score IS NULL OR
        (confidence_score >= 0 AND confidence_score <= 1)
    )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_search
    ON voice_knowledge USING GIN (search_vector);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_status
    ON voice_knowledge(status);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_subject
    ON voice_knowledge(subject_key);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_updated
    ON voice_knowledge(updated_at DESC);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_knowledge_evidence (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    knowledge_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_knowledge(id),
    origin TEXT NOT NULL,
    reference_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    description TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),

    UNIQUE (knowledge_id, origin, reference_id)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_evidence_reference
    ON voice_knowledge_evidence(origin, reference_id);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_knowledge_lifecycle (
    event_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    knowledge_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_knowledge(id),
    previous_status TEXT NOT NULL,
    new_status TEXT NOT NULL,
    reason TEXT NOT NULL,
    evidence_refs JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'::jsonb,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_lifecycle_history
    ON voice_knowledge_lifecycle(knowledge_id, created_at);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_knowledge_contradictions (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    left_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_knowledge(id),
    right_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_knowledge(id),
    subject_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    explanation TEXT NOT NULL,
    unresolved BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    CHECK (left_id < right_id),
    UNIQUE (left_id, right_id)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_knowledge_unresolved_conflicts
    ON voice_knowledge_contradictions(unresolved)
    WHERE unresolved = TRUE;


A production database migration should also establish the correct database role permissions, connection pool, backup procedures, and retention requirements. Do not create a second PostgreSQL connection architecture if your application already has a shared one.
repository.py
This adapter uses an injected asyncpg connection pool. It stores real supplied knowledge and does not use an in-memory dictionary as the production store.
from __future__ import annotationsimport jsonfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .contradiction import KnowledgeContradictionfrom .entities import (    ClaimStance,    EvidenceOrigin,    KnowledgeEvidence,    KnowledgeStatus,    KnowledgeType,    VoiceKnowledge,)from .lifecycle import KnowledgeLifecycleEventclass VoiceKnowledgeRepository(Protocol):    async def save(self, knowledge: VoiceKnowledge) -> None: ...    async def update(self, knowledge: VoiceKnowledge) -> None: ...    async def get(self, knowledge_id: str) -> VoiceKnowledge | None: ...    async def search(        self,        query: str,        limit: int,    ) -> Sequence[VoiceKnowledge]: ...    async def find_by_subject(        self,        subject_key: str,        limit: int = 50,    ) -> Sequence[VoiceKnowledge]: ...    async def save_contradiction(        self,



The database's full-text search provides a usable lexical baseline. The KnowledgeRetriever below adds a decision-safe filtering layer; semantic retrieval can later be provided through your existing vector memory or pgvector system rather than duplicating vector infrastructure.
8. Retrieve previous knowledge for new problems
retrieval.py
Aariya needs both established findings and historical failures. Those serve different purposes: established knowledge can inform decisions, while a failed experiment can warn against repeating an approach under unchanged conditions.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom typing import Sequencefrom .entities import KnowledgeStatus, VoiceKnowledgefrom .repository import VoiceKnowledgeRepositoryclass KnowledgeRetriever:    def __init__(        self,        repository: VoiceKnowledgeRepository,    ) -> None:        self._repository = repository    async def retrieve(        self,        query: str,        limit: int = 20,    ) -> Sequence[VoiceKnowledge]:        if not query.strip():            return ()        return await self._repository.search(            query=query,            limit=limit,        )    async def retrieve_established(        self,        query: str,        limit: int = 20,    ) -> Sequence[VoiceKnowledge]:        """Return findings that met the configured validation criteria."""        items = await self.retrieve(query, limit)



For the first version, recall is PostgreSQL full-text search. When you connect semantic retrieval, preserve the knowledge status, scope, evidence references and conflict records in the returned results.
9. Consolidate knowledge without deleting history
consolidation.py
Consolidation identifies old findings explicitly superseded by newer ones. It does not silently erase a claim that conflicts with another.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Sequencefrom .contradiction import KnowledgeContradictionfrom .entities import KnowledgeStatus, VoiceKnowledge@dataclass(frozen=True)class ConsolidationResult:    retained_ids: tuple[str, ...]    superseded_ids: tuple[str, ...]    unresolved_ids: tuple[str, ...]class KnowledgeConsolidator:    async def consolidate(        self,        items: Sequence[VoiceKnowledge],        contradictions: Sequence[KnowledgeContradiction],    ) -> ConsolidationResult:        by_id = {item.id: item for item in items}        conflict_ids = {            knowledge_id            for conflict in contradictions            if conflict.unresolved            for knowledge_id in (conflict.left_id, conflict.right_id)        }        proposed_superseded: set[str] = set()        unresolved: set[str] = set()        for item in items:            if item.id in conflict_ids:



Notice that the consolidator only proposes supersession when the newer item explicitly references the old one, has a higher validation status, and has an identical context and stance. That is intentionally conservative. For example, evidence showing that pitch mapping succeeds for short answers but fails for long high-energy passages should become context-specific knowledge, not a global replacement of the older finding.
10. Connect validation, persistence and contradiction detection
controller.py
This is the main entry point for Phase 31. It validates a new knowledge item, stores it initially as unverified, records any lifecycle transition it earns, and discovers direct contradictions with existing findings.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, replacefrom typing import Sequencefrom .contradiction import (    ContradictionDetector,    KnowledgeContradiction,)from .entities import KnowledgeStatus, VoiceKnowledge, utc_nowfrom .lifecycle import KnowledgeLifecyclefrom .repository import VoiceKnowledgeRepositoryfrom .retrieval import KnowledgeRetrieverfrom .validation import KnowledgeValidator@dataclass(frozen=True)class KnowledgeLearningResult:    accepted: bool    knowledge: VoiceKnowledge | None    reason: str    contradictions: tuple[KnowledgeContradiction, ...] = ()class VoiceKnowledgeController:    def __init__(        self,        repository: VoiceKnowledgeRepository,        validator: KnowledgeValidator,        retriever: KnowledgeRetriever,        contradiction_detector: ContradictionDetector,        lifecycle: KnowledgeLifecycle,    ) -> None:        self._repository = repository        self._validator = validator        self._retriever = retriever



This controller intentionally rejects a duplicate knowledge ID instead of silently replacing a previous record. New evidence should lead to a new, auditable knowledge update or an explicit lifecycle transition.

Important deployment note: the code demonstrates the application flow, but the full operation is not yet transactionally atomic across evidence resolution, the knowledge row, lifecycle history, and contradiction records. For production, make the database writes a single transaction or use a transactional outbox so a partial failure cannot leave incomplete history.

11. Connect Phase 30 research and real experiment outcomes
Knowledge should be created from the actual outputs of the research, experiment, audio-analysis and human-evaluation systems—not from a provisional research hypothesis alone.
from backend.app.voice.knowledge import (    ClaimStance,    EvidenceOrigin,    KnowledgeEvidence,    KnowledgeType,    VoiceKnowledge,)# These identifiers and values must come from the actual evaluated run.# Do not use values generated for demonstration in production.knowledge = VoiceKnowledge.create(    knowledge_type=KnowledgeType.EXPERIMENT_RESULT,    statement=validated_experiment_finding,    subject_key=canonical_claim_key,    stance=ClaimStance.AFFIRMS,    evidence=(        KnowledgeEvidence(            origin=EvidenceOrigin.EXPERIMENT,            reference_id=actual_experiment_result_id,            description="Completed experiment and measured outcome",        ),        KnowledgeEvidence(            origin=EvidenceOrigin.AUDIO_ANALYSIS,            reference_id=actual_audio_evaluation_id,            description="Measured acoustic results for the candidate",        ),        KnowledgeEvidence(            origin=EvidenceOrigin.HUMAN_EVALUATION,            reference_id=actual_human_review_id,            description="Recorded human assessment of the real audio",        ),    ),    scope={        "provider_id": actual_provider_id,        "provider_version": actual_provider_version,



The identifiers used by EvidenceResolver must point to actual records, and its evidence metadata must come from the corresponding systems. The sample does not assume that an experiment was successful: the validator determines the resulting knowledge status from the resolved evidence and configured criteria.
12. Verify the implementation
Before connecting this to Aariya's live voice runtime, add tests for these behaviors:
Test	Expected behavior
Evidence reference does not exist	Reject the new knowledge item
Source integrity verification fails	Reject the new knowledge item
Evidence is real but insufficient	Store as unverified, not as established knowledge
Repeated controlled experiments support a claim and pass configured gates	May become experimentally verified
Two canonical claims have opposing stances	Record a contradiction and preserve both claims
A newer finding explicitly supersedes an older finding	Propose supersession without deleting history
Provider/model/policy scope differs	Do not silently reuse the old evidence as context-matched support
A new diagnosis matches a previous failed experiment	Return the historical finding with its status and evidence

The confidence policy, validator thresholds, source-resolution adapters, PostgreSQL schema and lifecycle controller must be wired to your real application configuration. I have not run these modules against your actual repository, so compatibility and production readiness remain unverified.
What Phase 31 adds to Aariya
Persistent knowledge

A durable record of observations, external evidence, experiment results and known failures.

Evidence-aware contradiction handling

Conflicting claims remain visible, with provenance, instead of being silently merged.

Knowledge lifecycle and retrieval

Aariya can recall earlier findings, distinguish validated knowledge from uncertainty, and retain the history of superseded approaches.

This implements the central objective of Phase 31 in your notes: a future diagnosis should be able to find what has already been learned, use the strongest relevant evidence, and avoid repeating approaches whose failures are already understood—unless new evidence justifies reconsidering them.


Phase 32 — Autonomous Voice Knowledge Graph & Causal Reasoning
Phase 31 gave Aariya persistent knowledge. Phase 32 connects that knowledge so Aariya can investigate how problems, voice controls, acoustic behavior, human perception and experiment outcomes relate to one another. Your notes explicitly require the graph to distinguish correlation from causation, preserve conflicting relationships, and keep inferred relationships as candidates until evidence supports them. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


For the first implementation, keep graph storage in PostgreSQL. Your architecture notes say a separate graph database is not necessary to establish the graph semantics. *voice TTS.txt


The graph will represent connections like:
Response strategy
       │
       └── may increase ──> Response density
                                  │
                                  └── may reduce ──> Pause opportunities
                                                           │
                                                           └── correlates with ──> Interruptions

Provider capability
       │
       └── enables ──> Voice parameter mapping
                            │
                            └── affects ──> Pitch contour
                                                │
                                                └── contributes to ──> Perceived expressiveness


These are example structures from your design, not established causal findings about Aariya. Each relationship in the real graph must carry evidence, context and a lifecycle status.

2. Define graph entities and relationships
entity.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom enum import Enumfrom typing import Any, Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4import reclass EntityType(str, Enum):    PROBLEM = "problem"    CAUSE = "cause"    VOICE_PARAMETER = "voice_parameter"    ACOUSTIC_PROPERTY = "acoustic_property"    USER_SIGNAL = "user_signal"    PERCEPTION = "perception"    EXPERIMENT = "experiment"    RESULT = "result"    PROVIDER_CAPABILITY = "provider_capability"    STRATEGY = "strategy"    KNOWLEDGE = "knowledge"@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphEntity:    id: str    entity_type: EntityType    name: str    canonical_key: str    description: str | None = None    properties: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        entity_type: EntityType,



The canonical key makes entity reuse consistent. If the same parameter appears in multiple provider versions or contexts, include the relevant scope in the key rather than accidentally merging distinct entities.
evidence.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetimefrom enum import Enumfrom typing import Mappingclass EvidencePolarity(str, Enum):    SUPPORTS = "supports"    CONTRADICTS = "contradicts"class StudyDesign(str, Enum):    OBSERVATIONAL = "observational"    CONTROLLED_EXPERIMENT = "controlled_experiment"    HUMAN_EVALUATION = "human_evaluation"    EXTERNAL_DOCUMENTATION = "external_documentation"    OTHER = "other"@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphEvidence:    reference_id: str    origin: str    description: str    polarity: EvidencePolarity    study_design: StudyDesign    # These fields come from the real Phase 31 evidence resolver.    integrity_verified: bool    quality_score: float | None    observed_at: datetime    independence_key: str | None = None



Graph evidence references the underlying memory, experiment or evaluation record. A production evidence adapter must verify those references before graph inference is allowed to rely on them.
relation.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom enum import Enumfrom typing import Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4from .evidence import GraphEvidenceclass RelationType(str, Enum):    CAUSED_BY = "caused_by"    INFLUENCES = "influences"    AFFECTS = "affects"    CORRELATES_WITH = "correlates_with"    ASSOCIATED_WITH = "associated_with"    VALIDATED_BY = "validated_by"    OBSERVED_IN = "observed_in"    DERIVED_FROM = "derived_from"    CONTRADICTS = "contradicts"    SUPPORTS = "supports"    REQUIRES = "requires"    ENABLES = "enables"    IMPROVES = "improves"    DEGRADES = "degrades"    SUPERSEDES = "supersedes"class RelationStatus(str, Enum):    OBSERVED = "observed"    CORRELATED = "correlated"    HYPOTHESIZED = "hypothesized"    EXPERIMENTALLY_SUPPORTED = "experimentally_supported"    CONTRADICTED = "contradicted"



For a CAUSED_BY edge, source_id is the effect and target_id is the candidate cause. This keeps graph direction consistent when traversing backward from a symptom to possible causes.

The distinction between relation type and relation status is essential: CAUSED_BY describes the claim being made, while HYPOTHESIZED or EXPERIMENTALLY_SUPPORTED describes the available support for that claim.
3. Measure causal evidence without inventing certainty
confidence.py
This engine preserves the evidence components behind a confidence score rather than treating a number as proof. Its weights and minimum reproduction requirements come from an injected policy.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Mapping, Sequencefrom .evidence import EvidencePolarity, GraphEvidence, StudyDesignDIMENSIONS = (    "evidence_quality",    "independent_sources",    "controlled_reproduction",    "human_evaluation",)@dataclass(frozen=True)class CausalConfidencePolicy:    weights: Mapping[str, float]    target_independent_sources: int    target_controlled_experiments: int    target_human_evaluations: int    contradiction_penalty: float    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if set(self.weights) != set(DIMENSIONS):            raise ValueError(f"weights must contain {DIMENSIONS}")        for name, value in self.weights.items():            if not isfinite(value) or value < 0:                raise ValueError(f"Invalid weight: {name}")        if abs(sum(self.weights.values()) - 1.0) > 1e-6:



A high score alone must not change a relation into a causal fact. The evidence must also meet the configured controlled-experiment and human-evaluation requirements, and the result must remain auditable.
4. Represent candidate causes and inferences
causal.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom uuid import uuid4@dataclass(frozen=True)class CausalHypothesis:    id: str    # Direction is explicit: the effect is hypothesized to be caused by this cause.    effect_id: str    cause_id: str    mechanism: str    prediction: str    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]    status: str = "hypothesized"    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        effect_id: str,        cause_id: str,        mechanism: str,        prediction: str,        evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...],    ) -> "CausalHypothesis":        if not effect_id or not cause_id or effect_id == cause_id:            raise ValueError("Distinct effect and cause entities are required")        if not mechanism.strip():            raise ValueError("Causal mechanism cannot be empty")        if not prediction.strip():



A hypothesis is not a confirmed relationship. For example, “provider pitch mapping may explain weak question intonation” is a candidate explanation. Its prediction could be that changing the calibrated mapping increases measured sentence-final pitch movement without causing unacceptable identity drift. The Phase 29 experiment pipeline must test that prediction against real results.
inference.py
This engine inspects graph paths and returns candidate interpretations, not automatic facts.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom .relation import GraphRelation, RelationStatus, RelationTypefrom .traversal import GraphPath@dataclass(frozen=True)class InferenceCandidate:    entity_ids: tuple[str, ...]    relation_ids: tuple[str, ...]    explanation: str    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    # False unless every step is an experimentally supported causal link.    causal_claim_allowed: bool    status: str = "candidate"class GraphInferenceEngine:    async def infer(        self,        paths: tuple[GraphPath, ...],    ) -> tuple[InferenceCandidate, ...]:        results: list[InferenceCandidate] = []        for path in paths:            if not path.relations:                continue            evidence_refs = tuple(sorted({                item.reference_id                for relation in path.relations



This is deliberately stricter than simply following edges. Correlation paths can help Aariya generate a question for further investigation, but they cannot be relabeled as causal explanations just because several relationships form a chain.
5. Preserve contradictory relationships
contradiction.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom itertools import combinationsfrom uuid import uuid4from typing import Sequencefrom .relation import GraphRelation, RelationType@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphContradiction:    id: str    left_relation_id: str    right_relation_id: str    explanation: str    detected_at: datetime    unresolved: bool = TrueOPPOSITE_RELATIONS = {    RelationType.IMPROVES: RelationType.DEGRADES,    RelationType.DEGRADES: RelationType.IMPROVES,    RelationType.SUPPORTS: RelationType.CONTRADICTS,    RelationType.CONTRADICTS: RelationType.SUPPORTS,}class GraphContradictionDetector:    """    Finds direct conflicts only when endpoints and context match.    A parameter improving naturalness while degrading identity consistency    is not a direct contradiction: those are different outcomes.    """



The graph should retain both branches when one experiment finds that a control improves naturalness while another finds it degrades identity. Phase 20’s strategy system can then reason over the trade-off instead of assuming one metric represents the whole quality of Aariya’s voice. *voice TTS.txt

6. Persist the knowledge graph in PostgreSQL
schema.sql
This schema separates entities, relationships, evidence supporting each relationship, and detected contradictions. It uses normalized tables rather than storing the whole graph in one mutable JSON document.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_graph_entities (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    entity_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    canonical_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    description TEXT,
    properties JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_entity_key
    ON voice_graph_entities(canonical_key);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_entity_type
    ON voice_graph_entities(entity_type);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_graph_relations (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    source_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_graph_entities(id),
    target_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES voice_graph_entities(id),
    relation_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    confidence_score DOUBLE PRECISION,
    confidence_profile JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    context JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    CHECK (source_id <> target_id),
    CHECK (
        confidence_score IS NULL OR
        (confidence_score >= 0 AND confidence_score <= 1)
    )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_source
    ON voice_graph_relations(source_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_target
    ON voice_graph_relations(target_id);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_relation_type_status
    ON voice_graph_relations(relation_type, status);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_graph_relation_evidence (
    relation_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_graph_relations(id),
    reference_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    origin TEXT NOT NULL,
    description TEXT NOT NULL,
    polarity TEXT NOT NULL,
    study_design TEXT NOT NULL,
    integrity_verified BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    quality_score DOUBLE PRECISION,
    observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    independence_key TEXT,
    experiment_id TEXT,
    human_evaluation_id TEXT,
    context JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    PRIMARY KEY (relation_id, origin, reference_id),

    CHECK (
        quality_score IS NULL OR
        (quality_score >= 0 AND quality_score <= 1)
    )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_evidence_reference
    ON voice_graph_relation_evidence(origin, reference_id);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS voice_graph_contradictions (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    left_relation_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_graph_relations(id),
    right_relation_id TEXT NOT NULL
        REFERENCES voice_graph_relations(id),
    explanation TEXT NOT NULL,
    unresolved BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    CHECK (left_relation_id <> right_relation_id),
    UNIQUE(left_relation_id, right_relation_id)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_voice_graph_open_contradictions
    ON voice_graph_contradictions(unresolved)
    WHERE unresolved = TRUE;


The existing PostgreSQL connection pool can back these tables. There is no requirement to add Neo4j until actual traversal and query workload shows a need for a dedicated graph database. *voice TTS.txt

repository.py
from __future__ import annotationsimport jsonfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .entity import EntityType, GraphEntityfrom .evidence import (    EvidencePolarity,    GraphEvidence,    StudyDesign,)from .relation import (    GraphRelation,    RelationStatus,    RelationType,)def decode_json(value: object) -> object:    if isinstance(value, str):        try:            return json.loads(value)        except json.JSONDecodeError:            return value    return valueclass KnowledgeGraphRepository(Protocol):    async def save_entity(self, entity: GraphEntity) -> None: ...    async def get_entity(self, entity_id: str) -> GraphEntity | None: ...    async def find_entities_by_key(        self,        canonical_key: str,        limit: int = 10,    ) -> Sequence[GraphEntity]: ...



The repository relies on PostgreSQL constraints to reject relationships with nonexistent endpoints. The controller will enforce the stronger evidence-validation policy before a new relationship is written.
7. Traverse multiple relationships
traversal.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom collections import dequefrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom .entity import GraphEntityfrom .relation import GraphRelation, RelationStatus, RelationTypefrom .repository import KnowledgeGraphRepository@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphPath:    entities: tuple[GraphEntity, ...]    relations: tuple[GraphRelation, ...]    @property    def depth(self) -> int:        return len(self.relations)class GraphTraversal:    def __init__(self, repository: KnowledgeGraphRepository) -> None:        self._repository = repository    async def breadth_first(        self,        start_id: str,        max_depth: int = 3,        max_paths: int = 100,        relation_types: tuple[RelationType, ...] = (),        statuses: tuple[RelationStatus, ...] = (),    ) -> tuple[GraphPath, ...]:        if not 0 <= max_depth <= 8:            raise ValueError("max_depth must be between 0 and 8")        if max_paths < 1:



This traversal is bounded by depth, path count and expansion count. It avoids cycles within a path, while still allowing different paths to reach the same entity.

By default, it follows outgoing edges. Since CAUSED_BY is modeled from effect to candidate cause, starting at a voice problem can lead toward potential causes. You can narrow traversal to specific relationship types and statuses when investigating a particular hypothesis.
8. Give Aariya a graph query interface
query.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom .causal import CausalHypothesisfrom .entity import EntityType, GraphEntityfrom .inference import GraphInferenceEngine, InferenceCandidatefrom .relation import RelationStatus, RelationTypefrom .repository import KnowledgeGraphRepositoryfrom .traversal import GraphPath, GraphTraversal@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphQuery:    entity_name: str    entity_type: EntityType | None = None    relation_types: tuple[RelationType, ...] = ()    statuses: tuple[RelationStatus, ...] = ()    max_depth: int = 3    max_paths: int = 50@dataclass(frozen=True)class GraphInvestigation:    start_entity: GraphEntity    paths: tuple[GraphPath, ...]    inferences: tuple[InferenceCandidate, ...]class KnowledgeGraphQueryEngine:    def __init__(        self,        repository: KnowledgeGraphRepository,        traversal: GraphTraversal,



CausalHypothesis is imported here for future integration with Phase 29. At this stage, the query engine returns paths and candidate inferences; it does not automatically construct a causal hypothesis from every path. That proposal should be made only when there is a concrete prediction and supporting evidence.
9. Control what enters the graph
controller.py
The controller resolves evidence references against Phase 31 before accepting relationships. It also enforces a policy gate before marking relationships experimentally supported.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, replacefrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .confidence import (    CausalConfidenceAssessment,    CausalConfidenceEngine,)from .contradiction import (    GraphContradiction,    GraphContradictionDetector,)from .entity import GraphEntityfrom .evidence import GraphEvidencefrom .query import GraphQuery, GraphInvestigation, KnowledgeGraphQueryEnginefrom .relation import GraphRelation, RelationStatusfrom .repository import KnowledgeGraphRepositoryclass GraphEvidenceVerifier(Protocol):    async def verify(        self,        evidence: GraphEvidence,    ) -> GraphEvidence | None:        """        Resolve the reference against Phase 31 and return trusted metadata.        Return None if the reference cannot be verified.        Do not trust caller-supplied integrity/quality claims.        """        ...@dataclass(frozen=True)class CausalPromotionPolicy:



This is a promotion gate, not proof that a statistical conclusion is valid. The quality of the gate depends on your real experiment records, human evaluations and policy configuration. If the required evidence is absent or contradictory, the relation is not promoted.

One operational requirement remains: all verification and persistence steps that affect a promotion should be coordinated through the application's transaction/outbox strategy so failures cannot leave partially approved relationships.

11. Phase 32 validation checklist
Before considering this implementation production-ready, test that:
Scenario	Expected result
Entity does not exist	Relationship is rejected
Evidence reference cannot be verified	Relationship is rejected
Observation shows correlation only	It is not promoted to a causal finding
Controlled experiment supports a relationship	It remains subject to the configured evaluation gate
Evidence conflicts with an existing relationship	Both records remain available and a contradiction is recorded
Naturalness improves but identity degrades	Both effects remain independently represented
Graph contains a cycle	Traversal terminates within its configured limits
Entity names are ambiguous	Query requires explicit resolution
Provider version changes	Old findings remain recorded with their original context

Implementation status: the code establishes the graph models, PostgreSQL repository, traversal, evidence-strength assessment, contradiction detection, query engine and controller. It has not been executed against your actual Aariya project, and the integration adapters, database migration, tests and deployment configuration still require verification.


Phase 33 — Autonomous Causal Experiment Selection
Phase 33 adds the decision layer above the Phase 32 knowledge graph. When several explanations for a voice problem are plausible, Aariya should choose the experiment that is expected to resolve the most important uncertainty while respecting identity constraints, safety, latency and cost. *voice TTS.txt
 *voice TTS.txt


This is different from optimization:
- Phase 33: Which experiment should we run next?
- Phase 27: Which candidate voice policy performs best?
- Phase 34: How do we expose a selected experiment safely and stop or roll it back if necessary?
We'll implement the selector and safety checks, then connect scheduling to your existing Phase 29/34 execution infrastructure. The estimators will require actual graph history, policy data, telemetry and experiment results; they will not invent scores when those inputs are unavailable.
1. Create the experiment-selection package
backend/app/voice/experiments/
├── __init__.py
├── variable.py
├── candidate.py
├── objective.py
├── uncertainty.py
├── information_value.py
├── risk.py
├── cost.py
├── constraints.py
├── selector.py
├── scheduler.py
├── execution.py
├── result.py
└── controller.py


The implementation below focuses on selection, risk-aware scoring, rejection records and the handoff. It deliberately leaves experiment execution behind an interface to the existing controlled pipeline.
2. Define real experiment candidates
variable.py
An experiment variable represents an actual, controlled difference from the active policy. Its baseline and candidate values must come from Aariya’s real calibration/optimizer—not a hardcoded example.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Any@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentVariable:    name: str    baseline: Any    candidate: Any    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.name.strip():            raise ValueError("Experiment variable name cannot be empty")        if self.baseline == self.candidate:            raise ValueError(                f"Variable {self.name!r} has no baseline/candidate difference"            )



candidate.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom typing import Any, Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4from .variable import ExperimentVariable@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentCandidate:    id: str    hypothesis_id: str    baseline_policy_id: str    variables: tuple[ExperimentVariable, ...]    objective_ids: tuple[str, ...]    # Evidence supporting the hypothesis this experiment is designed to test.    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    # Other competing hypotheses this experiment is expected to distinguish.    distinguishes_hypotheses: tuple[str, ...]    # Fraction of production traffic proposed for exposure.    # Actual exposure must still be enforced by Phase 34.    proposed_exposure_fraction: float    metadata: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)    created_at: datetime = field(        default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc)    )    @classmethod    def create(



The experiment’s metadata is descriptive, not a trusted authorization mechanism. The safety gate must independently verify the active policy, allowed variables, actual evidence and exposure rules.
objective.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Literal@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentObjective:    id: str    metric: str    direction: Literal["maximize", "minimize"]    minimum_confidence: float    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.id.strip() or not self.metric.strip():            raise ValueError("Objective ID and metric are required")        if self.direction not in {"maximize", "minimize"}:            raise ValueError("Invalid objective direction")        if not 0.0 <= self.minimum_confidence <= 1.0:            raise ValueError("minimum_confidence must be within [0, 1]")



Use metrics that Aariya can measure through its existing evaluation pipeline: naturalness, question-final pitch movement, identity consistency, latency, interruption rate or speech-rate alignment. A metric that has no actual measurement source must not be used to justify selecting an experiment. *voice TTS.txt

3. Model uncertainty and information value
uncertainty.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclass@dataclass(frozen=True)class Uncertainty:    entity_id: str    description: str    confidence: float | None    evidence_count: int    contradiction_count: int    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if self.confidence is not None:            if not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0:                raise ValueError("confidence must be within [0, 1]")        if self.evidence_count < 0 or self.contradiction_count < 0:            raise ValueError("Evidence counts cannot be negative")    @property    def uncertainty_level(self) -> float | None:        if self.confidence is None:            return None        return 1.0 - self.confidence



Unknown confidence remains None. The system must not silently turn missing confidence into 0.5 or another made-up value.
information_value.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidatefrom .uncertainty import Uncertainty@dataclass(frozen=True)class InformationValue:    expected_uncertainty_reduction: float    decision_relevance: float    # Provenance from the actual graph/history/model used for this forecast.    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    model_version: str    historical_experiment_refs: tuple[str, ...]    @property    def total(self) -> float:        return (            self.expected_uncertainty_reduction            * self.decision_relevance        )    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        for name in (            "expected_uncertainty_reduction",            "decision_relevance",        ):            value = getattr(self, name)            if not isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:                raise ValueError(f"{name} must be within [0, 1]")



This is the key part of Phase 33. A high-value experiment is one expected to separate competing explanations that matter to the current decision. Merely improving a metric is not the same as learning which cause explains the problem. *voice TTS.txt


The InformationForecastProvider is an integration contract; the actual estimator must be connected to your Phase 32 graph and historical experiment repository before the score has operational meaning.
4. Estimate risk and cost from real operating data
risk.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Protocolfrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidate@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentRisk:    identity_risk: float | None    conversational_risk: float | None    technical_risk: float | None    user_experience_risk: float | None    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        for name in (            "identity_risk",            "conversational_risk",            "technical_risk",            "user_experience_risk",        ):            value = getattr(self, name)            if value is None:                continue            if not isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:                raise ValueError(f"{name} must be within [0, 1]")    @property    def complete(self) -> bool:        return all(            getattr(self, name) is not None            for name in (



Identity risk is not interchangeable with the other risks. An experiment that improves expressiveness but degrades Aariya’s recognizable voice must be rejected by the configured identity gate, even when its overall score looks attractive.
cost.py
The selector cannot subtract raw milliseconds and currency units from a dimensionless score. The cost adapter must normalize observed/predicted latency, provider usage, compute and interaction costs against the application’s configured budgets.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Mapping, Protocolfrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidateCOST_DIMENSIONS = (    "latency",    "compute",    "provider",    "interaction",)@dataclass(frozen=True)class CostNormalizationPolicy:    weights: Mapping[str, float]    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if set(self.weights) != set(COST_DIMENSIONS):            raise ValueError(f"weights must contain {COST_DIMENSIONS}")        for name, value in self.weights.items():            if not isfinite(value) or value < 0:                raise ValueError(f"Invalid cost weight: {name}")        if abs(sum(self.weights.values()) - 1.0) > 1e-6:            raise ValueError("Cost weights must sum to 1")@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentCost:    # Raw costs/measurements, in their documented units.



The normalized component values must be computed by the estimator from real configuration, monitoring and cost data. Requiring the adapter to return evidence references makes the scoring decision auditable.
5. Build the experiment safety gate
constraints.py
Before scoring an experiment, Aariya should check whether it is eligible to run at all. A high information-value score cannot override a protected voice-identity control or an active safety restriction.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidate@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentSafetyContext:    active_policy_id: str    safety_policy_version: str    identity_policy_version: str    experiment_window_open: bool    user_is_speaking: bool    urgent_turn_active: bool    safety_override_active: bool    # Produced by the actual governed rollout/configuration service.    maximum_exposure_fraction: floatclass SafetyContextProvider(Protocol):    async def current(self) -> ExperimentSafetyContext:        ...class ActivePolicyReader(Protocol):    async def active_policy_id(self) -> str:        ...    async def get_value(        self,        policy_id: str,        variable_name: str,



The context provider and governance interfaces must query the real active policy and safety state. If those values cannot be obtained, the gate must fail closed. These checks are preliminary selection gates; Phase 34 must independently enforce isolation, controlled exposure, live monitoring and rollback.
6. Score candidates by information, risk and cost
Add to information_value.py
This concrete adapter obtains uncertainty from the actual graph and asks the configured forecasting service to estimate how a proposed experiment would reduce it.
from typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidatefrom .uncertainty import Uncertaintyclass CandidateUncertaintySource(Protocol):    async def for_candidate(        self,        candidate: ExperimentCandidate,        graph_context: object,    ) -> Sequence[Uncertainty]:        """Read the actual relevant uncertainty records from the graph."""        ...class InformationValueEstimator:    def __init__(        self,        forecast_provider: InformationForecastProvider,        uncertainty_source: CandidateUncertaintySource,    ) -> None:        self._forecast = forecast_provider        self._uncertainties = uncertainty_source    async def estimate(        self,        candidate: ExperimentCandidate,        graph_context: object,    ) -> InformationValue:        uncertainties = await self._uncertainties.for_candidate(            candidate,            graph_context,        )        if not uncertainties:



The forecast provider is responsible for deriving expected uncertainty reduction and decision relevance from real historical data and graph state. If it lacks sufficient evidence, it must raise an error rather than inventing an information value.
selector.py
from __future__ import annotations        risk_estimator: RiskEstimator,        cost_estimator: CostEstimator,        cost_policy: CostNormalizationPolicy,        policy: ExperimentSelectionPolicy,    ) -> None:        self._information = information_estimator        self._risk = risk_estimator        self._cost = cost_estimator        self._cost_policy = cost_policy        self._policy = policy    async def score(        self,        candidate: ExperimentCandidate,        graph_context: object,        current_state: object,    ) -> ExperimentScore:        information = await self._information.estimate(            candidate,            graph_context,        )        risk = await self._risk.estimate(candidate, current_state)        cost = await self._cost.estimate(candidate)        if risk.maximum is None:            raise EstimationUnavailable(                "Risk estimates are incomplete."            )        if not risk.evidence_refs:            raise EstimationUnavailable(                "Risk estimate has no actual evidence references."            )        if not cost.evidence_refs:            raise EstimationUnavailable(                "Cost estimate has no actual data references."            )        normalized_cost = cost.total_normalized(self._cost_policy)        # Information, risk and cost are normalized before comparison.        # Weights are governed configuration, not silent defaults.        final_score = (            self._policy.information_weight * information.total            - self._policy.risk_penalty * risk.maximum            - self._policy.cost_penalty * normalized_cost        )        return ExperimentScore(            experiment_id=candidate.id,            information_value=information.total,            risk=risk.maximum,            normalized_cost=normalized_cost,            final_score=final_score,            risk_detail=risk,            cost_detail=cost,            information_detail=information,            _limits={                "identity": self._policy.max_identity_risk,                "conversation": self._policy.max_conversational_risk,                "technical": self._policy.max_technical_risk,                "user_experience": self._policy.max_user_experience_risk,            },        )    async def select(        self,        candidates: Sequence[ExperimentCandidate],



The ranking formula is a decision interface, not a scientifically validated universal equation. Its weights and risk limits must be configured against Aariya’s actual operating objectives. The individual risk gates remain mandatory regardless of the final score. *voice TTS.txt

7. Schedule the selected experiment
scheduler.py
Selection does not imply that now is a suitable time to run an experiment. The scheduler uses a real execution-window provider to check conversational context and obtain an approved scheduling decision.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom typing import Protocolfrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidatefrom .selector import ExperimentScore@dataclass(frozen=True)class SafeExecutionWindow:    approved: bool    scheduled_at: datetime | None    reason: str    context_reference: str    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if self.approved and self.scheduled_at is None:            raise ValueError(                "An approved scheduling window needs a real scheduled_at value"            )        if self.approved and not self.context_reference.strip():            raise ValueError(                "Approved scheduling requires context provenance"            )class ExecutionWindowProvider(Protocol):    async def next_safe_window(        self,        candidate: ExperimentCandidate,    ) -> SafeExecutionWindow:        """        Inspect the actual conversation, active policy, and experiment        execution controls. Do not invent a safe window or timestamp.



A real window provider might defer testing while the user is speaking, a conversation is urgent, the active policy changes, or the Phase 34 canary controller is not ready.
8. Keep execution connected to Phase 29/34
execution.py
Phase 33 chooses an experiment. It should not create a second system that modifies live voice configuration independently.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom typing import Protocolfrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidatefrom .scheduler import ScheduledExperimentclass ApprovedExperimentDispatcher(Protocol):    async def dispatch(        self,        candidate: ExperimentCandidate,        schedule: ScheduledExperiment,        authorization_id: str,    ) -> str:        """        Hand off to the existing Phase 29/34 controlled experiment pipeline. There is intentionally no implementation here that writes live voice parameters or autonomously edits Python source. Those actions remain outside the experiment selector.
9. Record actual experiment results
result.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom datetime import datetime, timezonefrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Mappingfrom uuid import uuid4@dataclass(frozen=True)class ExperimentResult:    id: str    experiment_id: str    hypothesis_id: str    baseline_policy_id: str    candidate_policy_id: str    # Metrics must come from the actual measurement pipeline.    metrics: Mapping[str, float]    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]    completed_at: datetime    successful: bool    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        *,        experiment_id: str,        hypothesis_id: str,        baseline_policy_id: str,        candidate_policy_id: str,        metrics: Mapping[str, float],        evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...],        successful: bool,    ) -> "ExperimentResult":



This result object does not manufacture data or declare an experiment successful on the basis of one metric. The success decision must come from your existing baseline/candidate comparator and governed release criteria, including naturalness, identity consistency, timing, latency and applicable safety checks.
10. Orchestrate selection, scheduling and handoff
controller.py
The controller persists selection decisions, including rejected candidates. Scheduling and execution remain separate steps, so Aariya cannot accidentally interpret “highest score” as permission to run an experiment.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom typing import Protocol, Sequencefrom .candidate import ExperimentCandidatefrom .constraints import ExperimentSafetyGatefrom .execution import (    ApprovedExperimentDispatcher,    ExperimentAuthorizationProvider,)from .scheduler import ExperimentScheduler, ScheduledExperimentfrom .selector import (    ExperimentScore,    ExperimentSelector,    SelectionReport,)class ExperimentDecisionRepository(Protocol):    async def record_selection(        self,        candidates: Sequence[ExperimentCandidate],        report: SelectionReport,    ) -> None:        """Persist ranking, evidence references and all rejection reasons."""        ...    async def record_schedule(        self,        schedule: ScheduledExperiment,    ) -> None:        ...    async def record_dispatch(        self,        experiment_id: str,



The dispatcher must verify the authorization and current policy again when execution actually begins. The selection controller cannot guarantee that a candidate remains safe merely because it passed an earlier check.

Also, if a database write fails while recording a decision, the production implementation should use a transaction or transactional outbox so the handoff and audit log cannot silently diverge.

selection = await controller.select(    candidates=actual_experiment_candidates,    graph_context=actual_current_graph_snapshot,    current_state=actual_voice_runtime_state,)print("Selection:", selection.reason)for score in selection.scores:    print(        "Candidate:", score.experiment_id,        "Information value:", score.information_value,        "Risk:", score.risk,        "Normalized cost:", score.normalized_cost,        "Score:", score.final_score,    )for rejected in selection.rejected:    print(        "Rejected:", rejected.experiment_id,        "Reasons:", rejected.reasons,    )if selection.selected_candidate is not None:    scheduled = await controller.schedule_selected(selection)    if scheduled is not None:        print("Scheduled experiment:", scheduled.experiment_id)        print("Scheduled at:", scheduled.scheduled_at.isoformat())        print("Reason:", scheduled.reason)



At this point, the selected experiment is only scheduled. The actual queue/worker should dispatch it through Phase 29/34 when the approved window arrives. Do not immediately call the dispatcher for a future scheduled time.

The selection record should capture the candidate IDs, relevant hypothesis, graph/evidence references, normalized risk and cost estimates, policy versions, rejected alternatives, chosen experiment and selection rationale. This is how Aariya can later explain why an experiment was selected and whether it actually reduced uncertainty.
Two integration safeguards to add
First, the import list in the wiring example should not import CausalConfidencePolicy from backend.app.voice.experiments. It belongs to the Phase 32 graph module and is not needed by ExperimentSelector.

Second, prevent a scheduler worker from dispatching a future-dated experiment. Add this check near the start of dispatch_selected() in controller.py, before authorization:
from datetime import datetime, timezoneif datetime.now(timezone.utc) < schedule.scheduled_at:    raise RuntimeError(        "Experiment is not due yet; keep it in the approved schedule queue."    )



Also validate at startup that the supplied safety-policy versions and risk limits are present, and reject selection if the underlying context or policy service is unavailable. A service outage must not be interpreted as a low-risk experiment.
12. Phase 33 verification checklist
Before calling this phase production-ready, verify these cases against the real adapters:
Scenario	Required behavior
No relevant uncertainty is available	Do not invent an information score
Risk estimator has missing dimensions	Reject the candidate from selection
Active policy has changed since candidate generation	Reject the stale candidate
Candidate touches protected identity controls	Reject it
Candidate changes too many variables	Reject it under the configured policy
More than one experiment has a positive score	Rank eligible candidates using governed weights
All candidates have unacceptable scores or risk	Return no selection
User is speaking or the turn is urgent	Defer scheduling
Authorization is denied	Do not dispatch
Experiment produces real results	Pass them to the comparison and knowledge-update systems
An experiment failed previously	Retain the failure evidence; reconsider it only if the underlying conditions have changed

Your notes define full Phase 33 completion as more than selecting a candidate: Aariya must execute against real audio through the controlled pipeline, measure actual output, compare it with baseline, update the graph, retain failed experiments and avoid repeating conclusively failed approaches without new evidence. *voice TTS.txt


Implementation status: the selection models, scoring interfaces, safety checks, scheduler interface and controlled-dispatch boundary are provided. The actual information-value model, risk/cost estimators, governance adapters, audit repository and Phase 29/34 dispatcher still need to be connected and tested against your codebase. I have not run this integration, so it is not yet verified as production-ready.


Phase 34 — Autonomous Voice Experiment Execution & Adaptive Canarying
Phase 33 selected which experiment Aariya should run next. Phase 34 gives Aariya a controlled way to test that experiment against real voice sessions, monitor the results continuously, stop unsafe changes, and restore the previous voice policy when necessary. This follows the sequence in your voice-system notes: validate → isolate → canary → measure → stop or continue → evaluate → promote or roll back. 
*voice TTS.txt

The critical design rule is that a selected experiment must never automatically become the production voice for everyone.

1. Phase 34 architecture
Phase 33: Selected experiment

Hypothesis, candidate, baseline, evidence and objectives

Validate and authorize

Check policy identity, approved changes, evidence and rollout authorization

Isolated adaptive canary

The stable policy remains the default; eligible real sessions are assigned consistently

Live observation and evaluation

Real audio measurements, latency, identity consistency, failures and statistical evidence

Stop / rollback

Unsafe regression, identity drift or unreliable telemetry

Complete / review

Enough real evidence, checks passed, promotion review required

The implementation below creates the core module contracts and the control flow. Your notes also require every experiment transition to be recorded, the baseline to be snapshotted before rollout, and failures to feed the knowledge system rather than merely being labelled “failed.” 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

2. Create the directory structure
Add these modules beneath the existing Phase 33 experiment package:

backend/app/voice/experiments/
├── canary/
│   ├── __init__.py
│   ├── rollout.py
│   ├── allocation.py
│   ├── thresholds.py
│   ├── stop_conditions.py
│   ├── decision.py
│   └── controller.py
└── rollback/
    ├── __init__.py
    ├── snapshot.py
    ├── controller.py
    └── recovery.py
Keep Phase 33's candidate selection and scoring modules. Phase 34 consumes their selected experiment; it should not duplicate or silently bypass that selection process.

3. Explicit lifecycle and audit trail
File: backend/app/voice/experiments/canary/rollout.py

Every experiment needs a persistent status and an auditable record of each transition. The repository must implement the status change and audit-event insertion in the same database transaction.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from enum import Enum
from typing import Protocol


class ExperimentStatus(str, Enum):
    CREATED = "created"
    VALIDATED = "validated"
    SCHEDULED = "scheduled"
    CANARY = "canary"
    MONITORING = "monitoring"
    STOPPED = "stopped"
    COMPLETED = "completed"
    PROMOTED = "promoted"
    ROLLED_BACK = "rolled_back"
    REJECTED = "rejected"


_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS: dict[
    ExperimentStatus, frozenset[ExperimentStatus]
] = {
    ExperimentStatus.CREATED: frozenset({
        ExperimentStatus.VALIDATED,
        ExperimentStatus.REJECTED,
    }),
    ExperimentStatus.VALIDATED: frozenset({
        ExperimentStatus.SCHEDULED,
        ExperimentStatus.REJECTED,
    }),
    ExperimentStatus.SCHEDULED: frozenset({
        ExperimentStatus.CANARY,
        ExperimentStatus.REJECTED,
    }),

Important: compare_and_record_transition() is a database contract, not an implemented database adapter yet. A real PostgreSQL implementation should use a transaction and compare-and-set semantics so two workers cannot simultaneously advance the same experiment.

The lifecycle deliberately has no direct CANARY → PROMOTED path. The experiment has to reach the evaluation and review stage first.

4. Adaptive canary allocation
Aariya should not expose every candidate to the same fraction of sessions. The allocation should use real evidence about risk, uncertainty, previous evidence, identity sensitivity, observed stability and failure rate. Your notes explicitly caution against pretending that a hardcoded percentage is an intelligent allocation policy. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/experiments/canary/allocation.py

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import hmac
import math
from dataclasses import dataclass


def _check_unit_interval(name: str, value: float) -> None:
    if not math.isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be between 0 and 1.")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ExposureEvidence:
    # These values must be returned by actual evidence providers.
    risk: float
    uncertainty: float
    prior_evidence_strength: float
    identity_sensitivity: float
    observed_stability: float
    failure_rate: float
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]
    source_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "risk",
            "uncertainty",
            "prior_evidence_strength",
            "identity_sensitivity",
            "observed_stability",
            "failure_rate",
        ):
            _check_unit_interval(name, getattr(self, name))


The allocation formula is a transparent starting policy—not something that has already learned reliable probabilities. Its inputs and bounds need to come from actual measurements and validated configuration.

The persistent session assignment store is still required. Use the HMAC-based fingerprint as the storage key and implement atomic get-or-create semantics so retries or concurrent requests cannot switch a session from baseline to candidate halfway through an experiment. Check session eligibility and required consent before assigning a candidate.

5. Versioned monitoring thresholds
File: backend/app/voice/experiments/canary/thresholds.py

Do not invent thresholds for identity drift or latency in the controller. Read them from a validated, versioned voice-release policy.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MetricGuard:
    metric: str

    # Candidate regression allowed by the validated release policy.
    maximum_regression: float

    # Optional emergency bound for a severe observed regression.
    emergency_regression: float | None

    # Number of observations needed for statistical stop evaluation.
    minimum_samples: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.metric.strip():
            raise ValueError("Metric name is required.")

        if self.maximum_regression < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "Maximum regression cannot be negative."
            )

        if self.emergency_regression is not None:
            if self.emergency_regression < self.maximum_regression:
                raise ValueError(
                    "Emergency regression must be at least as "
                    "large as the ordinary regression threshold."
                )

        if self.minimum_samples < 1:

The actual policy may include first-audio latency, end-to-end latency, interruption frequency, speech quality, pitch statistics, identity consistency, stream integrity and synchronization errors. Your notes identify those as relevant real measurements; the metric names, statistical definitions and acceptable boundaries must match Aariya's actual telemetry and validation framework. 
*voice TTS.txt

6. Stop conditions: hard failures versus soft regressions
File: backend/app/voice/experiments/canary/stop_conditions.py

The monitoring system needs to distinguish an immediate critical failure from a small fluctuation. It also needs to stop or fail closed if its own measurements are not reliable.

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Mapping, Protocol

from .thresholds import StopThresholds, ThresholdProvider


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MetricObservation:
    metric: str
    baseline_value: float
    candidate_value: float

    # Regression is normalized by the real metric evaluator:
    # a larger positive value always means "worse".
    regression_estimate: float

    # Lower confidence bound for regression, computed using
    # the evaluator's documented statistical method.
    regression_lower_bound: float | None

    sample_count: int
    comparison_valid: bool
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        values = [
            self.baseline_value,
            self.candidate_value,
            self.regression_estimate,
        ]
        if not all(math.isfinite(value) for value in values):
            raise ValueError("Metric values must be finite.")

How the stop engine behaves
Observed condition

Behavior

Audio corruption, a safety incident, or a critical synchronization failure

Stop the candidate immediately

Identity boundary violation

Stop and invoke the configured rollback/recovery path

Severe regression exceeding an approved emergency threshold

Stop

Smaller regression without sufficient statistical evidence

Continue collecting data; do not count it as success

Missing or unreliable required telemetry

Stop exposure rather than guess

Metrics are within limits

Continue monitoring; success is not yet established

The hard_failure_codes must originate from actual detectors or trusted incident records. The monitoring adapter must not classify arbitrary errors as critical merely to trigger a desired outcome.

7. Statistical evaluation before declaring success
File: backend/app/voice/experiments/canary/decision.py

A candidate should not be accepted just because one measured value is higher than the baseline. Your source calls for effect size, variance, sample size, confidence intervals, matched baseline data, experiment duration, confounders and human evaluation where necessary. 
*voice TTS.txt

The evaluator should obtain that information from real observations and return an evidence object. It must not fabricate missing samples or infer that an incomplete experiment has passed.
