8. Capture an exact baseline snapshot
Rollback is only dependable if Aariya knows exactly which approved policy and configuration were active before the canary began. Your notes require a version chain rather than silently overwriting the original voice policy. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/experiments/rollback/snapshot.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PolicySnapshot:
    policy_id: str
    version: str

    # An immutable reference to the exact approved configuration.
    immutable_config_ref: str

    # Digest of that exact configuration, obtained from the registry.
    config_sha256: str

    captured_at: datetime

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "policy_id",
            "version",
            "immutable_config_ref",
            "config_sha256",
        ):
            if not getattr(self, name).strip():
                raise ValueError(f"{name} is required.")

        if (
            self.captured_at.tzinfo is None
            or self.captured_at.utcoffset() is None
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Snapshot time must be timezone-aware."
            )

The config_sha256 must be calculated by the real policy/configuration registry from the actual stored configuration. Do not insert an example hash or manually assign one.

Persist the snapshot before enabling candidate routing. If the baseline version or hash cannot be verified, the canary must not start.

9. Rollback controller
File: backend/app/voice/experiments/rollback/controller.py

The rollback ordering is deliberate: disable the experiment first, verify that candidate exposure has stopped, restore the exact baseline, and confirm the resulting active policy.

10. Record failures as reusable knowledge

*voice TTS.txt

13. Fail closed when monitoring infrastructure fails
One last safeguard is important: the monitor itself can fail. An exception from the telemetry store, evaluation provider or checkpoint writer must not leave a candidate running indefinitely.

Add this contract to canary/controller.py:

class RuntimeFailureStore(Protocol):
    async def record_runtime_failure(
        self,
        *,
        experiment_id: str,
        stage: str,
        error_type: str,
        detail: str,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Persist the real runtime failure and return its durable
        evidence reference. Keep secrets and raw sensitive audio
        out of the diagnostic detail.
        """
        ...

Inject an implementation as runtime_failures into AdaptiveCanaryRunner. Then replace the observation/evaluation/checkpoint portion of monitor_once() with this failure-aware block:

        try:
            observations = await self._observations.observe(
                experiment_id
            )
            stop = await self._stop_engine.evaluate(observations)

            decision = None

            if stop.stop:
                checkpoint_decision = "stop"
                checkpoint_reason = stop.reason
            else:
                evaluation = (
                    await self._evaluation_provider.evaluate(
                        experiment,
                        snapshot,
                        observations,
                    )
                )
                decision = self._decision_engine.decide(evaluation)
                checkpoint_decision = decision.action.value
                checkpoint_reason = decision.reason

            if not observations.evidence_refs:
                raise RuntimeError(
                    "Monitoring returned no auditable evidence reference."
                )

            await self._audit.record_checkpoint(
                experiment_id=experiment_id,
                observation_reference=observations.evidence_refs[0],
                decision=checkpoint_decision,
                reason=checkpoint_reason,
            )

        except Exception as exc:

The controller's constructor must accept and store that new runtime_failures dependency. This patch assumes that its persistence adapter returns a genuine, durable reference; it should never manufacture one when logging fails.

Also, in CanaryStartService.start(), do not use a finally block to mark a failed startup as rolled back. Transition to ROLLED_BACK only after RollbackController.rollback() returns successfully. If restoration fails, keep the failure visible, raise the incident and do not claim that the baseline is active.

14. Integration order and completion criteria
Implement the adapters in this order so Aariya never gains uncontrolled production access while the system is incomplete.

Connect the real policy registry and session router. Baseline must remain the default for sessions that are not eligible or assigned to the canary. Store assignments consistently and attach actual experiment, policy, provider, configuration and generation IDs to each measured output.

Connect real metrics and the validated threshold policy. Obtain observations from actual TTS sessions. Confirm that failures, missing telemetry and invalid metric comparisons trigger the configured safe response.

Connect durable PostgreSQL storage. Persist the lifecycle, transitions, policy snapshot, allocation evidence, live checkpoints, evaluation evidence, rollback outcome and failure-memory references. Use atomic status changes and idempotent recovery.

Connect Phase 31–33. Publish actual experiment outcomes to the knowledge memory and graph. Have the selector check previous failures against the current configuration fingerprint before suggesting a repeat.

Run isolated tests before real canaries. Test rejected authorization, baseline-version mismatch, zero exposure, duplicate session assignment, critical identity drift, missing telemetry, evaluator failure, rollback failure, insufficient evidence and interrupted promotion. Verify that every failed rollback raises an incident and that no incomplete evaluation can promote a policy.



Phase 35 — Autonomous Voice Policy Synthesis
Phase 34 established controlled voice experiments. Phase 35 turns validated findings from those experiments into one coherent, context-aware voice policy. This is the central layer between Aariya's cognition and her TTS provider: it decides the intended delivery first, then translates that intention into supported voice controls. 
*voice TTS.txt

The most important design distinction is between what Aariya wants to communicate and how her voice produces it. A goal such as warmth should not become a fixed pitch or speed value. The appropriate combination of articulation, timing, pitch movement and energy depends on the conversation, Aariya's internal state, the linguistic structure, provider capabilities and validated evidence. 
*voice TTS.txt

1. Phase 35 architecture
Inputs from Aariya's existing systems

Internal state · Conversation · Linguistics · User rhythm · Relationship context · Identity · Provider capabilities · Validated knowledge

Policy synthesis

Derive objectives, choose controls, preserve evidence and record reasons

Policy validation and identity gate

Structure · Constraints · Provider compatibility · Evidence · Regression checks

Immutable policy version

Candidate → validated → explicitly approved for activation

Runtime performance plan → provider adapter → actual TTS

This phase does not let Aariya rewrite her source code or activate an unvalidated policy. The policy stays stable while the delivery can adapt to each conversation. Your notes explicitly call for versioning, provider-capability awareness, explainability and protection against personality drift. 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Define the policy schema
File: backend/app/voice/policy/schema.py

The schema keeps objectives, provider-independent controls, constraints, evidence references and version history separate. It also rejects invalid objective weights instead of allowing malformed policy candidates to proceed.

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any
from uuid import uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoicePolicy:
    id: str
    version: str
    previous_policy_id: str | None

    # What the voice should achieve.
    objectives: dict[str, float]

    # How the voice is instructed to realize those objectives.
    controls: dict[str, Any]

    # Invariants that adaptation must never violate.
    constraints: dict[str, Any]

    # References to actual evidence records, not copied claims.
    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]

    metadata: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        *,
        version: str,
        previous_policy_id: str | None,
        objectives: dict[str, float],
        controls: dict[str, Any],

The weights are not hardcoded in this schema. They must come from Aariya's current strategy and validated policy inputs. The ID is generated for each new candidate; a production repository must persist the candidate and its version history.

5. Keep objectives separate from priorities and controls
File: backend/app/voice/policy/priorities.py

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PolicyPriority:
    objective: str
    weight: float
    source_version: str
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.objective.strip():
            raise ValueError("Objective is required.")

        if not math.isfinite(self.weight) or self.weight < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "Priority weight must be finite and non-negative."
            )

        if not self.source_version.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Priority must identify its source version."
            )

        if not self.evidence_refs:
            raise ValueError(
                "Priority needs traceable source evidence."
            )


def resolve_priorities(
    priorities: tuple[PolicyPriority, ...],
) -> dict[str, float]:

An example set of objective names might include clarity, responsiveness, naturalness, warmth, expressiveness and identity consistency. These are not universal rankings: Aariya's active strategy and validated constraints determine which objectives matter most in a given situation. Your source explicitly says to derive priorities from the strategy/state system rather than fixed emotion mappings. 
*voice TTS.txt

6. Add hard constraint validation
File: backend/app/voice/policy/constraints.py

Identity, provider limits, articulation, latency and safety are constraints, not objectives that can simply be traded away for a better score.

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Mapping


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PolicyConstraint:
    name: str
    minimum: float | None = None
    maximum: float | None = None
    required_value: Any = None
    has_required_value: bool = False

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Constraint name is required.")

        if (
            self.minimum is not None
            and not math.isfinite(self.minimum)
        ):
            raise ValueError("Minimum must be finite.")

        if (
            self.maximum is not None
            and not math.isfinite(self.maximum)
        ):
            raise ValueError("Maximum must be finite.")

        if (
            self.minimum is not None
            and self.maximum is not None
            and self.minimum > self.maximum
        ):

This engine checks basic value constraints. The production integration also needs an identity validator that compares the actual voice against Aariya's calibrated identity boundaries, as well as provider and safety validators. Numeric bounds alone cannot establish voice identity consistency.

7. Create context-sensitive policy rules
File: backend/app/voice/policy/rules.py

Rules apply when the actual conversational conditions justify them. This is different from mapping an emotion label directly to fixed acoustic settings.

*voice TTS.txt

8. Build the policy synthesizer

Why unresolved contradictions block synthesis
Suppose the knowledge graph contains two findings:

More emphasis improves perceived clarity during technical explanations.

Excessive emphasis increases perceived artificiality and harms voice identity consistency.

The synthesizer must account for both. It cannot merely increase emphasis because one metric improved. Your source describes precisely this kind of evidence-backed trade-off. 
*voice TTS.txt

The derivation provider should obtain findings from real knowledge and experiment records. It must not generate a plausible-sounding “validated” result when no corresponding observation exists.

9. Validate every candidate before it can be used
File: backend/app/voice/policy/validator.py

The validator combines the hard constraint engine with independent identity, provider-compatibility, regression and evidence checks. The safety checks must use the current approved policy, not constraints proposed by the candidate itself.

    violations: tuple[str, ...]

The identity and regression gates are integration interfaces, not implemented detectors. Connect them to Aariya's actual calibration and validation systems. An unavailable gate must raise an error and block activation; it must not return passed=True simply because no failure was reported.

10. Version policies without overwriting them
File: backend/app/voice/policy/versioning.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum


class PolicyStatus(str, Enum):
    CANDIDATE = "candidate"
    VALIDATED = "validated"
    ACTIVE = "active"
    SUPERSEDED = "superseded"
    REJECTED = "rejected"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PolicyVersion:
    policy_id: str
    version: str
    previous_policy_id: str | None
    status: PolicyStatus

    validation_report_ref: str | None = None
    activation_approval_ref: str | None = None

The repository should assign each candidate an immutable version and retain its predecessor, validation report, source evidence and activation history. A new candidate must not edit the currently active version in place. 
*voice TTS.txt

11. Protect activation in the policy registry
File: backend/app/voice/policy/registry.py

The registry uses atomic compare-and-swap activation. An approval for one candidate cannot activate a different candidate, nor can an outdated candidate overwrite a policy that has changed since validation.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .schema import VoicePolicy
from .versioning import PolicyStatus, PolicyVersion

    approval_reference: str
    approved_by: str

    async def get_active(self) -> PolicyVersion | None:
        ...

        self,
        policy_id: str,
    ) -> PolicyVersion | None:
        ...

    async def get_policy(
        self,
        policy_id: str,
    ) -> VoicePolicy | None:
        ...

    async def save_candidate(
        self,
        policy: VoicePolicy,

The registry deliberately separates candidate creation, validation and activation. Its security depends on the repository enforcing the state transition transactionally and the approval verifier consulting a real authorization source.

12. Explain every policy decision
File: backend/app/voice/policy/explanation.py

Aariya should be able to trace why a voice decision happened using observable state, supporting evidence, applicable constraints and the controls actually sent to the provider—not an invented explanation about an internal feeling. This follows the explainability design in your notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)

    controls_applied: tuple[str, ...]

Store the trace alongside the generation/session record, using your normal privacy and retention controls. Avoid putting raw conversation content, private user data or secret provider configuration into an audit record when references and derived measurements are sufficient.

13. Resolve the policy for the current conversation
File: backend/app/voice/policy/resolver.py

This module starts from the stable policy, applies registered context rules, and rechecks the hard constraints before handing the result to the provider translation layer.

14. Translate into actual provider-supported controls
File: backend/app/voice/policy/runtime.py

The abstract policy must not pretend a TTS provider supports a control that is unavailable. A missing optional control may degrade gracefully, but a missing required control or an invalid provider response must stop the generation from using that plan.

from __future__ import annotations

One integration requirement is especially important: the hard constraints passed to this runtime must come from the trusted active identity/release configuration—not from the synthesized candidate's own proposed constraints.

16. Refine provider translation
One important implementation detail: Aariya's abstract controls and a provider's API fields are not necessarily named or represented the same way. The resolver must preserve the abstract control names for auditing, while the adapter constructs the provider-specific request.

Replace the ProviderTranslation definition in runtime.py with this version:

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderTranslation:
    provider_id: str
    provider_config_version: str

    # The actual request payload accepted by the provider adapter.
    provider_payload: Mapping[str, Any]

    # Abstract controls the adapter successfully represented.
    applied_control_names: frozenset[str]

    unsupported_controls: tuple[str, ...]
    limitations: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]

Update ResolvedPerformancePlan to retain that payload:

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResolvedPerformancePlan:
    policy_id: str
    policy_version: str

    requested_controls: Mapping[str, Any]
    applied_control_names: frozenset[str]
    provider_payload: Mapping[str, Any]

    provider_id: str
    provider_config_version: str

    limitations: tuple[str, ...]
    trace: PolicyDecisionTrace

Then revise the translation checks in VoicePolicyRuntime.resolve():

requested_names = set(resolved.controls)
applied_names = set(translated.applied_control_names)
unsupported_names = set(translated.unsupported_controls)

if applied_names & unsupported_names:
    raise RuntimeError(
        "A control cannot be both applied and unsupported."
    )

if applied_names | unsupported_names != requested_names:
    raise RuntimeError(
        "Provider translation has incomplete control coverage."
    )

if context.required_controls - applied_names:
    raise RuntimeError(
        "Provider cannot apply all required controls."
    )

if not translated.provider_payload:
    raise RuntimeError(
        "Provider translation returned no usable request payload."
    )

The provider adapter must validate that payload against the real provider schema and configuration, then return only the abstract controls it actually represented. The TTS adapter should submit provider_payload; the audit trace should retain applied_control_names and the limitations.

This avoids incorrectly assuming that an abstract control such as speaking_rate must have the same field name in every provider's API. It also prevents unsupported behavior from being silently reported as successful.

17. Harden schema validation
In validator.py, insert this return immediately after the schema validation try/except, before calling any other gates. Malformed policies must not reach identity, regression or provider validators that assume a valid schema.

if not checks["schema"]:
    return PolicyValidationReport(
        policy_id=policy.id,
        valid=False,
        check_results=checks,
        violations=tuple(violations),
        evidence_refs=(),
    )

Also tighten the usable_findings filtering in synthesis.py, so supported status alone is not enough:

usable_findings = tuple(
    item
    for item in findings
    if (
        item.status in {
            EvidenceStatus.SUPPORTED,
            EvidenceStatus.EXPERIMENTALLY_VERIFIED,
        }
        and item.finding_id.strip()
        and item.claim.strip()
        and item.source_version.strip()
        and item.evidence_refs
    )
)

The evidence verifier must still check those references against the real knowledge store. This filter only rejects obviously incomplete records; it does not prove that a finding is correct.

19. Connect Phase 35 to the existing Aariya architecture
The end-to-end path should now look like this:

Phase 31–32: Knowledge and causal graph

Grounded findings, evidence status, contradictions and applicability

Phase 33–34: Experiment selection and evaluation

Real outcomes, measured improvements and recorded failures

Phase 35: Policy synthesis

Objectives → controls → constraints → evidence-backed candidate

Independent validation and versioned registry

Identity · Regression · Provider compatibility · Approval

Runtime policy

Resolve current context, apply registered rules and translate to supported controls

Actual TTS → actual audio measurements → learning loop

This is consistent with the complete policy-evolution loop in your source notes: validated findings feed policy synthesis; the result is validated and versioned before controlled deployment, and real interactions supply new evidence. 
*voice TTS.txt

20. Phase 35 completion criteria
Do not consider this phase production-ready until the following are demonstrated with the actual application.

Policy synthesis uses real strategy, context, provider-capability and knowledge records.
Unverified or contradictory evidence cannot silently justify a policy change.
Objective priorities come from the active strategy and validated configuration, not fixed emotion presets.
Identity, safety and provider constraints remain enforced after context rules are applied.
Unsupported provider controls are reported honestly; actual provider request payloads are validated.
Every candidate has evidence references, an explanation and an immutable version history.
Validation failures, missing telemetry, stale baselines and missing approval block activation.
The active policy remains unchanged when a candidate is merely created or validated.
Real TTS output is measured and linked to the policy version that generated it.

Phase 36 — Autonomous Context-to-Performance Mapping
Phase 35 gave Aariya a coherent voice policy. Phase 36 makes that policy continuous across a conversation, so each response reflects the current context, the previous delivery, conversational momentum, the user's rhythm and the actual sound produced by the TTS engine. 
*voice TTS.txt

The key difference is:

Phase 35: What delivery is appropriate for this response?

Phase 36: How should delivery evolve from the previous moment into this response, and what should happen if the conversation changes unexpectedly?

We will add backend/app/voice/trajectory/ above the existing prosody, intonation, timing, breath, emphasis, streaming and synchronization systems. It should coordinate those systems rather than replace them. 
*voice TTS.txt

1. Architecture
Live conversation and Aariya state

Context · Intent · Linguistics · User rhythm · Identity · Performance history

Context-to-performance mapper

Resolve the target using the active Phase 35 policy

Momentum and transition planner

Preserve continuity without getting stuck in one expressive state

Trajectory validation

Identity limits · Provider capabilities · Timing constraints

Performance plan → TTS → measured audio → state update

Two safety rules govern this phase. First, predicted context can prepare a future performance, but cannot authorize speech by itself. Second, if the user interrupts Aariya, the current generation must be cancelled, the conversational context preserved, and the next performance recalculated from the new situation. 
*voice TTS.txt

2. Create the trajectory package
Add these modules:

backend/app/voice/trajectory/
├── __init__.py
├── state.py
├── target.py
├── transition.py
├── trajectory.py
├── momentum.py
├── context_window.py
├── anticipation.py
├── interruption.py
├── continuity.py
├── mapper.py
├── validator.py
└── controller.py
The runtime will hold the current performance, previous performance, recent performance history, active transition and any pending prediction. Persist meaningful lifecycle changes through your existing persistence layer; don't write every audio frame to PostgreSQL. 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Define continuous performance state
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/state.py

The source defines continuous performance dimensions such as energy, warmth, expressiveness, restraint, confidence, pitch activity, rhythm activity, pause activity and emphasis activity. It deliberately avoids fixed happy, sad or angry voice presets. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime, timezone
import math


PERFORMANCE_DIMENSIONS = (
    "energy",
    "warmth",
    "expressiveness",
    "restraint",
    "confidence",
    "pitch_activity",
    "rhythm_activity",
    "pause_activity",
    "emphasis_activity",
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceVector:
    energy: float
    warmth: float
    expressiveness: float
    restraint: float
    confidence: float

    pitch_activity: float
    rhythm_activity: float
    pause_activity: float
    emphasis_activity: float

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in PERFORMANCE_DIMENSIONS:
            value = getattr(self, name)

The dimensions are normalized internal planning variables, not direct instructions to a particular TTS provider. Your provider adapter is still responsible for mapping them to actual supported controls.

4. Define a performance target and transition
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/target.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping

from .state import (
    PERFORMANCE_DIMENSIONS,
    PerformanceVector,

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceTarget(PerformanceVector):
    rationale_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    policy_id: str = ""
    policy_version: str = ""
    context_revision: str = ""

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        super().__post_init__()

        if not self.policy_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Target must identify its policy.")

        if not self.policy_version.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Target must identify its policy version."
            )

        if not self.context_revision.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Target must identify the context revision used."
            )

        if not self.rationale_refs:
            raise ValueError(

File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/transition.py

A transition represents how the previous performance moves to the new target. Duration and permitted change rates must come from validated configuration and real conversational signals, not a universal hardcoded timing value.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math

from .state import PerformanceVector
from .target import PerformanceTarget


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TransitionSignals:
    urgency: float
    user_emotional_shift: float
    turn_boundary: bool
    currently_speaking: bool
    planned_speech_duration_ms: int | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in ("urgency", "user_emotional_shift"):
            value = getattr(self, name)
            if not math.isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{name} must be within [0, 1]."
                )

        if (
            self.planned_speech_duration_ms is not None
            and self.planned_speech_duration_ms <= 0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Planned speech duration must be positive."
            )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TransitionTimingPolicy:

user_emotional_shift and planned_speech_duration_ms are preserved as context signals for policy and transition logic. Any more advanced duration model should be based on validated rules or measured behavior. The planner must not invent a transition duration just because a conversation appears urgent.

5. Track conversational momentum without getting stuck
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/momentum.py

Your notes identify two opposite problems: resetting Aariya to neutral after every sentence, and preserving an expressive state for too long after the conversation changes. Momentum should decay based on the configured policy and update using current observed signals. 
*voice TTS.txt

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MomentumPolicy:
    half_life_seconds: float
    context_weight: float
    version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if (
            not math.isfinite(self.half_life_seconds)

half_life_seconds and context_weight must come from your configured, validated strategy—not values that are claimed to have been learned when no supporting data exists. When a meaningful new context arrives, its signals can shift the momentum quickly; the previous state should not dictate the answer.

7. Map context and policy into a target
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/mapper.py

The mapper delegates the context-sensitive decision to a provider connected to the Phase 35 policy runtime. This keeps the trajectory subsystem independent of a particular model and avoids inventing fixed emotion-to-pitch mappings.

        self,

8. Represent the full trajectory
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/trajectory.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from .target import PerformanceTarget
from .transition import Transition


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceTrajectory:
    conversation_id: str
    turn_id: str
    context_revision: str

    previous_target: PerformanceTarget | None
    target: PerformanceTarget
    transition: Transition | None

    source_evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]
    trajectory_policy_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.conversation_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("conversation_id is required.")

        if not self.turn_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("turn_id is required.")

        if not self.context_revision.strip():
            raise ValueError("context_revision is required.")

        if not self.source_evidence_refs:
            raise ValueError(
                "Trajectory requires traceable evidence."
            )


A trajectory includes the starting point, target, transition plan and source references. The runtime may sample the transition using its playback clock; it should not infer that the TTS provider has achieved the requested acoustic behavior without measuring the generated audio.

9. Transition continuity
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/continuity.py

from __future__ import annotations

from .context_window import PerformanceContextWindow
from .target import PerformanceTarget
from .transition import Transition, TransitionPlanner


class PerformanceContinuity:
    def __init__(
        self,
        planner: TransitionPlanner,
    ) -> None:
        self._planner = planner

    async def transition(
        self,
        previous,
        target: PerformanceTarget,
        context: PerformanceContextWindow,
    ) -> Transition:
        return self._planner.plan(
            previous=previous,
            target=target,
            signals=context.transition_signals,
        )

The continuity layer uses the actual conversational signals and configured timing policy. An urgent contextual change can authorize a faster transition only within the allowed rate limits. A large change that cannot fit safely inside the permitted duration must be rejected or handled with a validated intermediate trajectory.

10. Anticipate the next performance without speaking prematurely
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/anticipation.py

The prediction subsystem can prepare a likely future target in advance. It must keep that prediction separate from a committed decision, and neither prediction nor confirmation automatically invokes TTS. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Protocol
import math

from .target import PerformanceTarget


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictedPerformance:
    prediction_id: str
    source_context_revision: str
    expected_context_signature: str

    target: PerformanceTarget
    confidence: float
    model_version: str
    basis_refs: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AnticipationPolicy:
    minimum_confidence: float
    version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if (
            not math.isfinite(self.minimum_confidence)
            or not 0.0 <= self.minimum_confidence <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "minimum_confidence must be within [0, 1]."
            )


expected_context_signature must be constructed by a versioned context-signing component, not by a generative model making an arbitrary string. The evidence verifier remains a real integration dependency.

The resulting behavior is:

Predict → Wait for new observations → Validate → Commit target or discard

A confirmed target must still pass normal trajectory validation, identity constraints and the usual speech-generation flow.

12. Validate the complete trajectory
File: backend/app/voice/trajectory/validator.py

The target may be within the approved limits while the transition violates them. Validation therefore checks both ends of the transition, all required performance dimensions and the actual capabilities available for the current provider. Your notes explicitly require identity and provider-capability validation before a trajectory can be executed. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math
from typing import Mapping

from .state import PERFORMANCE_DIMENSIONS
from .trajectory import PerformanceTrajectory


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TrajectoryValidationReport:
    valid: bool
    violations: tuple[str, ...]
    checked_policy_version: str


class TrajectoryValidator:
    def validate(
        self,
        trajectory: PerformanceTrajectory,
        identity_constraints: Mapping[str, tuple[float, float]],
        provider_capabilities: frozenset[str],
        required_dimensions: frozenset[str],
    ) -> TrajectoryValidationReport:
        violations: list[str] = []

        missing_bounds = (
            set(PERFORMANCE_DIMENSIONS)
            - set(identity_constraints)
        )
        if missing_bounds:
            violations.append(
                "Missing identity bounds for: "
                + ", ".join(sorted(missing_bounds))
            )

The bounds must be fetched from Aariya's trusted, versioned identity and safety configuration. They must not be generated by the candidate trajectory itself. The provider_capabilities set must likewise come from actual provider metadata or validated configuration.

If the report fails, the trajectory must not be forwarded to the delivery planner.

13. Main trajectory controller
Before adding the controller, make this small type correction in trajectory.py:

# Replace:
previous_target: PerformanceTarget | None

# With:
previous_target: PerformanceVector | None

Also import PerformanceVector from state.py. The previous state comes from the actual runtime and therefore need not be a PerformanceTarget.

The controller intentionally records the plan only after validation. Once real audio is available, the second method links the intended target to the actual measured outcome. The measurement adapter must populate the PerformanceState from real output analysis using the same calibrated definitions as the target dimensions.

The resulting errors identify where the system's requested delivery differed from the measured delivery. They are observations for subsequent mapping and learning, not permission to change the active policy automatically.

14. Apply the continuity safeguards
One detail in transition.py needs to account for both urgency and a meaningful conversational shift. Inside TransitionPlanner.plan(), replace the line that uses only signals.urgency with:

transition_pressure = max(
    signals.urgency,
    signals.user_emotional_shift,
)

permitted_rate = regular + (
    urgent - regular
) * transition_pressure

Both signals must come from the actual context/turn-taking subsystems. The configured urgent slew rates remain the upper boundary, so a sudden contextual change cannot bypass the identity or timing constraints.

The existing plan then calculates the minimum duration required for those rates, applies the configured duration factors, and rejects a transition that cannot fit within the approved maximum. This is safer than shortening a transition until it appears responsive while quietly violating the change-rate limit.

Anticipation and interruption remain separate modules because they have different responsibilities and lifecycles. They should be wired to the existing conversation and generation managers rather than imported into the core trajectory data types.

16. Connect Phase 36 to the live voice engine
The intended execution path is:

REAL CONVERSATION
       ↓
Build current context window
       ↓
Read active Phase 35 voice policy
       ↓
Map context into a performance target
       ↓
Plan transition from previous measured state
       ↓
Validate identity, timing and provider capabilities
       ↓
Compile the delivery plan
       ↓
Generate and stream actual TTS audio
       ↓
Measure actual performance
       ↓
Compare actual delivery with intended target
       ↓
Persist completed turn and evidence
       ↓
Update the context for the next turn
An integration adapter should connect your existing services along these lines:

async def execute_voice_turn(
    *,
    conversation_id: str,
    turn_id: str,
    context_service,
    policy_registry,
    trajectory_controller,
    delivery_planner,
    tts_engine,
    audio_measurement_service,
    performance_measurement_adapter,
):
    # Build from actual conversation and runtime state.
    context_window = await context_service.build_window(
        conversation_id=conversation_id,
        turn_id=turn_id,
    )

    # Use the validated active Phase 35 policy.
    active_policy = await policy_registry.get_active()

    # Identity bounds and capabilities must be sourced from trusted,
    # current configuration for the selected voice/provider.
    runtime_constraints = (
        await context_service.get_runtime_constraints(
            conversation_id=conversation_id,
        )
    )

    trajectory = await trajectory_controller.create(
        context_window=context_window,
        policy=active_policy,
        identity_constraints=(
            runtime_constraints.identity_bounds
        ),
        provider_capabilities=(
            runtime_constraints.provider_capabilities
        ),
        required_dimensions=(
            runtime_constraints.required_dimensions
        ),
        trajectory_policy_version=(
            runtime_constraints.trajectory_policy_version
        ),
    )

    # The existing delivery planner combines the trajectory with
    # Phase 35's validated provider-control translation.
    delivery_plan = await delivery_planner.compile(
        trajectory=trajectory,
        policy=active_policy,
        context_window=context_window,
    )

    # Actual TTS implementation; this adapter must support cancellation
    # and the existing stream/session lifecycle.
    generation = await tts_engine.generate(
        delivery_plan=delivery_plan,
        conversation_id=conversation_id,
        turn_id=turn_id,
    )

    # Measure generated audio, rather than assuming the requested
    # controls produced the intended sound.
    measurement = await audio_measurement_service.measure(
        generation_id=generation.generation_id,
    )

    actual_state = (
        await performance_measurement_adapter.to_state(
            measurement=measurement,
            conversation_id=conversation_id,
            turn_id=turn_id,
            policy_id=active_policy.id,
            generation_id=generation.generation_id,
        )
    )

    # Commit actual measured performance at the completed-turn boundary.
    await trajectory_controller.commit_measured_outcome(
        trajectory=trajectory,
        actual_state=actual_state,
        actual_audio_evidence_refs=measurement.evidence_refs,
        measurement_version=measurement.pipeline_version,
    )

    return generation

This is an integration template, not plug-and-play code: the service methods and measurement fields must be adapted to your existing interfaces. Your streaming engine must also invoke the interruption controller when barge-in occurs, rather than waiting for the entire generation to finish.

Notice that commit_measured_outcome() records the actual measured state. It does not automatically rewrite the policy or alter all subsequent performance targets. Those changes still belong to the validated learning and policy-evolution path.

17. Phase 36 completion checklist
Your source gives Phase 36 a clear completion standard: continuous state, smooth transitions, contextual changes, interruption handling, prediction/commit separation, actual-audio feedback, persistence and integration with existing TTS systems. 
*voice TTS.txt

Previous performance influences the next target without dominating new context.
Meaningful context changes can cause faster—but still validated—transitions.
Momentum decays and updates using real state history rather than staying locked into one expression.
Predictions are validated against new evidence before their targets are considered for use.
Interruptions release the floor, cancel stale generation and invalidate pending predictions.
Identity bounds and required provider capabilities are checked before the delivery plan is used.
Actual acoustic measurements are linked to their generation, policy and turn.
Only meaningful lifecycle changes and completed-turn observations are persisted—not every audio frame.
The system remains operational when prediction is unavailable, using the existing validated policy fallback.
Phase 37 — Autonomous Prosodic Composition
Phase 36 established Aariya's performance trajectory. Phase 37 converts that trajectory and the meaning of the current utterance into coordinated vocal gestures. These gestures describe how the speech should be expressed without prematurely assigning provider-specific pitch, speed or volume values. Phase 38 will ground those abstract intentions in measurements of Aariya's actual TTS voice. 
*voice TTS.txt

For example, the sentence “I said tomorrow, not today” requires more than a general expressive setting. The linguistic system identifies the contrast, and the composition layer coordinates prominence, pitch movement, slight lengthening and phrase closure around that contrast. The specific controls are determined later by the real-voice grounding layer. 
*voice TTS.txt

1. Architecture and files
Create backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/:

prosodic_composition/
├── __init__.py
├── utterance.py
├── semantic_units.py
├── discourse_units.py
├── communicative_intent.py
├── emotional_intent.py
├── gesture.py
├── gesture_types.py
├── composition.py
├── interaction.py
├── conflict.py
├── timing.py
├── contour.py
├── articulation.py
├── breath.py
├── validator.py
└── controller.py
The composition pipeline is:

Utterance + actual linguistic analysis

Semantic units · Discourse structure · Communicative intent · Emotional intent

Vocal gesture composition

Prominence · Contrast · Pitch movement · Onset · Lengthening · Pause · Closure

Conflict resolution and coordination

Meaning, intent, trajectory, identity constraints and timing must agree

Timing and rhythm

Pitch contour

Articulation

Breath planning

Validated composition → Phase 2 delivery planner → actual TTS

The prosodic subsystem should use the existing linguistic layer to provide meaning and discourse analysis. It should not independently guess all the linguistic structure from raw text, and it should not generate randomness and call that naturalness. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

2. Define the utterance and semantic structure
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/semantic_units.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SemanticUnit:
    text: str
    information_role: str | None
    importance: float | None
    contrastive: bool
    uncertain: bool
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.text.strip():
            raise ValueError("Semantic-unit text is required.")

        if self.importance is not None:
            if (
                not math.isfinite(self.importance)
                or not 0.0 <= self.importance <= 1.0
            ):
                raise ValueError(
                    "Importance must be within [0, 1]."
                )

        if self.information_role is not None:
            if not self.information_role.strip():
                raise ValueError(
                    "Information role cannot be blank."
                )

File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/discourse_units.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DiscourseUnit:
    start_unit: int
    end_unit: int
    relation: str
    confidence: float | None
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.start_unit < 0 or self.end_unit < self.start_unit:
            raise ValueError("Invalid discourse-unit range.")

        if not self.relation.strip():
            raise ValueError("Discourse relation is required.")

        if self.confidence is not None:
            if (
                not math.isfinite(self.confidence)
                or not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0
            ):
                raise ValueError(
                    "Discourse confidence must be within [0, 1]."
                )

The semantic roles and discourse relations must originate from the actual linguistic analysis pipeline. Common roles include new or given information, contrast, correction, qualification, conclusion, condition, cause and result. The examples describe the schema, not a claim that Aariya's parser has already detected those roles. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/communicative_intent.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CommunicativeIntent:
    primary: str
    secondary: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    urgency: float = 0.0
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.primary.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Primary communicative intent is required."
            )

        if (
            not math.isfinite(self.urgency)
            or not 0.0 <= self.urgency <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Urgency must be within [0, 1]."
            )

File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/emotional_intent.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EmotionalIntent:
    warmth: float
    energy: float
    concern: float
    playfulness: float
    expressiveness: float
    restraint: float

    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "warmth",
            "energy",
            "concern",
            "playfulness",
            "expressiveness",
            "restraint",
        ):
            value = getattr(self, name)

            if (
                not math.isfinite(value)
                or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0
            ):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{name} must be within [0, 1]."
                )

Communicative intent and emotional intent are intentionally separate. “Reassure and clarify” and “challenge and correct” can require different vocal compositions even when the overall emotional state is similar. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/utterance.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from .communicative_intent import CommunicativeIntent
from .discourse_units import DiscourseUnit
from .emotional_intent import EmotionalIntent
from .semantic_units import SemanticUnit


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceUtterance:
    text: str
    semantic_structure: tuple[SemanticUnit, ...]
    discourse_structure: tuple[DiscourseUnit, ...]

    communicative_intent: CommunicativeIntent
    emotional_intent: EmotionalIntent

    source_revision: str
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.text.strip():
            raise ValueError("Utterance text cannot be empty.")

        if not self.semantic_structure:
            raise ValueError(
                "Actual semantic analysis is required."
            )

        if not self.source_revision.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Linguistic source revision is required."
            )


This object is the composition layer's input contract. Connect it to Aariya's existing clause parser, focus/contrast analysis, question analysis and discourse-relation services; don't populate it with guessed semantic labels.

Aariya should reason about gestures such as prominence, contrast, pitch rise, lengthening, pause, soft onset and phrase closure rather than independently selecting eight acoustic parameters. This is the central abstraction introduced by Phase 37.

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Mapping
import math

from .gesture_types import GestureType


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VocalGesture:
    gesture_type: GestureType

    # Inclusive semantic-unit range in the current utterance.
    start_unit: int
    end_unit: int

    # Abstract semantic strength, not a TTS pitch or volume value.
    intensity: float

    # Optional duration intent. Actual timing is resolved separately.
    duration_ms: int | None = None

    parameters: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    rationale: str = ""

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.start_unit < 0 or self.end_unit < self.start_unit:
            raise ValueError("Invalid gesture scope.")

        if (
            not math.isfinite(self.intensity)
            or not 0.0 <= self.intensity <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(

parameters may carry abstract intent metadata, but must not secretly contain provider-specific fields. Phase 38 is where abstract gestures are mapped to calibrated controls for the selected voice.

5. Compose gestures from real linguistic and conversational analysis
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/composition.py

The composer brings together three distinct sources of gestures: information structure, communicative intent and expressive behavior. Their implementations must use the actual outputs of Aariya's linguistic and context systems.

Returning actual gesture data from those services is essential; this code does not implement a new linguistic model or claim that one has already learned how to speak naturally.

6. Resolve incompatible gestures intelligently
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/conflict.py

Some gestures complement one another; others compete. Prominence and softness can combine to produce gentle but clear emphasis. A strong onset and a soft onset targeting the same unit, however, are incompatible. Your notes require a dedicated conflict layer rather than a “last rule wins” strategy. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

        self,

        context: CompositionContext,

        """
        Resolve conflicts using intent, semantic importance, emotion,
        trajectory, identity requirements and timing constraints.

        Do not implement this as an unconditional last-rule-wins merge.
        """
        ...

The conflict-resolution provider is an integration boundary. It must consider the actual utterance and policy context, preserve the rationale for its decision, and never resolve conflicts by silently discarding identity or timing constraints.

7. Coordinate timing and rhythm
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/timing.py

Pauses, phrase boundaries, emphasis, breath opportunities and turn-taking should be coordinated by one timing plan rather than independently assigning durations that may conflict. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Protocol

from .composition import GestureComposition
from .interaction import CompositionContext
from .semantic_units import SemanticUnit


class TimingEventType(str, Enum):
    PAUSE_BEFORE = "pause_before"
    PAUSE_AFTER = "pause_after"
    LENGTHEN = "lengthen"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TimingEvent:
    event_type: TimingEventType
    unit_index: int
    duration_ms: int
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.unit_index < 0:
            raise ValueError("Timing unit index cannot be negative.")

        if self.duration_ms <= 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "Timing duration must be positive."
            )

        if not self.evidence_refs:
            raise ValueError(
                "Timing event needs traceable evidence."

The TimingPlanProvider must derive actual durations from the validated timing configuration and linguistic context. This module deliberately contains no fabricated pause-duration formula.

10. Coordinate breath opportunities
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/breath.py

Breath planning should emerge from phrase structure, utterance length, emotional effort, timing and actual provider capability. It should not add a breath after an arbitrary number of sentences.

            raise ValueError(
                "Breath intensity must be within [0, 1]."

These are abstract breath opportunities, not a command to synthesize or insert a literal inhalation. Phase 38 and the actual TTS/voice implementation need to establish which effects the selected voice can reproduce.

11. Validate the complete composition
File: backend/app/voice/prosodic_composition/validator.py

Individual components are not enough: Aariya needs a final check across the assembled gestures, timing plan, contour, articulation and breath plan before handing them to the delivery planner.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from .articulation import ArticulationPlan
from .breath import BreathPlan
from .composition import GestureComposition
from .contour import PitchContourPlan
from .interaction import CompositionContext
from .timing import TimingPlan
from .utterance import VoiceUtterance


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CompositionValidationReport:
    valid: bool
    violations: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]


class ProsodicCompositionValidator:
    def validate(
        self,
        *,
        utterance: VoiceUtterance,
        context: CompositionContext,
        gestures: GestureComposition,
        timing: TimingPlan,
        contour: PitchContourPlan,
        articulation: ArticulationPlan,
        breath: BreathPlan,
    ) -> CompositionValidationReport:
        violations: list[str] = []

        if (
            gestures.conversation_id != context.conversation_id

The final validator enforces scope, policy version and evidence consistency. It is not a substitute for the actual identity and voice-grounding checks: those must be enforced downstream before provider-specific controls are executed.

Important evidence check for the conflict resolver
Before integrating the code, tighten conflict.py so the resolver cannot authorize its own unsupported evidence. Replace the existing trusted_refs initialization with:

trusted_refs = (
    set(composition.evidence_refs)
    | set(context.evidence_refs)
)

if not set(result.evidence_refs).issubset(trusted_refs):
    raise ValueError(
        "Conflict resolution introduced evidence that was not "
        "present in the original composition or context."
    )

Then keep the subsequent check that each resolved gesture's evidence references are a subset of trusted_refs. This ensures the conflict provider can decide how to resolve a conflict, but cannot create new evidence references merely to justify its decision.

14. Connect Phase 37 to the existing voice engine
The new composition layer belongs between Phase 36's trajectory engine and your existing delivery planner. It should not bypass the delivery planner, streaming manager, TTS adapter or audio-quality analysis.

async def prepare_voice_delivery(
    *,
    utterance,
    performance_target,
    composition_context,
    prosodic_composition_controller,
    existing_delivery_planner,
):
    composition = (
        await prosodic_composition_controller.compose(
            utterance=utterance,
            performance_target=performance_target,
            context=composition_context,
        )
    )

    # Convert the validated abstract composition into the existing
    # delivery-planner input contract.
    #
    # This adapter must preserve timing, contour, articulation,
    # breath, evidence, policy version and context revision.
    delivery_plan = await existing_delivery_planner.from_composition(
        composition=composition,
        utterance=utterance,
        performance_target=performance_target,
    )

    return delivery_plan

existing_delivery_planner.from_composition() is an integration contract, not an existing method I have verified in your codebase. Implement it against your actual Phase 2 delivery planner before wiring this function into production.

Your source describes the resulting hierarchy as Phase 36's performance trajectory → Phase 37's vocal gestures and coordinated timing/pitch/articulation/breath → the delivery planner → the real TTS engine. 
*voice TTS.txt

15. Validate the behavior before live integration
The minimum test set should verify:

Scenario

Expected behavior

Contrast or correction is identified by the real linguistic layer

Composition can coordinate prominence and appropriate phrase boundaries

An onset has conflicting soft and strong gestures

Conflict resolution must resolve the incompatibility or reject the composition

A gesture targets a missing semantic unit

Reject the composition

A required evidence reference cannot be verified

Reject the composition

The policy or context revision is stale

Reject the composition

Timing, contour, articulation or breath composition fails

Do not return a delivery-ready result

The provider cannot implement a proposed gesture

Phase 38 handles grounding or a supported alternative; never claim the unsupported behavior succeeded

There is no evidence for a breath or expressive variation

Do not invent one

The sentence is a question, continuation or completed statement

Use actual linguistic/discourse intent to derive its final boundary, not punctuation alone

The last case matters because your notes specifically distinguish question-appropriate boundaries, continuation gestures, closure gestures and context-dependent endings. 
*voice TTS.txt

16. Phase 37 completion criteria
Do not regard Phase 37 as production-ready until these conditions are met:

Real linguistic and discourse analysis supplies the semantic structure, focus, contrast, uncertainty and intent.

Gesture proposals are evidence-backed and remain separate from provider-specific acoustic controls.

Conflict resolution coordinates expressive behavior rather than applying arbitrary fixed mappings.

Timing, pitch contour, articulation and breath plans are mutually consistent and validated.

The complete composition flows through the existing delivery planner and actual TTS adapter.

Actual audio is measured and compared with the intended gestures, feeding Phase 38's calibration system.

No unsupported gesture is reported as executed, and no random expressive behavior is introduced as a substitute for naturalness. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

Implementation status: The Phase 37 data models, orchestration and validation contracts are now provided inline. The actual gesture-proposal providers, conflict-resolution policy, timing/contour/articulation/breath engines, final delivery-plan adapter and integration tests still need to be implemented and verified against Aariya's codebase. No real TTS output has been generated or measured by this scaffold.


Phase 38 — Prosodic Grounding & Real-Voice Calibration
Phase 37 taught Aariya to compose abstract vocal gestures. Phase 38 establishes how those gestures translate into the actual acoustic behavior of Aariya's selected TTS voice. It measures what the provider actually produces, builds a calibration mapping from real observations, tracks confidence, and detects when the mapping becomes unreliable. 
*voice TTS.txt

The distinction is important: a request for a “gentle pitch rise” does not prove that the generated audio contains that rise. Calibration connects the intended gesture to the actual provider response and measured audio. This is a grounding layer, not another emotion system. 
*voice TTS.txt

1. Architecture
Phase 37: Abstract vocal gesture

Meaning · Gesture type · Semantic intensity · Desired effect

Calibration lookup

Provider · Voice reference · Configuration version · Gesture

Grounded provider request

Supported controls derived from validated real calibration data

Actual TTS → real audio → Phase 8 measurement

Calibration candidate → validation → versioned activation

Confidence · Reproducibility · Identity consistency · Drift checks

Your notes specify that calibration must be tied to the real provider, voice, voice configuration and relevant version information. If those change, existing calibration may no longer be valid. They also require interpolation to stay inside empirically observed bounds, and missing calibration must not be replaced by an invented mapping. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Represent the abstract grounding target
File: backend/app/voice/grounding/target.py

A grounding target describes the desired communicative effect, without embedding a provider's specific pitch, rate or emphasis API parameters. Your source gives contrastive emphasis as one example of an abstract target. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Mapping
import math
from uuid import uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GroundingTarget:
    gesture: str
    semantic_intensity: float
    desired_effect: Mapping[str, Any]

    target_id: str = field(
        default_factory=lambda: str(uuid4())
    )
    source_evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.gesture.strip():
            raise ValueError("Gesture cannot be empty.")

        if (
            not math.isfinite(self.semantic_intensity)
            or not 0.0 <= self.semantic_intensity <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Semantic intensity must be within [0, 1]."
            )

        if not self.target_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Target ID is required.")

        if not self.desired_effect:
            raise ValueError(

The desired effect might describe prominence, clarity and contrast. It does not yet prescribe how many hertz of pitch movement or how much duration to add.

4. Define actual acoustic measurements
File: backend/app/voice/grounding/feature.py

The feature schema should represent measurements extracted from real audio, including mean fundamental frequency (F0), F0 range and slope, speech rate, pause duration, energy, voiced ratio, spectral measures and duration. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AcousticFeature:
    name: str
    value: float
    unit: str
    extraction_method: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Feature name is required.")

        if not self.unit.strip():
            raise ValueError("Feature unit is required.")

        if not self.extraction_method.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Measurement method/version is required."
            )

        if not math.isfinite(self.value):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Measured value for {self.name} is not finite."
            )

For example, an F0 measurement might be recorded in hertz, while a speech-rate measurement might be recorded in words per minute. The measurement adapter must supply the units and extraction-method version from the real analysis pipeline.

5. Identify the exact provider configuration
File: backend/app/voice/grounding/mapping.py

A calibration learned for one provider and configuration cannot automatically be reused for another. Your source requires the mapping to remain tied to the provider, voice, configuration version and any other relevant runtime identifiers. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderConfigurationIdentity:
    provider: str
    voice_reference: str
    configuration_version: str

    provider_version: str | None
    audio_format: str | None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "provider",
            "voice_reference",
            "configuration_version",
        ):
            if not getattr(self, name).strip():
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Provider identity requires {name}."
                )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationMapping:
    mapping_id: str
    identity: ProviderConfigurationIdentity

    gesture: str
    input_features: tuple[str, ...]
    output_features: tuple[str, ...]

    # Reference to the actual validated calibration/model artifact.

model_reference is an identifier for a real model or calibration artifact, not a model path that the application assumes exists. The actual artifact must be created from real data, validated and stored by the calibration pipeline.

6. Store an observation of the actual voice

The observation also retains the exact configuration identity and provider-request reference, making it possible to trace which real request produced each measurement.

7. Reuse the existing audio-measurement system
File: backend/app/voice/grounding/measurement.py

Connect this interface to the audio-quality analysis infrastructure you already defined in Phase 8. Do not build an independent acoustic analyzer that can produce conflicting measurements.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .feature import AcousticFeature


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeasurementResult:
    features: tuple[AcousticFeature, ...]
    pipeline_version: str
    quality_report_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.features:
            raise ValueError(
                "Measurement produced no acoustic features."
            )

        if not self.pipeline_version.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Measurement pipeline version is required."
            )

        if not self.quality_report_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Measurement needs an auditable quality report."
            )


class AudioMeasurementEngine(Protocol):
    async def measure(
        self,
        audio_reference: str,
    ) -> MeasurementResult:

An empty or failed measurement is not a zero-valued result. Treat it as missing evidence and prevent it from contributing to a calibration mapping.

        self,

        gesture: str,

The interface separates saving a mapping candidate from activating a mapping. A calibration experiment must never replace an active mapping simply because it generated new data.

For the production database, make activation transactional and retain the prior mapping version. If provider configuration changes, the old mapping can remain in history but must not be silently used for the new configuration.

9. Calculate calibration confidence from evidence
Confidence must reflect actual observation count, consistency, measurement quality, reproducibility, provider/configuration stability, model fit and outlier behavior. Your source explicitly identifies those factors. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/grounding/confidence.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
import math
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationAssessment:
    observation_count: int
    reproducibility: float
    stability: float
    measurement_quality: float
    model_fit: float
    outlier_rate: float

    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]
    assessment_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.observation_count < 1:
            raise ValueError(
                "Calibration assessment requires real observations."
            )

        for name in (
            "reproducibility",
            "stability",
            "measurement_quality",
            "model_fit",
            "outlier_rate",
        ):
            value = getattr(self, name)
            if not math.isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{name} must be within [0, 1]."

This service delegates the assessment to an injected evaluator; it deliberately does not invent a numeric confidence formula and claim it is learned. The evaluator must calculate the assessment from the real dataset and document its method. A promotion gate can then use a separately validated policy to decide whether the confidence is sufficient.

Add these imports at the top of confidence.py:

from .mapping import CalibrationMapping
from .observation import VoiceObservation

Those imports are needed for the type annotations in the confidence-evaluation interface.

10. Interpolate only inside the real calibration range
Your notes allow interpolation for targets that lie between calibrated levels, but prohibit extrapolation beyond the evidence. 
*voice TTS.txt

The interpolation layer needs the actual request controls associated with each measurement. Add the following representation to interpolation.py.

File: backend/app/voice/grounding/interpolation.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol
import math

from .mapping import ProviderConfigurationIdentity
from .observation import VoiceObservation
from .target import GroundingTarget


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationSample:
    target: GroundingTarget
    observation: VoiceObservation

    # Obtained from the actual persisted provider request, not guessed.
    provider_control_values: Mapping[str, float]

    # Optional outcome evaluation from the actual audio/quality system.
    desired_effect_evaluation_ref: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.desired_effect_evaluation_ref.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Calibration sample requires a real outcome evaluation."
            )

        if (
            self.target.target_id != self.observation.target_id
            or not self.provider_control_values
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Calibration sample is incomplete."
            )


The final refs expression above must be replaced: the interpolator needs the observation's actual durable ID, which is VoiceObservation.id, not target_id. Use this corrected ending for the method instead:

        refs = tuple(dict.fromkeys((
            low.observation.id,
            high.observation.id,
            *low.target.source_evidence_refs,
            *high.target.source_evidence_refs,
        )))

        return GroundedProviderControl(
            identity=identity,
            gesture=target.gesture,
            target_intensity=target.semantic_intensity,
            control_values=resolved_values,
            lower_observation_ref=low.observation.id,
            upper_observation_ref=high.observation.id,
            evidence_refs=refs,
            calibration_model_version=calibration_model_version,
        )

This is a deliberately limited interpolator: it handles one-dimensional interpolation across semantic intensity for the same gesture and exact provider configuration. It cannot infer arbitrary multidimensional effects or prove that the desired effect was achieved. The dataset provider must return only samples with real audio measurements, actual provider-request values and recorded outcome evaluations. The resulting request still needs capability, bounds and identity validation before rendering.

For a clean, copyable implementation, use this complete replacement for interpolation.py instead of the draft above. It includes the corrected evidence-reference handling.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol
import math

from .mapping import ProviderConfigurationIdentity
from .observation import VoiceObservation
from .target import GroundingTarget


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationSample:
    target: GroundingTarget
    observation: VoiceObservation
    provider_control_values: Mapping[str, float]
    desired_effect_evaluation_ref: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.target.target_id != self.observation.target_id:
            raise ValueError(
                "Target and observation do not match."
            )

        if not self.desired_effect_evaluation_ref.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "A real outcome-evaluation reference is required."
            )

        if not self.provider_control_values:
            raise ValueError("Provider controls are required.")

        for name, value in self.provider_control_values.items():
            if (
                not name.strip()
                or not math.isfinite(value)

One important limit remains: the calibrated samples need to be selected using an actual validated evaluation of the intended effect. Interpolating provider parameters alone does not establish that the output is natural, clear or identity-consistent.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .mapping import CalibrationMapping
from .observation import VoiceObservation


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationValidationReport:
    valid: bool
    consistency_passed: bool
    bounds_passed: bool
    evidence_refs: tuple[str, ...]
    violations: tuple[str, ...]
    validation_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.validation_version.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Validation report must identify its version."
            )

        if self.valid and (
            not self.consistency_passed or not self.bounds_passed
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "A valid report cannot contain a failed required check."
            )

    async def check_consistency(
        self,
        mapping: CalibrationMapping,

The assessment engine is a required integration. Its tolerances, measurement-quality requirements, observation sufficiency and model-fit checks must come from the calibrated voice's validated release policy; they are not provided as invented values here.

A candidate with missing observations, a different provider identity or failed bounds checks cannot activate.

14. Detect calibration drift
If a provider change or runtime update causes a gesture to produce different audio, the old mapping may become unreliable. The documented flow is: compare planned with actual behavior, detect drift, invalidate or lower confidence, and then recalibrate. 
*voice TTS.txt

File: backend/app/voice/grounding/drift.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .mapping import CalibrationMapping
from .observation import VoiceObservation


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationDriftAssessment:
    drift_detected: bool
    reason: str
    observation_refs: tuple[str, ...]
    assessment_reference: str
    assessment_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.reason.strip():
            raise ValueError("Drift assessment needs a reason.")

        if not self.assessment_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Drift assessment must reference its persisted record."
            )

        if not self.assessment_version.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Drift assessment version is required."
            )

        if self.drift_detected and not self.observation_refs:
            raise ValueError(
                "Confirmed drift must reference actual observations."
            )


The drift engine must compute its decision using real measurements and an approved policy. For example, an established mapping for a moderate pitch rise might become unreliable if recent output repeatedly shows much less pitch movement. The controller should invalidate that mapping only when the validated drift assessment reports the problem—not merely because one sample differs. 
*voice TTS.txt

Mapping training itself is deliberately an interface: the actual fitting algorithm must operate on persisted calibration samples, with the real provider configuration and measured feature definitions.
