These exports make the subsystem interfaces available to the surrounding application. They do not instantiate providers, load secrets or connect the subsystem to a database automatically.

17. Connect Phase 37 to Phase 38
The runtime should now convert a validated abstract gesture into an actual provider-supported request.

async def ground_vocal_gesture(
    *,
    grounding_target,
    provider_identity,
    grounding_resolver,
    provider_request_builder,
    actual_tts_adapter,
    audio_measurement_engine,
    generation_context,
):
    resolution = await grounding_resolver.resolve(
        grounding_target,
        provider_identity,
    )

    if resolution.mode.value == "calibrated":
        if resolution.grounded_control is None:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "Calibrated resolution returned no grounded controls."
            )

        provider_request = (
            await provider_request_builder.from_grounded_control(
                resolution.grounded_control,
                provider_identity=provider_identity,
            )
        )

    else:
        if not resolution.fallback_request_reference:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "No validated fallback request is available."
            )

        provider_request = (
            await provider_request_builder.from_validated_reference(

This is a wiring template: the request-builder and generation interfaces must be adapted to your actual Phase 21 provider adapter and Phase 8 measurement system. The generation object must expose a real audio reference produced by that adapter.

The returned measurement must then enter your existing observation and telemetry pipeline. Calibration should not be inferred from the request-builder output alone; it comes from the relationship between the requested gesture and the measured audio.

18. Make confidence evidence-derived and reproducible
One remaining integration detail is important: the confidence record used by GroundedVoiceResolver must actually be produced and persisted during candidate building. A scoring policy should be versioned and validated rather than silently hardcoded.

Replace CalibrationConfidenceService in confidence.py with this implementation, retaining the CalibrationAssessment and CalibrationConfidence dataclasses from above.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol
import math

from .mapping import CalibrationMapping
from .observation import VoiceObservation


_CONFIDENCE_COMPONENTS = (
    "reproducibility",
    "stability",
    "measurement_quality",
    "model_fit",
    "inlier_rate",
    "sample_support",
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationConfidenceScoringPolicy:
    weights: Mapping[str, float]
    full_support_observation_count: int
    version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if set(self.weights) != set(_CONFIDENCE_COMPONENTS):
            raise ValueError(
                "Confidence policy must define every scoring component."
            )

        if self.full_support_observation_count < 1:
            raise ValueError(
                "Full-support observation count must be positive."
            )

The weights and observation-support requirement must come from a validated release configuration. This score is a transparent operational confidence indicator—not a claim that the calibration is scientifically perfect. The mapping builder should store the returned confidence_reference in its candidate, and the resolver should retrieve the corresponding persisted confidence record.

Before scoring, the evaluator also needs to ensure that observation count, measurement quality, reproducibility, outlier rate and model-fit values really were computed from the exact dataset referenced by the candidate.

Update the grounding package exports to include the scoring policy:

from .confidence import CalibrationConfidenceScoringPolicy

Add "CalibrationConfidenceScoringPolicy" to __all__.

19. Phase 38 completion criteria
Do not consider this phase production-ready until these checks pass against Aariya's actual TTS implementation.

Calibration trials use the existing real TTS adapter and persist references to the actual generated audio.
Acoustic measurements come from the established Phase 8 analyzer, with units and extraction-method versions recorded.
Each mapping is tied to the exact provider, voice configuration and relevant versions.
Interpolation uses actual matching calibration samples and refuses to extrapolate beyond their observed range.
Calibration confidence is computed from real evidence and a versioned scoring policy.
Mapping candidates undergo consistency and bounds validation before approval.
Missing calibration causes an explicitly recorded conservative fallback or a safe failure—not an invented control mapping.
Drift is assessed using real recent audio measurements. Confirmed drift invalidates the affected mapping and triggers recalibration review.
Mapping activation is authorized, versioned, auditable and atomic; existing versions remain recoverable.
Changes in provider configuration invalidate the assumption that older calibration is still applicable.
Your source also emphasizes that calibration should characterize Aariya's own selected voice, including its usable pitch range, speaking-rate behavior, emphasis response, articulation, pause behavior, stability and identity consistency. This is calibration of the selected voice, not cloning another person's voice. 
*voice TTS.txt

Implementation status: The Phase 38 core data models and orchestration interfaces are provided. The actual TTS/measurement adapters, dataset repository, calibration model builder, assessment engine, confidence persistence, drift policy and activation transaction remain to be implemented and tested against your codebase. No real audio has been generated or calibrated in this conversation.


Phase 39 — Autonomous Conversational Voice Adaptation
Phase 38 grounded Aariya’s prosody in real TTS output and measured acoustic evidence. Phase 39 adds the next layer: adapting Aariya’s delivery to the user’s conversational rhythm without copying the user’s voice.

Your TTS notes specify five adaptation dimensions: tempo, pauses, energy, interruption/turn length, and semantic emphasis. They also require separate handling for the user’s current behavior and long-term rhythm profile, with every adapted performance passing Aariya’s identity constraints. 
*voice TTS.txt

39.1 Target architecture
Real user observations

VAD / ASR / audio analysis · speaking rate · pauses · energy · turn-taking

Current state + long-term rhythm profile

Adaptation controller

Tempo · pause timing · energy · turn length · semantic emphasis

Policy + identity gate

Reject unauthorized or out-of-bounds changes

Phase 37 prosody → Phase 38 grounding → real TTS

Measure the actual outcome → store evidence → evaluate learning

The key boundary is adaptation, not imitation. Aariya can become more concise or adjust response timing, but must not reproduce the user’s timbre, accent, vocal identity, or exact pitch contour. Long-term preferences must emerge from repeated real interactions, not a single interruption or temporary change in speaking rate. 
*voice TTS.txt

39.3 Real user observations
observation.py

Observations must originate from the real microphone, VAD, ASR, and audio-analysis pipeline. Record missing measurements as None; do not manufacture replacement values. The authorization reference ties collection to the applicable microphone/consent workflow.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from math import isfinite
from typing import Mapping


def _validate_features(
    features: Mapping[str, float],
    field_name: str,
) -> None:
    for name, value in features.items():
        if not name.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                f"{field_name} contains an empty feature name"
            )

        if not isfinite(value):
            raise ValueError(
                f"{field_name}.{name} must be finite"
            )


def _validate_probability(
    value: float | None,
    field_name: str,
) -> None:
    if value is None:
        return

    if not isfinite(value) or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{field_name} must be a probability in [0, 1]"
        )


This validates measurements and their provenance; it does not perform VAD, speech recognition, speaking-rate extraction, or emotion analysis. Those values must come from your existing actual analysis pipeline.

39.4 Separate current behavior from the long-term profile
user_state.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Mapping

from .observation import UserVoiceObservation


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UserVoiceState:
    current_speaking_rate: float | None
    current_energy: float | None
    current_pause_pattern: Mapping[str, float]

    interruption_probability: float | None
    turn_completion_probability: float | None

    observation_reference: str
    observed_at: datetime

    @classmethod
    def from_observation(
        cls,
        observation: UserVoiceObservation,
        observation_reference: str,
    ) -> "UserVoiceState":
        if not observation_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Persisted observation reference is required"
            )

        return cls(
            current_speaking_rate=observation.speaking_rate,
            current_energy=observation.current_energy,
            current_pause_pattern=observation.pause_statistics,

profile.py

A user's long-term rhythm profile is different from their present conversational state. The profile is absent until the system has enough genuine history to derive it.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Mapping


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class UserRhythmProfile:
    typical_speaking_rate: float | None
    typical_pause_pattern: Mapping[str, float]
    typical_energy: float | None
    interruption_tendency: float | None
    turn_shape_distribution: Mapping[str, float]

    observation_count: int
    history_reference: str
    profile_version: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    updated_at: datetime

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.observation_count <= 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "A persisted profile requires real observations"
            )

        if not self.history_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("history_reference is required")

        if not self.profile_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("profile_version is required")

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "A profile requires evidence references"

A profile estimator should aggregate actual historical observations and return a versioned profile. Its minimum sample requirements and statistical method belong in configuration and validation policy—not arbitrary constants embedded in the runtime.

39.5 Adaptation context and policy

from __future__ import annotations

    PAUSE = "pause"
    ENERGY = "energy"
    INTERRUPTION = "interruption"

    pause_alignment_allowed: bool

    interruption_alignment_allowed: bool
    emphasis_alignment_allowed: bool

        required = (
            self.policy_reference,
            self.identity_model_reference,
            self.calibration_context_reference,
        )

        if any(not value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError(
                "Policy, identity, and calibration references "

The policy only authorizes adaptation dimensions. It does not authorize voice imitation, override the identity gate, or imply that a provider can produce an uncalibrated effect.

39.6 Build the calibrated adjustment interface
adjustment_engine.py

This is the boundary between Phase 39 and the validated voice-performance space from Phase 38.

The semantics matter here:

Tempo: Adjust Aariya's response rhythm without matching the user's exact speaking rate.

Pauses: Use conversational meaning and turn-taking information rather than copying the user's pause lengths.

Energy: Change Aariya's expressive delivery within validated limits, not copy the user's loudness.

Interruption: Use repeated evidence about turn boundaries and response length; do not permanently modify a profile after one interruption.

Emphasis: Highlight concepts selected by the semantic/discourse system, not acoustic characteristics copied from the user.

These are the distinctions specified in your Phase 39 notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

39.7 Protect Aariya's identity

The identity gate must consult the real Phase 11 identity model and validated constraints. It should not contain an improvised pitch range or other guessed identity limits.

If the identity model or calibration validator is unavailable, the adaptation does not pass. The original performance target is returned instead of the unvalidated candidate.

39.8 Combine the adapters in the controller
controller.py

This follows your notes' sequence: apply the dimension adapters, then validate the combined result against Aariya's identity. If an adapter fails, discard the partial set of changes rather than leaving a partly adapted target active.

39.10 Keep long-term learning separate
learning.py

The live adaptation controller should make a decision for the current response. Learning evaluates whether that decision helped after the actual outcome becomes available.

The evaluator must use real outcome evidence. Useful outcomes from your notes include turn-taking success, interruption frequency, conversation continuation, latency, acoustic quality, and human evaluation. Those signals should not automatically be treated as evidence that the voice itself caused the result; the content, timing, or technical pipeline may also be responsible. 
*voice TTS.txt

39.11 Connect the live pipeline
The service coordinating the new modules should follow this sequence:

# 1. Receive the observation generated by the actual voice-analysis
#    pipeline, after applicable permission checks.

observation_reference = await repository.save_observation(
    real_user_observation
)

# 2. Construct current user state from that persisted observation.
current_state = UserVoiceState.from_observation(
    real_user_observation,
    observation_reference,
)

# 3. Retrieve only the user's real, persisted profile.
profile = await repository.get_profile(
    real_user_observation.user_id
)

# 4. Build context from the actual conversation, current
#    Aariya performance state, and validated identity model.
context = AdaptationContext(
    current_state=current_state,
    long_term_profile=profile,
    conversation_context=real_conversation_context,
    aariya_performance_state=current_aariya_performance,
    aariya_identity=validated_aariya_identity,
)

# 5. Adapt the Phase 37 performance target.
result = await controller.adapt(
    context=context,
    performance_target=phase37_performance_target,
    adaptation_policy=active_adaptation_policy,
)

# 6. Persist the attempt and its evidence references.

The names prefixed with real_, phase37_, current_, and active_ above represent dependencies to bind to your existing implementation, not predefined objects already available in this package.

39.12 Completion criteria
Do not mark Phase 39 complete just because the modules import successfully.


Live user-rhythm observations originate from actual authorized audio-analysis events.

Current user behavior remains separate from the long-term rhythm profile.

All five adapters use the real conversation context and calibrated performance constraints.

Missing calibration or missing evidence prevents an unsupported change.

The identity gate validates the combined candidate before it reaches prosodic composition.

An adapter or identity-validation failure restores the original performance target.

Actual TTS output and its measurements are linked to the adaptation attempt.

Learning uses actual outcomes and does not permanently update the profile after one isolated event.

PostgreSQL persistence, operational telemetry, and the existing Phase 16, 17, 36, 37, and 38 integrations are exercised by integration tests.


Phase 40 — Autonomous Outcome-Based Voice Learning
Phase 39 adapts Aariya’s delivery to conversational rhythm. Phase 40 asks whether that delivery actually helps the conversation.

Your TTS document defines the learning chain as response → performance plan → actual voice → user reaction → conversation outcome → interaction analysis → future voice decisions. It also distinguishes acoustic quality from conversational success and requires real evidence with provenance. 
*voice TTS.txt

40.1 Core architecture
Aariya's actual response

Policy · trajectory · prosodic composition · TTS

Measured voice performance

Actual rate · pauses · emphasis · latency · turn boundaries

User response and interaction sequence

Continuation · interruption · clarification · follow-up · session outcome

Outcome evaluator + interaction model

Feature attribution · association · causal hypotheses · evidence quality

Knowledge graph → experiments → governed policy updates

The key rule is that an interruption after a response is an observed event—not proof that the voice caused it. Topic difficulty, response length, content completeness, urgency, and other contextual factors can contribute. Phase 40 must separate observed associations from causal hypotheses and leave uncertain findings unresolved. 
*voice TTS.txt

40.2 Create the module
Use the directory specified in your source:

backend/app/voice/interaction_model/

├── event.py
├── response.py
├── outcome.py
├── sequence.py
├── attribution.py
├── features.py
├── association.py
├── causal.py
├── reward.py
├── evaluator.py
├── repository.py
└── controller.py
The implementation below establishes the event contracts, evidence records, analysis interfaces, and orchestration. Components that require your real audio measurements, user-response signals, statistical analysis, or knowledge graph remain explicit integration points rather than pretending to be completed.

40.3 Record actual runtime events
event.py

The interaction model must record events from the real runtime rather than invent event sequences for training. Each event has a session, turn, timestamp, type, and optional reference to its source payload. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceInteractionEvent:
    id: UUID
    session_id: UUID
    turn_id: UUID
    event_type: str
    timestamp: datetime
    payload_reference: str | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.event_type.strip():
            raise ValueError("event_type cannot be empty")

        if (
            self.timestamp.tzinfo is None
            or self.timestamp.utcoffset() is None
        ):
            raise ValueError("timestamp must be timezone-aware")

        if (
            self.payload_reference is not None
            and not self.payload_reference.strip()
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "payload_reference cannot be blank"
            )

    @classmethod
    def create(

Connect this to the events Aariya's runtime already emits, including:

40.5 Model what happened after the response
outcome.py

One important implementation detail: an unknown measurement must not be treated as a negative result. For example, no interruption event detected is only evidence of no interruption when the event-monitoring pipeline actually observed the relevant turn.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite
from typing import Any, Mapping
from uuid import UUID


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ConversationOutcome:
    turn_id: UUID

    continued: bool | None
    interruption: bool | None
    clarification_requested: bool | None

    response_latency_ms: float | None
    next_turn_latency_ms: float | None

    user_signal_features: Mapping[str, Any]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for field_name in (
            "continued",
            "interruption",
            "clarification_requested",
        ):
            value = getattr(self, field_name)

            if value is not None and not isinstance(value, bool):
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{field_name} must be bool or None"
                )

        for field_name in (

Outcome evidence should come from the conversation event stream, actual response timings, real user-response signals, and validated interaction analysis. Avoid inferring a user's feelings or satisfaction from a single interruption.

40.6 Preserve the event sequence
sequence.py

The source emphasizes analyzing the sequence of events rather than isolating one event. A response followed by an interruption, cancellation, and user rephrasing contains more information than the interruption by itself. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from .event import VoiceInteractionEvent


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class InteractionSequence:
    events: tuple[VoiceInteractionEvent, ...] = ()

    def append(
        self,
        event: VoiceInteractionEvent,
    ) -> "InteractionSequence":
        if any(existing.id == event.id for existing in self.events):
            raise ValueError("Duplicate event ID")

        return InteractionSequence(
            events=self.events + (event,)
        )

    def chronological(
        self,
    ) -> tuple[VoiceInteractionEvent, ...]:
        # Sort for analysis because distributed event ingestion
        # may not follow the original event timestamps.
        return tuple(
            sorted(
                self.events,
                key=lambda item: item.timestamp,
            )
        )

In production, retain the original event identities and ingestion metadata too. Timestamp sorting is useful for analysis but must not destroy the original event records.

40.7 Extract performance and context features
features.py

This layer combines measured voice properties with linguistic features, turn-taking events, user responses, and conversational progression.

The protocol deliberately leaves speech-feature extraction to your existing measured-audio pipeline. It does not assume that an intended speaking rate is the same as the rate actually produced.

40.8 Attribute outcomes to the performance that preceded them
attribution.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Mapping
from uuid import UUID

from .features import InteractionFeatureSet
from .outcome import ConversationOutcome


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceAttribution:
    turn_id: UUID

    features: InteractionFeatureSet
    outcome: ConversationOutcome
    response_record_reference: str

    context_reference: str
    policy_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.features.turn_id != self.turn_id:
            raise ValueError(
                "Feature set and attribution turn IDs differ"
            )

        if self.outcome.turn_id != self.turn_id:
            raise ValueError(
                "Outcome and attribution turn IDs differ"
            )

        if not self.response_record_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Response record reference is required"
            )

This produces a traceable relationship between what Aariya delivered, the context, the policy that was active, and what followed. It is evidence for analysis, not proof of causality.

40.9 Represent outcomes without inventing a universal reward
Your notes explicitly avoid a fake universal reward and instead use a structured outcome vector. 
*voice TTS.txt

reward.py

The following version adds units and provenance to each metric. That prevents, for example, a latency value in milliseconds from being compared directly with a continuation measure as if they shared the same scale.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OutcomeMetric:
    value: float
    unit: str
    definition: str
    evaluator_version: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not isfinite(self.value):
            raise ValueError("Metric value must be finite")

        for name, value in (
            ("unit", self.unit),
            ("definition", self.definition),
            ("evaluator_version", self.evaluator_version),
        ):
            if not value.strip():
                raise ValueError(f"{name} is required")

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Metric requires evidence references"
            )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class InteractionOutcomeVector:
    conversational_continuation: OutcomeMetric | None
    interruption_behavior: OutcomeMetric | None

Every dimension can be absent if it was not measured. The system should retain the individual metrics instead of collapsing them into one arbitrary score. If you later choose to make a multi-objective decision, define its metric direction, trade-offs, and policy in a separately versioned component.

40.11 Distinguish association from causality
Phase 40 has two separate analysis interfaces, matching your source's distinction. 
*voice TTS.txt

causal.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import Enum
from typing import Any, Protocol, Sequence

from .association import AssociationFinding


class CausalEvidenceStatus(str, Enum):
    SUPPORTED = "supported"
    INCONCLUSIVE = "inconclusive"
    INSUFFICIENT_DATA = "insufficient_data"
    CONTRADICTED = "contradicted"


@dataclass(frozen=True)

    status: CausalEvidenceStatus
    explanation: str

    outcome_feature: str

    async def analyze(
        self,
        *,
        association: AssociationFinding,
        observations: Sequence[Any],

An actual causal analyzer needs a defined statistical method, sufficient real observations, and appropriate treatment of confounding. These interfaces do not claim to implement causal inference by themselves. If the data cannot support a conclusion, the correct result is INSUFFICIENT_DATA or INCONCLUSIVE.

40.12 Persist the results with evidence scope
Phase 40 separates three scopes:

Global: general voice-behavior evidence validated across appropriate data.

User-specific: evidence used to personalize one user's experience.

Session: the active conversation's short-lived state.

This prevents one user's preference from becoming a global voice rule. 
*voice TTS.txt

repository.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any, Protocol, Sequence

from .association import AssociationFinding
from .attribution import PerformanceAttribution
from .causal import CausalAssessment
from .evaluator import InteractionEvaluation


class EvidenceScopeType(str, Enum):
    GLOBAL = "global"
    USER = "user"
    SESSION = "session"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EvidenceScope:
    scope_type: EvidenceScopeType
    user_id: str | None = None
    session_id: str | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.scope_type == EvidenceScopeType.GLOBAL:
            if self.user_id is not None or self.session_id is not None:
                raise ValueError(
                    "Global scope cannot contain user/session IDs"
                )

        elif self.scope_type == EvidenceScopeType.USER:
            if not self.user_id or self.session_id is not None:
                raise ValueError(
                    "User scope requires only user_id"
                )

The repository protocol still needs a PostgreSQL implementation, migrations, indexes, transaction handling, and your existing privacy/retention controls. It must not silently mix user-specific observations into a global analysis cohort.

40.15 Integrate Phase 40 into Aariya's voice runtime
The live path and the learning path should be separate.

LIVE RESPONSE
    ↓
Phase 36 performance trajectory
    ↓
Phase 37 prosodic composition
    ↓
Phase 38 grounded TTS
    ↓
Actual audio and audio measurements
    ↓
Interaction events collected
    ↓
Actual user-response events collected
    ↓
Phase 40 turn analysis persisted
    ↓
Background/cohort analysis over real history
    ↓
Association findings
    ↓
Causal assessment with limitations
    ↓
Knowledge graph
    ↓
Phase 33 experiment selection
    ↓
Phase 34 controlled validation
    ↓
Phase 35 governed policy update
This division prevents an unvalidated pattern from changing the voice policy during the same response that produced the evidence.

40.16 What must be implemented before calling Phase 40 complete
Integration checklist
0 of 10


Connect actual runtime events to VoiceInteractionEvent.

Link the actual TTS output to policy, trajectory, composition, and acoustic measurements.

Implement the feature extractor using actual voice, language, context, and turn-taking data.

Implement outcome evaluators using the existing validated subsystems.

Implement PostgreSQL persistence, transactional writes, and authorized cohort selection.

Implement and test association analysis on repeated real observations.

Implement causal analysis with documented confounders, assumptions, and uncertainty.

Keep global, user-specific, and session evidence isolated.

Connect validated findings to the knowledge graph and governed experiment workflow.

Test missing data, out-of-order events, duplicate events, persistence failures, and inconclusive results.


Phase 41 — Autonomous Conversational Voice Prediction
Phase 40 records what happens after Aariya speaks. Phase 41 adds a forward-looking model: before speaking, Aariya estimates the likely effects of several valid delivery strategies, compares their predicted outcomes and uncertainty, and selects a strategy using the current decision policy. 
*voice TTS.txt

41.1 Architecture
Current conversation + Aariya state

User rhythm · context · policy · history · knowledge · provider capabilities

Generate candidate performances

Only alternatives supported by the validated voice space

Hard-constraint filtering

Identity · active voice policy · actual provider capabilities

Predict outcomes + quantify uncertainty

Continuation · interruption · clarification · naturalness · latency

Rank candidates under the active decision policy

Revalidate → compose selected performance → grounded TTS

Compare predictions with actual outcomes → learn

The distinction between the phases is important:

Phase 39: Adapt Aariya's delivery to the user's present conversational rhythm.

Phase 40: Learn associations between actual delivery and subsequent conversation outcomes.

Phase 41: Use accumulated evidence to estimate which valid delivery is likely to work best before it is executed.

The prediction must remain an estimate, not a fact. A high predicted benefit cannot override identity, policy, provider capability, or safety constraints. If prediction is unavailable, Aariya should retain a validated deterministic way to continue the conversation rather than becoming unable to speak. 
*voice TTS.txt

41.2 Create the package
Add these modules under backend/app/voice/prediction/. This follows the directory in your TTS document, with a small amount of additional structure in the implementation to keep decisions and evidence traceable. 
*voice TTS.txt

├── context.py
├── candidate.py
├── features.py
├── predictor.py
├── outcome.py
├── uncertainty.py

41.3 Build the prediction context
context.py

The predictor receives explicit context instead of discovering dependencies through hidden global state. This includes the user's present state, Aariya's state, the voice policy, recent history, knowledge, identity, and actual provider capabilities.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictionContext:
    session_id: str
    turn_id: str
    context_reference: str
    history_reference: str

    conversation_context: Any
    user_state: Any
    aariya_state: Any
    voice_policy: Any

    performance_history: tuple[Any, ...]
    knowledge_context: tuple[Any, ...]

    identity_state: Any
    provider_capabilities: Any

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "session_id",
            "turn_id",
            "context_reference",
            "history_reference",
        ):
            if not getattr(self, name).strip():
                raise ValueError(f"{name} is required")

The context references should point to real, appropriately authorized data in Aariya's existing memory and interaction systems. They should not contain fabricated histories to make a prediction possible.

41.4 Define candidate performances
candidate.py

A candidate is a proposed delivery—not audio that has already been generated.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Mapping


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceCandidate:
    candidate_id: str
    target: Any
    composition_parameters: Mapping[str, Any]
    rationale: tuple[str, ...]
    source_evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.candidate_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("candidate_id is required")

        if not self.rationale:
            raise ValueError(
                "Candidate must explain why it was generated"
            )

        if not self.source_evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Candidate requires policy or calibration provenance"
            )

The candidate generator should create a small set of meaningful alternatives from Aariya's current policy and validated performance space. It must not generate arbitrary voice parameter combinations.

For example, valid candidates might differ in response density, emphasis, timing, expressive activity, and pause structure. Whether any specific alternative is valid depends on Aariya's actual policy and Phase 38 calibration.

41.5 Generate candidates from validated capabilities
features.py

from __future__ import annotations

from .context import PredictionContext


class CandidatePerformanceGenerator(Protocol):
    async def generate(
        self,
        context: PredictionContext,
    ) -> tuple[PerformanceCandidate, ...]:
        """
        Generate meaningful alternatives from the active policy
        and actual validated performance space.

        Do not use random parameter combinations or assume that
        the TTS provider supports unverified behavior.
        """
        ...

This interface belongs above Phase 37's prosodic composition and Phase 38's grounding. Candidate generation proposes alternatives; only the selected candidate is fully composed and sent through the real TTS pipeline.

41.6 Represent predicted outcomes and uncertainty
uncertainty.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictionUncertainty:
    overall: float
    reasons: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if (
            not isfinite(self.overall)
            or not 0.0 <= self.overall <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "overall uncertainty must be in [0, 1]"
            )

        if not self.reasons:
            raise ValueError(
                "Uncertainty assessment needs an explanation"
            )

The uncertainty value must come from a documented model or uncertainty-estimation method. The range above defines the interface's normalized representation; it does not claim that a predictor already has a calibrated uncertainty estimator.

outcome.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite

from .uncertainty import PredictionUncertainty


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictedOutcome:
    # Predicted probabilities, when the model supports them.
    conversational_continuation: float | None
    interruption_likelihood: float | None
    clarification_likelihood: float | None

    # These three metrics use documented, versioned scales.
    expected_naturalness: float | None
    expected_identity_stability: float | None
    expected_latency: float | None

    uncertainty: PredictionUncertainty

    model_reference: str
    model_version: str
    training_cohort_reference: str
    context_reference: str
    metric_scale_reference: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        probabilities = (
            "conversational_continuation",
            "interruption_likelihood",
            "clarification_likelihood",
        )


A predicted outcome is not an observed outcome. For example, a predicted interruption probability must not be saved as though an interruption actually occurred. The predictor should use real Phase 40 interaction history and preserve the model version and evidence cohort used to produce its estimate. 
*voice TTS.txt

41.8 Filter hard constraints before prediction
constraints.py

Predicted benefit cannot make an identity violation acceptable. Filter invalid candidates before spending resources on predictions, then revalidate the selected candidate before execution.

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

from .candidate import PerformanceCandidate

        self,

        provider_capabilities: object,

        Consult actual identity, policy, provider capability,
        and calibrated performance-space validators.
        """
        ...


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CandidateFilterResult:
    valid_candidates: tuple[PerformanceCandidate, ...]

CandidateValidator remains an integration contract to the real Phase 11 identity model, active voice policy, Phase 38 grounded calibration, and provider capability registry. Until those validators are connected, the constraint engine is a scaffold—not proof that any particular voice candidate is safe.

41.9 Rank candidates without an unexplained universal score
ranking.py

Your source requires ranking to consider conversational benefit, naturalness, identity, latency, risk, and uncertainty without concealing everything inside an arbitrary hardcoded score. 
*voice TTS.txt

A practical implementation is a policy-configured lexicographic ranker. It compares metrics in an explicit order rather than adding unlike measurements together. The active policy supplies the objective priorities and maximum permitted prediction uncertainty.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from math import isfinite
from typing import Mapping, Sequence

from .candidate import PerformanceCandidate
from .outcome import PredictedOutcome


class PredictionDimension(str, Enum):
    CONTINUATION = "conversational_continuation"
    INTERRUPTION = "interruption_likelihood"
    CLARIFICATION = "clarification_likelihood"
    NATURALNESS = "expected_naturalness"
    IDENTITY_STABILITY = "expected_identity_stability"
    LATENCY = "expected_latency"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RankingObjective:
    dimension: PredictionDimension
    maximize: bool


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CandidateRankingPolicy:
    policy_reference: str
    objectives: tuple[RankingObjective, ...]
    maximum_uncertainty: float

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.policy_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("policy_reference is required")


Configuration is part of the design, not an omitted detail. The ranking policy must be supplied by Aariya's active voice/meta-decision system. This code does not invent objective priorities or uncertainty thresholds. It also skips candidates missing a metric required by the configured policy, rather than treating missing values as favorable.

The ranker cannot override hard constraints. A candidate that scores well but cannot reliably produce its requested vocal behavior should already have been rejected by the constraint engine. 
*voice TTS.txt

41.11 Implement the selection controller
controller.py

The controller generates candidates, filters them, predicts outcomes, ranks the eligible candidates, and validates the winner again. It uses a configured real-time deadline. On timeout, prediction failure, or lack of a usable candidate, it delegates to the existing validated deterministic voice policy.

The prediction deadline must come from the real latency configuration in Phase 25. It is deliberately not assigned a made-up default.

One subtle but important detail: the candidate is revalidated immediately before it is returned. The execution layer should also check that the selected target is still current before sending it to TTS. This prevents an outdated prediction from bypassing a changed policy or provider capability.

41.12 Compare predictions with actual outcomes
A prediction model needs feedback, otherwise it can become confidently wrong as conversations or provider configurations change. Your notes specify comparing predictions against actual outcomes, recording prediction errors, and checking whether accuracy is degrading under distribution shift. 
*voice TTS.txt

feedback.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Mapping, Protocol

from .outcome import PredictedOutcome


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictionError:
    prediction_reference: str
    actual_outcome_reference: str

    model_reference: str
    model_version: str

    metric_errors: Mapping[str, float | None]
    comparator_version: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        for name in (
            "prediction_reference",
            "actual_outcome_reference",
            "model_reference",
            "model_version",
            "comparator_version",
        ):
            if not getattr(self, name).strip():
                raise ValueError(f"{name} is required")

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Prediction error needs outcome provenance"
            )


The actual comparison method must be calibrated for each metric. For example, a predicted interruption probability can be compared with an observed interruption indicator using a suitable probability-scoring method. A latency forecast requires a numeric residual with consistent units. Naturalness should be compared only when an actual, validated naturalness assessment exists.

calibration.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Mapping, Protocol, Sequence


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictionCalibrationReport:
    model_reference: str
    model_version: str
    cohort_reference: str

    observations_evaluated: int
    calibration_metrics: Mapping[str, float | None]

    valid_for_current_context: bool
    limitations: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    evaluator_version: str


class PredictionCalibrationEvaluator(Protocol):
    async def evaluate(
        self,
        historical_prediction_outcome_pairs: Sequence[Any],
        *,
        cohort_reference: str,
    ) -> PredictionCalibrationReport:
        """
        Assess predictions against matched actual outcomes.

        The implementation must detect insufficient evidence,
        degraded calibration, and relevant distribution changes.
        """
        ...

This interface must be connected to the same provider and voice-configuration identity used by Phase 38. A provider change, policy change, or changed interaction pattern can invalidate previous model assumptions. The calibration report should inform whether a model remains fit for a given context, not automatically authorize a model update.

41.13 Connect the selected candidate to the live voice pipeline
The integration point should look like this:

# A) Gather the actual current context from Aariya's runtime.
context = await prediction_context_builder.build(
    conversation=current_conversation,
    user_state=current_user_state,
    aariya_state=current_aariya_state,
    policy=active_voice_policy,
    history=authorized_performance_history,
    knowledge=actual_voice_knowledge,
    identity=active_identity_state,
    provider_capabilities=current_provider_capabilities,
)

# B) Select a target using predictions or the existing validated fallback.
selection = await prediction_controller.select(context)

if selection.candidate is None:
    # Preserve the existing voice-system failure policy.
    # Do not attempt to execute an unvalidated candidate.
    return await existing_voice_failure_handler.handle(
        context=context,
        reason=selection.reason,
    )

# C) Only the selected target proceeds through Phase 37.
composed = await existing_prosodic_composer.compose(
    selection.candidate.target,
    context=context,
)

# D) Ground the actual vocal gestures with Phase 38.
grounded = await existing_voice_grounder.resolve(
    composed,
    context=context,
)

# E) Generate real audio using the existing TTS adapter.

The adapters above refer to existing Aariya subsystems; they are placeholders for your actual class and method names, not newly verified APIs. Prediction output must also remain separate from actual audio and outcome records so the learning pipeline cannot confuse estimates with observations.

41.14 Completion criteria
Phase 41 checklist
0 of 11


Build candidates from real policy and calibrated performance space.

Filter candidates against current identity, policy, and provider capabilities.

Train or configure the predictor using actual Phase 40 interaction evidence.

Attach model, context, calibration-scale, and training-cohort references to predictions.

Represent missing metrics and uncertainty explicitly.

Rank using a versioned, explicit policy rather than an unexplained universal score.

Revalidate the selected candidate immediately before execution.

Use the existing validated deterministic fallback on prediction failure or deadline exhaustion.

Persist predictions alongside actual outcomes and measured prediction errors.

Monitor prediction accuracy and distribution shift across provider and policy versions.

Test low-data contexts, conflicting evidence, tied candidates, missing metrics, timeouts, and validator failure.
Implementation status: Phase 41's data contracts, constraint-filtering logic, explicit ranking approach, prediction orchestration, fallback boundary, and feedback interfaces are laid out here. The actual prediction model, candidate generator, real identity/provider validators, database adapter, outcome comparators, and calibration evaluator still need to be connected to Aariya's implementation and tested. No live prediction model was trained or run in this conversation.


Phase 42 — Autonomous Voice Decision Policy & Meta-Control
Phase 41 predicts what may happen if Aariya uses a particular voice strategy. Phase 42 decides which objectives matter most right now, whether changing the current delivery is worthwhile, and when Aariya should abstain from adaptive optimization.

Your source defines the core principle: meta-control determines the priorities for lower-level systems; it does not directly synthesize TTS parameters. It also identifies “do nothing” as a legitimate decision and requires hard constraints to be enforced before optimization. 
*voice TTS.txt

42.1 Architecture
Actual conversation + system state

User state · context · voice policy · predictions · identity · provider capabilities

Abstention check

Weak evidence · high uncertainty · unclear identity impact · unknown provider

Resolve objectives and hard constraints

Filter strategies before optimization

Select strategy or retain current delivery

Decision confidence + explanation + audit record

For example, a technical explanation may prioritize clarity and responsiveness over expressiveness. A sensitive conversation may prioritize warmth and restraint. In neither case can an objective override Aariya’s validated identity boundary. The priorities come from the situation; they are not permanent personality settings. 
*voice TTS.txt

The implementation below keeps Phase 20’s Voice Strategy Engine, Phase 41’s predictor, and Aariya’s existing voice pipeline as separate dependencies.

Your notes specify this package. Keep it independent from the existing Voice Strategy Engine and TTS implementation.

42.3 Define objectives
objective.py

An objective is something to optimize, not permission to break a hard constraint.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceObjective:
    name: str
    importance: float
    minimum_required: float | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Objective name is required")

        if (
            not isfinite(self.importance)
            or not 0.0 <= self.importance <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Objective importance must be in [0, 1]"
            )

        if self.minimum_required is not None:
            if (
                not isfinite(self.minimum_required)
                or not 0.0 <= self.minimum_required <= 1.0
            ):
                raise ValueError(
                    "minimum_required must be in [0, 1]"
                )

Possible objectives, as defined in your source, include identity_consistency, clarity, responsiveness, naturalness, warmth, expressiveness, restraint, turn_taking, latency, and user_alignment. 
*voice TTS.txt

Their actual priorities must be resolved from the current situation, not permanently embedded as default numbers.

The controller receives the relevant context, but doesn't own or replace the underlying conversation, memory, prediction, or identity systems.

42.5 Resolve a priority profile
priority.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ObjectivePriority:
    objective: str
    priority: float

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.objective.strip():
            raise ValueError("Objective name is required")

        if (
            not isfinite(self.priority)
            or not 0.0 <= self.priority <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Priority must be in [0, 1]"
            )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PriorityProfile:
    priorities: tuple[ObjectivePriority, ...]
    policy_reference: str
    context_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        names = [item.objective for item in self.priorities]

        if len(names) != len(set(names)):
            raise ValueError(
                "Priority profile contains duplicate objectives"

The profile should be a versioned decision output. The same identity can express different levels of warmth or expressiveness in different contexts without changing Aariya's underlying vocal identity.

42.6 Represent objective trade-offs
tradeoff.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ObjectiveTradeoff:
    objective_a: str
    objective_b: str
    conflict_strength: float
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.objective_a.strip():
            raise ValueError("objective_a is required")

        if not self.objective_b.strip():
            raise ValueError("objective_b is required")

        if self.objective_a == self.objective_b:
            raise ValueError(
                "A trade-off must involve distinct objectives"
            )

        if (
            not isfinite(self.conflict_strength)
            or not 0.0 <= self.conflict_strength <= 1.0
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "conflict_strength must be in [0, 1]"
            )

This gives Aariya an explicit representation of conflicts such as expressiveness versus identity consistency, or detail versus responsiveness. It doesn't assume that the conflict strength is already known; a real context or trade-off analysis service must supply it.

42.7 Enforce hard constraints before optimization
constraint.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Protocol, Sequence

from .context import MetaControlContext
from .decision import StrategyCandidate


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MetaConstraint:
    name: str
    required: bool
    description: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Constraint name is required")

        if not self.description.strip():
            raise ValueError("Constraint description is required")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CandidateConstraintAssessment:
    candidate_reference: str
    allowed: bool
    checked_constraints: tuple[str, ...]
    violation_reasons: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ConstraintFilterResult:
    allowed_candidates: tuple[StrategyCandidate, ...]
    assessments: tuple[CandidateConstraintAssessment, ...]

The enforcement implementation must treat required=True constraints as hard boundaries. It should reject candidates whose required constraints cannot be validated, not simply assign them a lower optimization score.

42.8 Define the available decisions
decision.py

A meta-decision may select a new strategy, retain the current strategy, fall back to a stable validated strategy, or report that no safe strategy is available.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Any

from .priority import PriorityProfile


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StrategyCandidate:
    reference: str
    strategy: Any
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("Strategy reference is required")

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Strategy candidate needs provenance"
            )


class DecisionAction(str, Enum):
    SELECT_STRATEGY = "select_strategy"
    RETAIN_CURRENT = "retain_current"
    USE_STABLE_VALIDATED = "use_stable_validated"
    NO_SAFE_STRATEGY = "no_safe_strategy"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StrategySelection:
    candidate: StrategyCandidate
    action: DecisionAction

An important distinction is that RETAIN_CURRENT means Aariya has determined that changing the current strategy is not justified. USE_STABLE_VALIDATED means she declined adaptive optimization and relied on a stable fallback instead.

                not isfinite(value)

The confidence evaluator needs a real implementation. The four measures are explicit fields, not a claim that they can be meaningfully averaged without a validated method.

42.10 Add the abstention mechanism

                not isfinite(value)
                or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0

                    f"{name} must be in [0, 1]"

The thresholds must be injected from a versioned, governed policy based on real validation evidence. No numerical thresholds have been invented here.

42.14 Implement the meta-controller

42.16 How the phases connect
The completed integration should preserve the following hierarchy, which matches your source's distinction between meta-control, strategy, policy, and actual performance. 
*voice TTS.txt

CURRENT CONVERSATION
        ↓
PHASE 41 — PREDICT OUTCOMES
        ↓
PHASE 42 — SELECT PRIORITIES
        ↓
HARD CONSTRAINTS / IDENTITY GATE
        ↓
PHASE 20 — VOICE STRATEGY
        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

        ↓

The meta-controller must not replace any of those systems. It coordinates the objectives and constraints they should use.

42.17 Completion checklist
Phase 42 readiness
0 / 12


Objectives and priority profiles are resolved from actual context.

Hard safety and identity constraints are enforced before optimization.

Prediction uncertainty and evidence quality can trigger abstention.

Retaining the current strategy is a valid outcome.

No candidate can bypass the constraint filter or final validation.

Decision confidence uses a real, versioned evaluator.

Every significant decision has a structured explanation.

Urgent overrides expire and do not permanently modify identity or policy.

Priority changes are temporally stable unless real context changes justify a transition.

Integration tests cover unknown providers, missing evidence, identity failures, abstention, and persistence errors.

Phase 43 — Autonomous Voice Planning & Multi-Turn Performance
Phase 42 decides what matters most now. Phase 43 allows Aariya to plan how her delivery may evolve across several upcoming conversational turns while keeping those plans provisional.

Your TTS notes specify three essential rules: plan across turns, commit only the current turn, and replan when reality changes. Future turns may depend on different user responses, so the plan needs conditional branches rather than one fixed sequence. 
*voice TTS.txt

43.1 Core architecture
Current user turn + actual context

Phase 42 meta-control

Resolve current objectives and priorities

Multi-turn voice plan

Current turn · possible next turns · conditional branches · voice trajectory

Validate plan → commit current turn only

Phase 36 trajectory → Phase 37 prosody → Phase 38 grounding → real TTS

Observe response → validate plan → continue or replan

For example, Aariya might plan to clarify a technical problem, explain its likely cause if the user confirms an assumption, and recommend a fix after enough information is available. If the user corrects that assumption, only the affected branches need to be invalidated; the rest of the plan can be retained if still valid. 
*voice TTS.txt

43.2 Create the package
Add these modules:

backend/app/voice/planning/
├── __init__.py
├── horizon.py
├── objective.py
├── context.py
├── state.py
├── turn_plan.py
├── trajectory.py
├── dependency.py
├── prediction.py
├── replanning.py
├── commitment.py
├── interrupt.py
├── uncertainty.py
├── validator.py
├── repository.py
└── controller.py
The planning layer manages future possibilities, dependencies, and voice trajectories. It does not replace Aariya's content planner or directly synthesize speech.

43.3 Make planning depth respond to uncertainty
horizon.py

The planning horizon should become shorter when the conversation is uncertain. The thresholds and planning depth must come from validated configuration rather than hardcoded assumptions.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .context import PlanningContext


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PlanningHorizon:
    turns: int
    confidence: float
    evidence_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.turns < 1:
            raise ValueError("Planning horizon must be positive")

        if not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Confidence must be in [0, 1]")

        if not self.evidence_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("Evidence reference is required")


class PlanningHorizonResolver(Protocol):
    async def resolve(
        self,
        context: PlanningContext,
    ) -> PlanningHorizon:
        """
        Use actual context and a validated horizon policy.

        Higher uncertainty should not result in a longer committed
        sequence. Future turns remain provisional in all cases.
        """

The resolver is an interface until you connect it to Aariya's actual context-confidence estimator and planning configuration.

43.4 Define the planning context
context.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PlanningContext:
    session_id: str
    current_turn_index: int
    context_reference: str
    actual_state_reference: str

    conversation_context: Any
    meta_decision: Any
    current_performance: Any

    identity_state: Any
    voice_policy: Any
    provider_capabilities: Any

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.session_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("session_id is required")

        if self.current_turn_index < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "current_turn_index must be non-negative"
            )

        if not self.context_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("context_reference is required")

        if not self.actual_state_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("actual_state_reference is required")

This binds planning to the current runtime state and Phase 42's decision. It does not independently decide conversational objectives or infer the user's next response.

43.5 Represent each planned turn

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite
from typing import Any


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PlannedTurn:

    # Produced by the existing content/voice planning components.

        if self.turn_index < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                "turn_index must be non-negative"
            )

        if not self.communicative_goal.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "communicative_goal is required"
            )

        if (
            not isfinite(self.confidence)
            or not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0
        ):

For example, a plan may contain a current clarification turn followed by a possible explanation and recommendation. Those future targets are hypotheses, not prewritten responses that Aariya is obliged to deliver.

43.6 Represent conditional dependencies
dependency.py

A future turn can depend on what the user says or does in the current turn. These dependencies should be explicit and testable.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TurnDependency:
    previous_turn_index: int
    next_turn_index: int
    condition: str

    condition_reference: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.previous_turn_index >= self.next_turn_index:
            raise ValueError(
                "Dependency must point to a later turn"
            )

        if self.previous_turn_index < 0:
            raise ValueError("Invalid previous turn index")

        if not self.condition.strip():
            raise ValueError("Condition is required")

        if not self.condition_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "Condition reference is required"
            )

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Dependency requires planning provenance"
            )

An example dependency graph:

TURN 1 — Clarify the problem
            |
       User response
        /       \
   confirms    corrects
      |           |
   TURN 2A      TURN 2B
   Explain      Reframe
      |           |
   TURN 3A      TURN 3B
   Recommend    Reassess
The dependency conditions need a real evaluator connected to conversation understanding. A branch must not execute simply because its condition was predicted to occur.

43.7 Define the multi-turn plan state
state.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from enum import Enum

from .dependency import TurnDependency

43.8 Plan a coherent voice trajectory
trajectory.py

The objective is to maintain continuity across turns instead of independently choosing an unrelated delivery for every response.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any, Protocol, Sequence

from .context import PlanningContext
from .turn_plan import PlannedTurn


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceTrajectory:
    trajectory_reference: str
    turn_indices: tuple[int, ...]
    transition_references: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.trajectory_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "trajectory_reference is required"
            )

        if not self.turn_indices:
            raise ValueError("Trajectory cannot be empty")

        if not self.evidence_references:
            raise ValueError(
                "Trajectory requires evidence references"
            )


class MultiTurnTrajectoryPlanner(Protocol):
    async def build(
        self,
        planned_turns: Sequence[PlannedTurn],
        current_performance: Any,

A complex problem-solving session could move from an accessible opening to focused explanation, then a precise solution, and finally a relaxed closure. These descriptions represent possible performance characteristics, not rigid emotional modes.

43.9 Predict possible future turns
prediction.py

This reuses Phase 41's prediction capability but extends the prediction target across upcoming turns.

            raise ValueError("Invalid turn_index")

        if not self.predicted_condition.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "predicted_condition is required"
            )

        if self.likelihood is not None:
            if (
                not isfinite(self.likelihood)
                or not 0.0 <= self.likelihood <= 1.0
            ):
                raise ValueError(

The plan should carry more uncertainty for turns further into the future. It is acceptable to prepare several possible branches; it is not acceptable to treat a low-confidence prediction as a confirmed user intention.

43.10 Validate the complete plan
validator.py

Every planned performance must remain within Aariya's validated identity, voice policy, and actual provider capabilities. Future turns receive validation as proposals; they still require current-state validation before commitment.

43.12 Replan when reality changes
replanning.py

The plan needs explicit invalidation triggers. Your notes list topic changes, user corrections, interruptions, unexpected emotional shifts, new information, system changes, and collapsing prediction confidence. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Protocol

from .context import PlanningContext
from .state import VoicePlan


class ReplanningTrigger(str, Enum):
    TOPIC_CHANGED = "topic_changed"
    USER_CORRECTION = "user_correction"
    USER_INTERRUPTION = "user_interruption"
    CONTEXT_SHIFT = "context_shift"
    NEW_INFORMATION = "new_information"
    SYSTEM_STATE_CHANGED = "system_state_changed"
    PROVIDER_CAPABILITY_CHANGED = "provider_capability_changed"
    PREDICTION_CONFIDENCE_COLLAPSED = (
        "prediction_confidence_collapsed"
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReplanRequest:
    trigger: ReplanningTrigger
    reason: str
    affected_turn_indices: tuple[int, ...]
    context_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.reason.strip():
            raise ValueError("Replan reason is required")

        if not self.context_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("context_reference is required")

Partial invalidation is preferable to deleting an entire plan when only one assumption has changed. For example, correcting the diagnosis of a technical problem should discard the branches that depend on the wrong diagnosis but may preserve unrelated conversation context.

43.15 Orchestrate the planning lifecycle

The controller below builds a plan, collects future-branch predictions, validates the plan, commits only the current turn, and delegates replanning when the conversation changes.

Production integration note: the repository operations that supersede a plan, invalidate branches, and save its replacement must be made atomic. The sequence above shows their responsibilities, but separate calls without transaction or compare-and-swap support can race with another turn or interruption. Implement those operations through a transactional repository method before relying on this controller in a live session.

Also, if plan building or validation fails, the caller should use Aariya's existing validated current-turn path when available. A speculative multi-turn plan must never block the ability to respond.

43.17 Integration with Aariya
The finished live flow should be:

CURRENT USER TURN
        ↓
PHASE 42 — META-CONTROL
        ↓
PHASE 43 — BUILD CONDITIONAL MULTI-TURN PLAN
        ↓
PHASE 41 — PREDICT UPCOMING BRANCHES
        ↓
VALIDATE PLAN AND VOICE TRAJECTORY
        ↓
COMMIT CURRENT TURN ONLY
        ↓
PHASE 36 — PERFORMANCE TRAJECTORY
        ↓
PHASE 37 — PROSODIC COMPOSITION
        ↓
PHASE 38 — REAL VOICE GROUNDING
        ↓
REAL TTS / PLAYBACK
        ↓
OBSERVE ACTUAL USER RESPONSE
        ↓
PLAN STILL VALID?
     /        \
   YES         NO
    ↓           ↓
CONTINUE       REPLAN
This is the intended relationship in your TTS notes: Phase 41 predicts immediate outcomes, Phase 42 determines current priorities, and Phase 43 plans conditional behavior across turns. 
*voice TTS.txt

43.18 Completion checklist
Phase 43 readiness
0 / 12


Planning horizon comes from an actual confidence estimator and governed configuration.

Current and future turns have explicit communicative goals and provenance.

Future branches are conditional and never committed as executable responses.

Trajectory planning integrates with Phase 36's existing performance state.

Predicted branches use Phase 41 and remain distinct from observed user responses.

Every planned target passes identity, voice-policy, and provider-capability validation.

Only the live current turn can cross the commitment boundary.

User interruption connects to the actual cancellation and turn-taking systems.

Topic changes, corrections, new information, and state shifts trigger appropriate replanning.

Unchanged branches are preserved only when their dependencies still hold.

Plan replacement and branch invalidation use atomic persistence and version checks.

Tests cover interruption races, stale context, invalid branches, low-confidence horizons, and failed validators.
Status: Phase 43's core data models, planning interfaces, commitment gate, interruption handler, and controller scaffold are defined. The horizon resolver, plan builder, trajectory integration, outcome predictor, actual validators, cancellation adapter, and transactional repository still require connection and testing against Aariya's codebase. No live multi-turn plan was executed in this conversation.

Phase 44 — Autonomous Voice Social Dynamics Modeling
Phase 43 plans how Aariya's delivery may evolve across several turns. Phase 44 adds the evolving social context of the conversation: formality, familiarity, rapport, interaction tension, cooperation, playfulness, conversational distance, and responsiveness.

Your TTS notes make an important distinction: observable interaction patterns are not proof of someone's internal feelings. Aariya should infer only what the available evidence supports, preserve uncertainty, and adapt expression without changing her core identity or assuming an increasingly intimate relationship. *voice TTS.txt

44.1 Architecture
Real conversational observations

Register · corrections · cooperation · turn-taking · repair attempts · response patterns

Social inference + per-dimension confidence

Three separate timelines

Current session · recent interactions · long-term profile

Boundary-aware social adaptation

Identity · personality · social boundaries · current context

Phase 42 meta-control → Phase 43 trajectory → Phase 37/38 voice pipeline

Real user response → update state and evaluate outcomes


Phase 39 and Phase 44 should remain separate inputs: Phase 39 models conversational rhythm; Phase 44 models observable social interaction dynamics. Both can influence delivery, but they must not be confused with one another. *voice TTS.txt

44.2 Create the module structure
Add the package specified by your source:
backend/app/voice/social/
├── __init__.py
├── observation.py
├── signals.py
├── dimensions.py
├── state.py
├── transition.py
├── profile.py
├── relationship.py
├── context.py
├── inference.py
├── confidence.py
├── boundaries.py
├── adaptation.py
├── repository.py
└── controller.py


The code below separates state representation, inference, adaptation, boundary checks, and persistence. The actual inference and database integrations remain explicit dependencies.
44.3 Define the observable social signals
signals.py

These signals should be extracted from actual conversations. Aariya should not infer a social state from an arbitrary numerical value or from a made-up example.
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Mapping@dataclass(frozen=True)class SocialSignalSet:    # Each measured signal is normalized by its actual extractor.    values: Mapping[str, float | None]    # Each signal maps to references for the evidence supporting it.    evidence_references: Mapping[str, tuple[str, ...]]    extractor_version: str    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.extractor_version.strip():            raise ValueError("extractor_version is required")        for name, value in self.values.items():            if not name.strip():                raise ValueError("Signal name is required")            if value is None:                continue            if (                not isfinite(value)                or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0            ):                raise ValueError(                    f"Signal '{name}' must be in [0, 1]"                )



Possible signal families from your notes include conversational cooperation, interruptions, reciprocal engagement, conversation initiative, formality/register, playfulness, repair frequency, and response alignment. Their exact meanings and extraction methods must be defined by the real signal-analysis components. *voice TTS.txt

44.4 Define the social dimensions
dimensions.py
from enum import Enumclass SocialDimension(str, Enum):    FAMILIARITY = "familiarity"    FORMALITY = "formality"    RAPPORT = "rapport"    TENSION = "tension"    COOPERATION = "cooperation"    PLAYFULNESS = "playfulness"    CONVERSATIONAL_DISTANCE = "conversational_distance"    RESPONSIVENESS = "responsiveness"



These are continuous dimensions rather than rigid relationship categories. For example, the system can infer a lower preference for formality without deciding that a person is a “close friend.” *voice TTS.txt

44.6 Track social transitions

from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom .state import SocialState@dataclass(frozen=True)class SocialTransition:    previous: SocialState | None    current: SocialState    changed_dimensions: tuple[str, ...]    reason: str    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.reason.strip():            raise ValueError("Transition reason is required")    @classmethod    def create(        cls,        *,        previous: SocialState | None,        current: SocialState,        reason: str,    ) -> "SocialTransition":        if previous is None:            changed = tuple(                sorted(d.value for d in current.values)            )        else:            dimensions = set(previous.values) | set(current.values)            changed = tuple(                sorted(                    dimension.value



This records what changed; it does not itself smooth or decay the values. Any smoothing, temporal stability, and decay behavior must use a separately defined policy calibrated against actual interaction history. Your notes explicitly warn against treating arbitrary decay constants as learned facts. *voice TTS.txt

44.10 Gate low-confidence social estimates
confidence.py

Configure the confidence threshold through your validated policy. If no social dimensions survive the gate, Aariya should make no social adaptation based on that state; the existing voice strategy can continue unchanged.
44.11 Define explicit social boundaries
Your source calls out expressiveness, playfulness, intimacy, and formality as dimensions that may need protected boundaries. It requires the final decision to pass identity, personality, social-boundary, and contextual checks. *voice TTS.txt


boundaries.py
from __future__ import annotationsfrom dataclasses import dataclassfrom math import isfinitefrom typing import Any, Mapping, Protocol@dataclass(frozen=True)class SocialBoundary:    dimension: str    minimum: float | None    maximum: float | None    reason: str    def __post_init__(self) -> None:        if not self.dimension.strip():            raise ValueError("dimension is required")        if not self.reason.strip():            raise ValueError("reason is required")        for name in ("minimum", "maximum"):            value = getattr(self, name)            if value is not None:                if (                    not isfinite(value)                    or not 0.0 <= value <= 1.0                ):                    raise ValueError(                        f"{name} must be in [0, 1]"                    )        if (            self.minimum is not None            and self.maximum is not None            and self.minimum > self.maximum



This local layer checks explicit numerical bounds. The injected validator must still connect to Aariya's real identity and personality constraints; a boundary check on expression values alone is not a complete identity validation.
44.12 Apply social adaptation conservatively
adaptation.py

Social dynamics should influence expression, not overwrite Aariya's identity. A positive rapport estimate does not automatically warrant increased intimacy, playfulness, or exaggerated emotional behavior. Your source also explicitly allows a “do nothing” outcome. *voice TTS.txt

The engine that proposes the change is still an integration interface. The boundaries, context, and actual voice-performance constraints must be connected before a proposed target reaches Phase 36/37.

44.14 Integrate the social dynamics controller
controller.py

This controller follows the sequence in your notes: infer social state from real signals and history, persist the state, construct the relationship context, and pass the proposed delivery through the boundary-aware adaptation path. *voice TTS.txt

The actual SocialObservation must be constructed from your measured interaction signals; its schema must include the user_id, session_id, context_reference, signal set, timestamp, and authorization/evidence metadata specified by the project's runtime contract.

If inference fails, persistence fails, or boundary validation does not succeed, the caller must retain the existing validated performance target rather than treating the social adaptation as successful.
