44.16 Important integration rules
The completed system should preserve four separations:
Concern	Required behavior
Current vs. long-term social state	A temporary disagreement or unusually informal conversation must not permanently rewrite the profile.
Social dynamics vs. user rhythm	Social interaction signals and speaking rhythm remain independent inputs.
Social state vs. Aariya's identity	Social context affects current expression, not core personality or vocal identity.
Prediction vs. observed fact	Inferences retain confidence and evidence; unknown values remain unknown.

These rules follow the source's requirements for evidence-backed social state, calibrated decay, session/recent/long-term separation, and boundary protection. *voice TTS.txt

44.17 Completion checklist
Phase 44 readiness
0 / 11

[ ] Social signals come from actual conversation-analysis events.
[ ] Each inferred dimension has a value, confidence, evidence, timestamp, and context.
[ ] Current session state remains separate from the long-term profile.
[ ] Low-confidence dimensions are omitted from adaptation.
[ ] Social inference does not assume that informal language proves intimacy or friendship.
[ ] Temporal smoothing and tension decay use validated configuration rather than arbitrary constants.
[ ] Adaptation is checked against explicit expression, identity, personality, and context constraints.
[ ] The existing voice strategy and TTS systems remain unchanged.
[ ] Social state and adaptation decisions are persisted with appropriate provenance and privacy controls.
[ ] Real post-response outcomes can update social inference through the existing feedback system.
[ ] Integration tests cover low-confidence evidence, corrections, successful repair, topic change, and boundary rejection.


Phase 44 gave Aariya social awareness in her voice behavior. Phase 45 protects Aariya’s recognizable vocal identity while her expression and behavior adapt. 
*voice TTS.txt

The central rule is:

Aariya can change how she expresses herself without losing the vocal characteristics that make her sound like Aariya.

This protection must hold across emotional changes, user-rhythm adaptation, social adaptation, policy updates, experiments, TTS-provider changes, calibration drift, prosodic variation, and long conversations. 
*voice TTS.txt

1. What Phase 45 adds
Real generated audio

Observe the actual TTS output, not assumed output characteristics.

Identity measurement

Pitch characteristics, timing, articulation, spectral features, expressive range and stability.

Validated identity baseline

Compare measurements with Aariya’s approved voice configuration and acoustic reference.

Within envelope

Allow normal expression and adaptation.

Drift or uncertainty

Investigate, constrain, reject or recover through validated controls.

The design follows your TTS notes’ Phase 45 architecture: measure real output, compare it with the intended identity, attribute drift, apply the smallest effective correction, and revalidate. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Record measurements from actual Aariya audio
observation.py
An observation records which generated audio was measured and which feature-extractor version produced the measurements. Store a reference to the audio, rather than copying the complete audio into each record.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from math import isfinite
from typing import Mapping
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IdentityFeatureSet:
    values: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]
    extractor_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.extractor_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Extractor version is required")

        if not self.values:
            raise ValueError("Identity features are required")

        for name, vector in self.values.items():
            if not name.strip() or not vector:
                raise ValueError("Each feature needs a name and values")

            if not all(isfinite(value) for value in vector):
                raise ValueError(f"Non-finite identity feature: {name}")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IdentityObservation:
    id: UUID
    session_id: UUID
    turn_id: UUID
    audio_reference: str
    extracted_features: IdentityFeatureSet

These records must come from real TTS output. A reference to a file is not proof that the file exists or that the features are valid; the audio storage and extractor integrations must verify those conditions.

feature_extractor.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

from .observation import IdentityFeatureSet


class IdentityFeatureExtractor(Protocol):
    async def extract(
        self,
        audio_reference: str,
    ) -> IdentityFeatureSet:
        """
        Analyze actual generated audio.

        Reuse Phase 8's established analysis pipeline where possible.
        Return measured features and the real extractor version.
        """
        ...

Integration requirement: connect this contract to the actual audio analysis pipeline. Do not populate feature vectors with invented values or assume every TTS provider produces the same acoustic characteristics.

4. Establish Aariya’s validated identity baseline
A generic profile such as "female_voice_baseline" is not sufficient. The baseline should represent the particular voice configuration validated for Aariya.

representation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping
from uuid import UUID


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceIdentityRepresentation:
    features: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]
    model_version: str
    source_observation_ids: tuple[UUID, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.model_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Identity model version is required")

        if len(set(self.source_observation_ids)) < 2:
            raise ValueError(
                "Baseline requires multiple distinct observations"
            )

        if not self.features:
            raise ValueError("Baseline features cannot be empty")

The baseline must be constructed from multiple valid observations of Aariya’s actual output. Its creation or replacement must use the baseline-building process and validation evidence, not simply promote the most recent utterance. 
*voice TTS.txt

The repository is an interface, not a database implementation. Connect it to Aariya’s real persistence system, and only activate baselines that have passed the required validation process.

5. Compare identity and detect drift
Aariya must not treat every change in pitch movement or timing as an identity failure. Expressive changes can be acceptable, while a change in one particular dimension can exceed the validated envelope.

The solution needs two separate operations:

Calculate an interpretable distance from the baseline.

Determine whether that distance exceeds limits calibrated for the active configuration and context.

distance.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol

from .baseline import IdentityBaseline
from .observation import IdentityObservation


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IdentityDistanceResult:
    overall_distance: float
    dimension_distances: Mapping[str, float]
    confidence: float
    metric_version: str


class IdentityDistanceCalculator(Protocol):
    async def compare(
        self,
        baseline: IdentityBaseline,
        observation: IdentityObservation,
    ) -> IdentityDistanceResult:
        ...

The actual distance algorithm must be connected to the chosen acoustic representation. It should report per-dimension measurements, not just one unexplained score.

threshold.py
The source notes explicitly reject an arbitrary global threshold. Limits must take account of the voice dimension, measurement uncertainty, calibration confidence, provider and configuration, expected expressive range, and context. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol

from .representation import VoiceIdentityRepresentation


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IdentityThresholdPolicy:
    overall_max_distance: float
    dimension_max_distances: Mapping[str, float]
    minimum_confidence: float
    calibration_version: str
    configuration_revision: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.overall_max_distance < 0:
            raise ValueError("Overall distance limit cannot be negative")

        if not 0.0 <= self.minimum_confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Confidence must be between 0 and 1")

        if any(v < 0 for v in self.dimension_max_distances.values()):
            raise ValueError("Dimension limits cannot be negative")

        if not self.dimension_max_distances:
            raise ValueError("Dimension limits are required")


class IdentityThresholdResolver(Protocol):
    async def resolve(
        self,
        context: object,
        identity_model: VoiceIdentityRepresentation,
    ) -> IdentityThresholdPolicy:

The resolver must be implemented against Aariya’s actual calibration and policy data. The dataclass validates supplied limits; it does not magically calibrate them.

drift.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum

from .distance import IdentityDistanceResult
from .threshold import IdentityThresholdPolicy


class IdentityStatus(str, Enum):
    CLEAR = "clear"
    DRIFT = "drift"
    INDETERMINATE = "indeterminate"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IdentityDriftResult:
    status: IdentityStatus
    overall_distance: float
    dimensions: dict[str, float]
    confidence: float
    reasons: tuple[str, ...]

    @property
    def drift_detected(self) -> bool:
        return self.status == IdentityStatus.DRIFT


class IdentityDriftDetector:
    def evaluate(
        self,
        distance: IdentityDistanceResult,
        policy: IdentityThresholdPolicy,
    ) -> IdentityDriftResult:
        reasons: list[str] = []


This distinguishes three outcomes:

CLEAR: the measurement is sufficiently reliable and within the supplied envelope.

DRIFT: one or more validated limits have been exceeded.

INDETERMINATE: the system lacks enough reliable information to make the identity decision.

That distinction matters. Uncertainty is not evidence that identity is stable.

6. Distinguish temporary variation from persistent drift
The notes require analysis over multiple timescales: a single utterance, one session, multiple sessions, and long-term voice history. One unusual utterance must not redefine Aariya’s identity. 
*voice TTS.txt

The temporal analyzer is deliberately an injected component. Its actual implementation must account for the acoustic representation, measurement uncertainty, timestamps, and applicable calibration. There is no fabricated trend model here.

7. Attribute the cause before correcting anything
The source identifies these possible causes of drift:

Voice policy change

User or social adaptation

Prosodic composition

TTS provider or voice configuration change

Calibration drift

Audio processing or streaming-path changes

This is the connection to Phase 29 diagnosis and Phase 38 calibration. A provider change should not automatically be treated as a personality change. 
*voice TTS.txt

8. Apply the smallest effective correction
The Phase 45 notes prescribe a correction order ranging from temporary runtime constraints to reverting adaptation, reverting a policy candidate, recalibration, policy rollback, and provider investigation. The principle is to use the smallest effective correction first. 
*voice TTS.txt

        This method proposes a change; it does not activate it.

        ...

For example, if real measurements show excessive pitch variability, the correction may constrain pitch activity rather than replace the entire voice configuration.

The proposal should not silently modify production state. Apply it only through the relevant authorization, validation, and deployment mechanisms.

rollback.py
from __future__ import annotations

        self._executor = executor

    async def rollback(
        self,
        previous_valid_policy: object | None,
        authorization: object,
    ) -> object:
        if previous_valid_policy is None:
            raise ValueError("No validated rollback target exists")

        if authorization is None:
            raise PermissionError("Rollback authorization is required")

        return await self._executor.rollback_to(

The rollback controller is an integration boundary to the actual governed policy system. A real authorization object and a previously validated rollback target must be supplied; this scaffold does not implement either system.

controller.py
from __future__ import annotations

from .observation import IdentityObservation
from .repository import IdentityRepository
from .feature_extractor import IdentityFeatureExtractor
from .validator import IdentityValidator


class IdentityGuardController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        extractor: IdentityFeatureExtractor,
        repository: IdentityRepository,
        validator: IdentityValidator,
    ) -> None:
        self._extractor = extractor
        self._repository = repository
        self._validator = validator

    async def inspect(
        self,
        audio_reference: str,
        session_id,
        turn_id,
        voice_configuration: str,
        context: object,
    ):
        # Use actual generated audio.
        features = await self._extractor.extract(audio_reference)

        observation = IdentityObservation.create(
            session_id=session_id,
            turn_id=turn_id,
            audio_reference=audio_reference,
            features=features,
        )

This connects actual output, a baseline, calibrated threshold resolution, drift detection, and persistence. It still needs the real extractor, distance algorithm, threshold resolver, storage backend, and runtime context.

11. Integrate Phase 45 with the rest of Aariya
Phase 45 should not operate as an isolated score checker. It must protect the decisions made by existing voice subsystems.

Existing phase

Phase 45’s role

Phase 8 — Quality analysis

Reuse actual acoustic measurements

Phase 29 — Diagnosis

Attribute drift to probable causes

Phase 33 — Controlled experimentation

Prefer targeted corrections and controlled tests

Phase 34 — Canarying

Check real output before continuing rollout

Phase 35 — Policy synthesis

Reject candidates that violate the validated identity envelope

Phase 38 — Calibration

Use real calibration and provider-configuration evidence

Phase 39 — User-aware adaptation

Allow appropriate rhythm changes without copying the user’s vocal identity

Phase 41 — Prediction

Supply identity risk to candidate selection

Phase 42 — Meta-control

Make identity protection a hard constraint, not a preference

Phase 44 — Social dynamics

Change warmth, formality or restraint without transforming vocal identity

These connections follow the Phase 45 integration requirements in the source notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

Two operational distinctions are important:

Monitoring after TTS: analyze actual output asynchronously when possible, so identity analysis does not automatically add latency to playback.

Candidate promotion: identity validation must be an approval gate. A candidate with an unverified identity result must not be promoted as though it passed.

12. Required validation scenarios
Before considering Phase 45 operational, validate it using real recorded output and controlled test execution:

Scenario

Expected behavior

Normal expressive variation within calibrated limits

Pass

Pitch variation exceeds the applicable validated limit

Report drift

Measurement confidence is insufficient

Indeterminate; not an automatic pass

A new TTS provider changes spectral or timing behavior

Detect the difference and investigate attribution

User-rhythm adaptation increases speaking tempo

Permit only if identity checks remain within the validated envelope

Social adaptation changes warmth or restraint

Preserve the underlying vocal identity

A proposed policy introduces unacceptable drift

Block promotion and preserve the previous validated policy

One utterance is an outlier while session history is stable

Record the observation; use temporal evidence rather than redefining the baseline

No active validated baseline exists

Report unverified and do not approve promotion

Corrective change is applied

Measure the resulting real output again

The source also calls for human validation when changes meaningfully affect identity. Objective similarity alone is insufficient: evaluators should assess whether the output still sounds like the same Aariya. 
*voice TTS.txt


Phase 46 — Autonomous Voice Development & Capability Evolution
Phase 45 protects Aariya’s vocal identity. Phase 46 enables Aariya to identify missing voice capabilities, investigate real solutions, test compatible candidates, and introduce validated improvements without destabilizing her existing system. 
*voice TTS.txt

This phase is about controlled capability evolution, not unrestricted self-modifying code.

For example, suppose Aariya’s real performance measurements show that her TTS pipeline cannot provide sufficiently precise phoneme timing. She should identify that gap from evidence, research possible solutions, test a candidate against the real voice pipeline, measure the results, and seek approval before production activation.

The lifecycle is:

1. Capability audit

Discover actual installed features and their measured limitations.

2. Gap identification and prioritization

Connect requirements to evidence, then decide what matters most.

3. Research and compatibility

Investigate real candidates and check architectural fit.

4. Isolated integration and calibration

Use real provider behavior, real audio, and actual measurements.

5. Experiment and canary

Validate functionality, quality, latency, reliability, and identity.

Validated

Promote through the approved deployment gate.

Failed

Reject, preserve evidence, or roll back.

This follows the capability-development and production-gate structure in your source notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

Each module has a distinct responsibility. The inventory describes what actually exists; assessment determines how well it works; the research and compatibility layers find possible improvements; and lifecycle management controls whether a capability is allowed to progress.

2. Define what a capability actually is
capability.py
A capability is a meaningful function of the voice system, not merely a parameter.

For example, pitch activity is a parameter; continuous pitch-contour control is a capability.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


class CapabilityStatus(str, Enum):
    AVAILABLE = "available"
    LIMITED = "limited"
    DEGRADED = "degraded"
    UNAVAILABLE = "unavailable"
    EXPERIMENTAL = "experimental"
    RETIRED = "retired"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceCapability:
    id: UUID
    name: str
    description: str
    status: CapabilityStatus
    version: str
    provenance: tuple[UUID, ...]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        name: str,
        description: str,
        status: CapabilityStatus,
        version: str,
        provenance: tuple[UUID, ...] = (),
    ) -> "VoiceCapability":
        if not name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Capability name is required")

The status must be derived from actual discovery and calibration rather than guessed. A provider advertising a feature does not prove that Aariya can use that feature reliably in her configuration. 
*voice TTS.txt

This is deliberately an interface. The concrete inventory provider must inspect Aariya’s installed adapters, provider capabilities, runtime behavior, and existing validated data.

It must not silently populate values such as streaming = available or phoneme_timing = limited without evidence from the actual environment.

4. Identify capability gaps from evidence
The next three models turn requirements and observed limitations into explicit, traceable work items.

requirement.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityRequirement:
    capability_name: str
    reason: str
    importance: float
    current_limitation: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.capability_name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Capability name is required")
        if not self.reason.strip():
            raise ValueError("A reason is required")
        if not self.current_limitation.strip():
            raise ValueError("Current limitation is required")
        if not isfinite(self.importance):
            raise ValueError("Importance must be finite")

The importance value should come from an established prioritization scale and real evidence, not an arbitrary value produced by the controller.

gap.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite
from uuid import UUID

from .requirement import CapabilityRequirement


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityGap:
    requirement: CapabilityRequirement
    evidence_ids: tuple[UUID, ...]
    confidence: float

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.evidence_ids:
            raise ValueError(
                "A capability gap requires supporting evidence"
            )
        if not isfinite(self.confidence):
            raise ValueError("Gap confidence must be finite")
        if not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Confidence must be between 0 and 1")

This prevents a missing capability from being declared solely because an agent thinks a new feature would be interesting.

assessment.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityAssessment:
    capability_name: str
    functional_quality: float
    reliability: float
    latency: float
    identity_impact: float
    confidence: float
    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        measurements = (
            self.functional_quality,
            self.reliability,
            self.latency,
            self.identity_impact,
            self.confidence,
        )

        if not all(isfinite(value) for value in measurements):
            raise ValueError("Assessment contains invalid measurements")

        if not self.evidence_ids:
            raise ValueError(
                "An assessment requires measurement evidence"
            )

The measurement scales for quality, latency, and identity impact must be defined by their respective measurement systems. This model does not pretend those different quantities are interchangeable.

The source explicitly requires evaluating availability alongside functional quality, reliability, latency, identity impact, confidence, and measured outcome. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

5. Represent competing solutions as candidates
candidate.py
One gap can have several possible solutions: a provider feature, an updated provider version, a local processing layer, or a research-backed algorithmic component.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityCandidate:
    id: UUID
    name: str
    capability: str
    source: str
    integration_requirements: dict[str, Any]
    metadata: dict[str, Any]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        name: str,
        capability: str,
        source: str,
        integration_requirements: dict[str, Any],
        metadata: dict[str, Any],
    ) -> "CapabilityCandidate":
        if not name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Candidate name is required")
        if not capability.strip():
            raise ValueError("Target capability is required")
        if not source.strip():
            raise ValueError("Candidate provenance is required")

        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            name=name,
            capability=capability,

Discovery does not confer trust. Every candidate still needs provenance, compatibility checks, isolated testing, calibration, validation, and a controlled promotion decision. 
*voice TTS.txt

6. Check compatibility before integrating anything
compatibility.py
A new capability must be checked against Aariya’s actual architecture, provider adapter, audio formats, streaming behavior, cancellation, synchronization, identity model, latency budget, and security boundaries.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .candidate import CapabilityCandidate


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CompatibilityReport:
    compatible: bool
    blockers: tuple[str, ...]
    warnings: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]


class CompatibilityAnalyzer(Protocol):
    async def analyze(
        self,
        candidate: CapabilityCandidate,
        architecture: object,
    ) -> CompatibilityReport:
        """
        Evaluate the candidate against the actual Aariya architecture.
        """
        ...

A compatible=True result should only be issued by a real analyzer backed by appropriate checks. The protocol alone does not inspect any library, provider, dependency, or security configuration.

7. Connect research and integration planning
research.py
Phase 30 supplies the research and external evidence that Phase 46 needs to evaluate candidates.

        ...

Do not generate fictional provider documentation or claim a capability exists without evidence.

integration.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class IntegrationPlan:
    capability_name: str
    components: tuple[str, ...]
    migration_strategy: str
    rollback_strategy: str
    validation_requirements: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:

        if not self.components:
            raise ValueError("Affected components must be specified")
        if not self.migration_strategy.strip():

        if not self.rollback_strategy.strip():
            raise ValueError("Rollback strategy is required")
        if not self.validation_requirements:
            raise ValueError("Validation requirements are required")

8. Enforce the capability lifecycle
lifecycle.py
The source defines these lifecycle states: DISCOVERED, ASSESSED, RESEARCHED, CANDIDATE, INTEGRATED, CALIBRATED, EXPERIMENTAL, VALIDATED, ACTIVE, REJECTED, and RETIRED. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import Enum


class CapabilityLifecycle(str, Enum):
    DISCOVERED = "discovered"
    ASSESSED = "assessed"
    RESEARCHED = "researched"
    CANDIDATE = "candidate"
    INTEGRATED = "integrated"
    CALIBRATED = "calibrated"
    EXPERIMENTAL = "experimental"
    VALIDATED = "validated"
    ACTIVE = "active"
    REJECTED = "rejected"
    RETIRED = "retired"


_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS: dict[
    CapabilityLifecycle, frozenset[CapabilityLifecycle]
] = {
    CapabilityLifecycle.DISCOVERED: frozenset({
        CapabilityLifecycle.ASSESSED,
        CapabilityLifecycle.REJECTED,
    }),
    CapabilityLifecycle.ASSESSED: frozenset({
        CapabilityLifecycle.RESEARCHED,
        CapabilityLifecycle.REJECTED,
    }),
    CapabilityLifecycle.RESEARCHED: frozenset({
        CapabilityLifecycle.CANDIDATE,
        CapabilityLifecycle.REJECTED,
    }),
    CapabilityLifecycle.CANDIDATE: frozenset({
        CapabilityLifecycle.INTEGRATED,

This transition map is an implementation-level enforcement of the lifecycle. It is not explicitly supplied as executable code in the notes. In particular, rollback to a previous version should be handled by version management and the deployment system rather than by pretending an already retired record can simply become active again.

The important rule is that a candidate must not skip from DISCOVERED straight to ACTIVE.

9. Calibrate and validate the capability
calibration.py
Calibration connects Phase 46 to Phase 38. New features must be grounded against actual generated audio and measurements from the real voice pipeline. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

class CapabilityCalibration(Protocol):
    async def calibrate(
        self,
        capability: object,
        real_voice: object,
        measurement_engine: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Run calibration using actual voice output and measurements.
        """
        ...

For example, a new word-level emphasis capability should be evaluated by comparing requested emphasis with actual measured prominence—not merely by checking that the provider accepts an emphasis parameter.

validation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CapabilityValidationReport:
    functional_tests_passed: bool
    reliability_acceptable: bool
    latency_acceptable: bool
    identity_preserved: bool
    intended_outcome_improved: bool
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    @property
    def approved(self) -> bool:
        return (
            self.functional_tests_passed
            and self.reliability_acceptable
            and self.latency_acceptable
            and self.identity_preserved
            and self.intended_outcome_improved
            and bool(self.evidence_references)
        )


class CapabilityValidator(Protocol):
    async def validate(
        self,
        capability: object,
        calibration: object,
        experiments: object,
        identity_results: object,
        quality_results: object,
    ) -> CapabilityValidationReport:

A candidate is not production-ready merely because its feature works in one test. It must be reliable, acceptable for latency, safe for Aariya’s identity, and improve the intended outcome using real validation evidence. 
*voice TTS.txt

10. Preserve capability provenance and version history

12. Connect the capability evolution controller
The controller coordinates the interfaces; it should not itself invent capabilities, fabricate measurements, or directly rewrite production source code.

controller.py
from __future__ import annotations

from .capability import VoiceCapability
from .gap import CapabilityGap


class CapabilityEvolutionController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        inventory,
        assessor,
        prioritizer,
        researcher,
        compatibility,

        self._inventory = inventory
        self._assessor = assessor
        self._prioritizer = prioritizer
        self._researcher = researcher
        self._compatibility = compatibility
        self._sandbox = sandbox
        self._calibration = calibration
        self._validator = validator
        self._repository = repository

    async def identify_gaps(
        self,
        requirements,
    ):
        actual_inventory = await self._inventory.get()

        gaps = await self._assessor.find_gaps(

This is a coordinator scaffold based on the source’s main controller. The assessor, prioritizer, sandbox provider, research engine, calibration engine, validator, and persistence backend must be actual implementations. 
*voice TTS.txt

Important: the controller above deliberately does not automatically activate a capability. Its validation result must be passed to the established canary and deployment-governance system. Only that system should authorize production promotion, and a failed candidate should not be allowed to bypass the normal rejection or rollback process.

13. How Aariya should decide what to improve
The source describes several candidate improvements that could emerge from measured limitations: finer prosodic control, phoneme/viseme alignment, native breath behavior, better streaming, and faster interruption recovery. These are examples of potential gaps, not claims that Aariya currently lacks all of them. 
*voice TTS.txt

Prioritization should consider:

Severity of the actual failure and expected benefit.

Integration complexity, maintenance burden, and research confidence.

Latency, reliability, privacy, cost, and availability.

Impact on Aariya’s validated vocal identity.

For example, a capability that greatly improves expressive range but creates unacceptable latency may be a worse choice than a modest improvement that preserves quality, reliability, and identity. This decision connects to Phase 42’s meta-control logic rather than relying on a simplistic feature-count score. 
*voice TTS.txt

14. Final promotion requirements
Before a new capability becomes active, its complete path should be:

Gate

Required evidence

Discovery

Actual capability or gap evidence

Research

Real sources and technical provenance

Compatibility

Evaluation against Aariya’s architecture

Integration

Isolated installation using relevant real interfaces

Calibration

Actual voice measurements

Experiment

Measured functional and quality results

Identity validation

Phase 45 confirms the identity envelope is preserved

Latency validation

Actual performance meets the established requirements

Canary

Controlled release and monitoring pass

Promotion

Existing deployment governance authorizes activation

A failure at any required gate must block promotion. Capabilities that are no longer useful may be retired, but their evidence and history must remain available. 
*voice TTS.txt

Phase 47 — Autonomous Voice Architecture Optimization
Phase 46 taught Aariya how to identify and integrate missing voice capabilities. Phase 47 lets Aariya investigate whether the architecture connecting those capabilities is itself inefficient, redundant, unstable, or unnecessarily coupled. 
*voice TTS.txt

The difference is important. Phase 46 asks, “What capability am I missing?” Phase 47 asks, “Is my existing system organized in a way that makes the best use of the capabilities I already have?”

For example, Aariya might discover from actual runtime traces that the emotion analyzer, user-rhythm analyzer, and energy analyzer repeatedly process the same audio. The potential improvement is a shared analysis layer—but only if real workload tests demonstrate that the change improves performance without harming quality, identity protection, or reliability.

1. The architecture-optimization lifecycle
Observe the real system

Phase 19 observability and Phase 25 performance telemetry

Reconstruct actual architecture

Components, interfaces, dependencies, traces and configuration

Analyze and diagnose

Critical-path bottlenecks, duplication, coupling, inconsistencies and resource contention

Propose an architecture candidate

Document predicted benefits, risks, affected components and rollback plan.

Isolated implementation and real validation

Use relevant real interfaces, workloads, provider behavior and measurements.

Canary and governance gate

Validate identity, quality, latency, reliability and architectural invariants.

Promote

Only through the authorized deployment process.

Reject or roll back

Preserve the previous validated architecture and evidence.

This follows Phase 47's architecture evolution loop in your TTS notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

2. Create the architecture subsystem
Add the source-defined structure:

backend/app/voice/architecture/
├── __init__.py
├── component.py
├── interface.py
├── dependency.py
├── topology.py
├── trace.py
├── observation.py

├── redundancy.py
├── coupling.py
├── consistency.py
├── resource.py
├── diagnosis.py
├── candidate.py
├── impact.py
├── sandbox.py
├── validation.py
├── migration.py
├── rollback.py
├── versioning.py
├── repository.py
└── controller.py
The architecture model must describe the actual installed system. It should not be a separately maintained diagram that gradually diverges from production. The source specifically calls for reconciling expected architecture against runtime observations and configuration drift. 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Model the real architecture
component.py
Every installed component needs an identifiable type, version, owner, and enabled state.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from uuid import UUID


class ComponentType(str, Enum):
    ANALYZER = "analyzer"
    PLANNER = "planner"
    POLICY = "policy"
    ADAPTER = "adapter"
    STREAMING = "streaming"
    SYNCHRONIZATION = "synchronization"
    STORAGE = "storage"
    MODEL = "model"
    CONTROLLER = "controller"
    INFRASTRUCTURE = "infrastructure"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceComponent:
    id: UUID
    name: str
    component_type: ComponentType
    version: str
    owner: str
    enabled: bool

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip():
            raise ValueError("Component name is required")
        if not self.version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Component version is required")
        if not self.owner.strip():
            raise ValueError("Component owner is required")

dependency.py
Represent the relationships between components explicitly.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from uuid import UUID


class DependencyType(str, Enum):
    SYNCHRONOUS = "synchronous"
    ASYNCHRONOUS = "asynchronous"
    DATA = "data"
    CONTROL = "control"
    STORAGE = "storage"
    EXTERNAL = "external"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ComponentDependency:
    source_id: UUID
    target_id: UUID
    dependency_type: DependencyType
    required: bool

These relationships help answer questions such as: Which components depend on this provider adapter? Which dependencies are on the first-audio critical path? What other components could be affected if one component fails?

topology.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from uuid import UUID

from .component import VoiceComponent
from .dependency import ComponentDependency


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceArchitectureTopology:
    components: tuple[VoiceComponent, ...]
    dependencies: tuple[ComponentDependency, ...]
    version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Architecture version is required")

        component_ids = [item.id for item in self.components]

        if len(component_ids) != len(set(component_ids)):
            raise ValueError("Component IDs must be unique")

        known_ids: set[UUID] = set(component_ids)

        for dependency in self.dependencies:
            if (
                dependency.source_id not in known_ids
                or dependency.target_id not in known_ids
            ):
                raise ValueError(
                    "Dependency references an unknown component"
                )

    def component(self, component_id: UUID) -> VoiceComponent:

This is a validated architecture snapshot. The production topology provider still needs to discover the actual components, dependency edges, versions, and configuration references. It should not assume that the topology is current merely because its schema validates.

These models implement the component, dependency, and topology concepts defined in Phase 47. 
*voice TTS.txt

4. Connect real runtime telemetry
trace.py
Phase 47 needs actual execution traces to determine which components contribute to response latency.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceTrace:
    trace_id: str
    session_id: str
    component: str
    started_at: datetime
    finished_at: datetime
    metadata: dict
    parent_trace_id: str | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.trace_id or not self.session_id:
            raise ValueError("Trace and session IDs are required")
        if not self.component.strip():
            raise ValueError("Trace component is required")

        if (
            self.started_at.tzinfo is None
            or self.finished_at.tzinfo is None
        ):
            raise ValueError("Trace timestamps must be timezone-aware")

        if self.finished_at < self.started_at:
            raise ValueError("Trace finishes before it starts")

    @property
    def duration_ms(self) -> float:
        return (
            self.finished_at - self.started_at
        ).total_seconds() * 1000

parent_trace_id is an additional implementation field to represent causal trace relationships. Real tracing data must populate that relationship consistently before the optimizer can reliably reconstruct dependencies along a response path.

observation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureObservation:
    component: str
    metric: str
    value: float
    context: dict
    evidence_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.component.strip():
            raise ValueError("Component is required")
        if not self.metric.strip():
            raise ValueError("Metric is required")
        if not isfinite(self.value):
            raise ValueError("Metric value must be finite")
        if not self.evidence_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("Evidence reference is required")

The context should retain enough information to interpret the observation correctly, such as the configuration revision, workload, provider, and collection window. Sensitive transcripts and raw audio should not be copied into telemetry unnecessarily.

Phase 47 reuses Phase 25 performance telemetry and Phase 19 observability. 
*voice TTS.txt

5. Detect genuine architectural bottlenecks
A high resource measurement is not, by itself, proof of a latency bottleneck. An expensive analyzer might execute asynchronously, outside the path that determines when the user first hears Aariya speak.

The optimizer must use trace relationships and dependency information to identify the actual critical path. Potential sources of delay include TTS startup, network calls, serialization, database operations, audio buffering, synchronization, inference, locking, and duplicated computations. 
*voice TTS.txt

bottleneck.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .topology import VoiceArchitectureTopology
from .trace import VoiceTrace


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BottleneckFinding:
    component: str
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    critical_path_relevance: str
    explanation: str


class BottleneckDetector(Protocol):
    async def detect(
        self,
        traces: Sequence[VoiceTrace],
        topology: VoiceArchitectureTopology,
    ) -> Sequence[BottleneckFinding]:
        """
        Identify bottlenecks from real traces and their relationships.

        Do not infer critical-path impact from resource consumption alone.
        """
        ...

The detector is an interface; it does not yet calculate bottlenecks. Its implementation needs correctly correlated traces, an actual dependency graph, and a measured baseline.

6. Analyze duplication, coupling, and consistency
Three types of architectural problems are particularly important in Aariya's voice pipeline:

Redundancy: multiple subsystems repeatedly calculate the same information from the same audio.

Coupling: an analyzer directly changes a provider instead of passing information through strategy and policy interfaces.

Cross-system inconsistency: systems such as VAD, ASR, and turn-taking repeatedly disagree about whether the user has finished speaking.

The source calls for separate analyzers for these problem types. 
*voice TTS.txt

redundancy.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .topology import VoiceArchitectureTopology
from .trace import VoiceTrace


class RedundancyAnalyzer(Protocol):
    async def detect(
        self,
        topology: VoiceArchitectureTopology,
        traces: Sequence[VoiceTrace],
    ) -> Sequence[object]:
        """
        Identify evidence-backed overlapping computations.
        """
        ...

coupling.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .topology import VoiceArchitectureTopology
from .trace import VoiceTrace


class CouplingAnalyzer(Protocol):
    async def analyze(
        self,
        topology: VoiceArchitectureTopology,
        runtime_traces: Sequence[VoiceTrace],
    ) -> Sequence[object]:
        ...

consistency.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .trace import VoiceTrace


class CrossSystemConsistencyAnalyzer(Protocol):
    async def analyze(
        self,
        observations: Sequence[VoiceTrace],
    ) -> Sequence[object]:
        """
        Find recurring, evidence-backed disagreement between systems.
        """
        ...

These interfaces need implementations using actual telemetry. A single disagreement between VAD and ASR should not automatically lead to an architecture refactor; the analysis must establish a meaningful recurring problem.

7. Represent diagnoses and architecture candidates
diagnosis.py
Phase 47 should reuse Phase 29's diagnosis architecture rather than create a second autonomous root-cause system. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureDiagnosis:
    problem: str
    affected_components: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    severity: str
    likely_causes: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.problem.strip():
            raise ValueError("Diagnosis problem is required")
        if not self.affected_components:
            raise ValueError("Affected components are required")
        if not self.evidence_ids:
            raise ValueError("Diagnosis evidence is required")

A diagnosis is an evidence-backed explanation of the problem, not permission to change the architecture.

candidate.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureCandidate:
    description: str
    changed_components: tuple[str, ...]
    removed_dependencies: tuple[str, ...]
    added_dependencies: tuple[str, ...]
    expected_effects: Mapping[str, str]
    evidence_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    rollback_strategy: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.description.strip():
            raise ValueError("Candidate description is required")
        if not self.changed_components:
            raise ValueError("Candidate changes must be explicit")
        if not self.evidence_ids:
            raise ValueError("Candidate needs supporting evidence")
        if not self.rollback_strategy.strip():
            raise ValueError("Rollback strategy is required")

For example, if the evidence demonstrates repeated analysis of the same audio, a candidate could propose sharing an analysis result between the emotion and rhythm subsystems. Its expected effects might include reduced duplicated work, more consistent measurements, and lower latency—but these remain hypotheses until measured.

8. Evaluate the impact before changing anything
An architectural optimization must be evaluated across multiple objectives. Lower latency does not automatically make a design better if it weakens failure isolation, reduces voice naturalness, breaks synchronization, or removes identity measurements.

Phase 47 explicitly considers latency, quality, identity, reliability, maintainability, resource utilization, security, observability, and deployment complexity. 
*voice TTS.txt

impact.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Protocol

from .candidate import ArchitectureCandidate
from .topology import VoiceArchitectureTopology


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureImpactReport:
    candidate_description: str
    predicted_effects: Mapping[str, object]
    risks: tuple[str, ...]
    unresolved_questions: tuple[str, ...]
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    @property
    def ready_for_isolated_testing(self) -> bool:
        return (
            bool(self.evidence_references)
            and not self.unresolved_questions
        )


class ArchitectureImpactAnalyzer(Protocol):
    async def analyze(
        self,
        candidate: ArchitectureCandidate,
        topology: VoiceArchitectureTopology,
        production_constraints: object,
    ) -> ArchitectureImpactReport:
        ...

ready_for_isolated_testing is only a preliminary check. It is not a promotion decision. The real impact analyzer must identify the assumptions and risks that need to be tested, including any unknown effects on existing components.

9. Define the invariants that must never silently break
Before testing a candidate, Aariya must know which behaviors the architecture is required to preserve.

The source identifies these examples:

Voice identity remains protected.

User interruption cancels active generation.

Provider failures do not corrupt session state.

Actual TTS output remains measurable.

Adaptive decisions remain attributable.

Production changes remain reversible. 
*voice TTS.txt

validation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .candidate import ArchitectureCandidate


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureValidationReport:
    functionality_passed: bool
    invariants_passed: bool
    performance_acceptable: bool
    identity_preserved: bool
    quality_acceptable: bool
    reliability_acceptable: bool
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    failures: tuple[str, ...]

    @property
    def passed(self) -> bool:
        return (
            self.functionality_passed
            and self.invariants_passed
            and self.performance_acceptable
            and self.identity_preserved
            and self.quality_acceptable
            and self.reliability_acceptable
            and bool(self.evidence_references)
            and not self.failures
        )


class ArchitectureValidator(Protocol):
    async def validate(
        self,

This validation model is intentionally fail-closed: missing evidence cannot produce a pass merely because no failure was reported. The actual validator still needs to implement functionality tests, invariant checks, performance comparisons, and the identity and quality gates.

10. Test candidates in an isolated environment
sandbox.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

from .candidate import ArchitectureCandidate


class ArchitectureSandbox(Protocol):
    async def create(
        self,
        candidate: ArchitectureCandidate,
    ) -> str:
        ...

    async def execute(
        self,
        sandbox_id: str,
        workload: object,
    ) -> object:
        ...

    async def destroy(
        self,
        sandbox_id: str,
    ) -> None:
        ...

The test environment should reproduce the relevant real interfaces, provider behavior, audio formats, synchronization, and actual workloads wherever practical and safe. A benchmark that does not represent Aariya's actual execution path is not sufficient evidence for an architectural change. 
*voice TTS.txt

A safe orchestration pattern is to clean up the sandbox even if testing fails:

async def evaluate_candidate(
    sandbox,
    candidate,
    workload,
):
    sandbox_id = await sandbox.create(candidate)

    try:
        return await sandbox.execute(
            sandbox_id,
            workload,
        )
    finally:
        await sandbox.destroy(sandbox_id)

This handles cleanup for an ordinary execution failure. The real sandbox implementation also needs isolation, resource controls, cleanup after process crashes, and a way to identify test results separately from production results.

11. Make migrations incremental and reversible
The notes prescribe an incremental migration path:

OLD COMPONENT
      ↓
NEW COMPONENT
      ↓
COMPATIBILITY LAYER
      ↓
CANARY
      ↓
MIGRATION
      ↓
REMOVE OLD PATH
Aariya should not replace the entire voice architecture because a new design appears more efficient. 
*voice TTS.txt

migration.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureMigrationPlan:
    source_version: str
    target_version: str
    compatibility_strategy: str
    canary_requirements: tuple[str, ...]
    rollback_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.source_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Source version is required")
        if not self.target_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Target version is required")
        if not self.compatibility_strategy.strip():
            raise ValueError("Compatibility strategy is required")
        if not self.canary_requirements:
            raise ValueError("Canary requirements are required")
        if not self.rollback_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Rollback version is required")

rollback.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class ArchitectureRollbackExecutor(Protocol):
    async def restore(
        self,
        architecture_version: str,
        authorization: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Restore a previously validated architecture snapshot.
        """
        ...


class ArchitectureRollback:
    def __init__(
        self,
        executor: ArchitectureRollbackExecutor,
    ) -> None:
        self._executor = executor

    async def restore(
        self,
        architecture_version: str,
        authorization: object,
    ) -> object:
        if not architecture_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Rollback version is required")

        if authorization is None:
            raise PermissionError("Rollback authorization is required")

        return await self._executor.restore(

Rollback must restore a real, previously validated architecture snapshot, not just switch a version string. The executor must restore the necessary component graph, configuration references, compatible capability versions, policies, and provider configuration.

12. Version the whole architecture
versioning.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ArchitectureVersion:
    version: str
    component_graph_reference: str
    dependency_graph_reference: str
    configuration_reference: str
    capability_versions: tuple[str, ...]
    policy_versions: tuple[str, ...]
    provider_configuration_reference: str
    validation_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        required = (
            self.version,
            self.component_graph_reference,
            self.dependency_graph_reference,
            self.configuration_reference,
            self.provider_configuration_reference,
            self.validation_reference,
        )

        if not all(value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError("Architecture snapshot is incomplete")

Every promoted version must retain references to the components, dependencies, configurations, capability versions, policies, provider settings, and validation evidence that define it. The source requires a complete architecture snapshot so that a previous validated version can be restored. 
*voice TTS.txt

13. Coordinate the analysis in one controller
controller.py
The controller reuses dedicated analysis services rather than embedding separate versions of bottleneck detection, diagnosis, or validation inside the optimizer.

from __future__ import annotations


class VoiceArchitectureOptimizer:
    def __init__(
        self,
        topology,
        trace_analyzer,
        bottleneck_detector,
        redundancy_analyzer,
        coupling_analyzer,
        consistency_analyzer,
        diagnosis,
        candidate_generator,
        impact_analyzer,
        sandbox,
        validator,

        self._topology = topology
        self._traces = trace_analyzer
        self._bottlenecks = bottleneck_detector
        self._redundancy = redundancy_analyzer
        self._coupling = coupling_analyzer
        self._consistency = consistency_analyzer
        self._diagnosis = diagnosis
        self._candidates = candidate_generator
        self._impact = impact_analyzer
        self._sandbox = sandbox
        self._validator = validator
        self._repository = repository

    async def analyze(self, runtime_traces):
        topology = await self._topology.current()

        bottlenecks = await self._bottlenecks.detect(

The topology, analysis services, Phase 29 integration, candidate generator, impact analyzer, and repository are interfaces to connect—not implementations supplied by this example. Before the full validation and canary flow is complete, this controller should be treated as an analysis and proposal coordinator, not an autonomous production change system.

The source's core controller follows this same structure: obtain the topology, analyze bottlenecks, redundancy, coupling, and consistency, and return structured findings.

15. How to know Phase 47 is working
The optimizer should eventually demonstrate, through real workload evidence, that it can:

Reconstruct the actual component and dependency topology.

Identify genuine critical-path bottlenecks instead of merely expensive components.

Detect duplicated computation and recurring cross-system inconsistencies.

Explain the evidence behind an architectural diagnosis.

Propose a bounded, reversible change rather than an unrestricted rewrite.

Validate real latency, quality, identity, synchronization, and failure behavior before promotion.

Roll back a failed candidate to a complete, previously validated snapshot.

Record results in the knowledge system and update the self-model only when evidence supports the update.

The source calls this the point at which Aariya becomes self-observing at the architecture level: she has a model of what her voice system can do, what it has learned, how components relate, and where the architecture may be inefficient. 
*voice TTS.txt

Phase 48 — Autonomous Voice Reliability & Fault-Recovery Intelligence
Phase 47 gave Aariya the ability to analyze and optimize her voice architecture. Phase 48 makes that architecture resilient when its components fail.

The objective is not simply to detect errors and retry. Aariya must understand which component failed, trace how the failure affects other components, estimate the impact on the active conversation, select an available and validated recovery path, verify the result, and learn from the actual outcome. 
*voice TTS.txt

A failure in speech generation should not automatically destroy the conversation. If TTS fails but the response text remains valid, for example, Aariya may preserve that text and attempt a validated alternative path.

1. The complete recovery architecture
Live voice system

Provider errors, timeouts, audio failures, network changes, session faults

Detect and classify

Identify the affected component and preserve an evidence reference.

Propagation and impact analysis

Use Phase 47's dependency graph to determine what else is affected.

Recovery candidates

Find only recovery paths supported by the actual infrastructure.

Recovery selection and safety gate

Check capability availability, identity, quality, latency and system safety.

Execute and verify

Recover the voice or gracefully degrade the affected functionality.

Recovered

Continue, measure and record the outcome.

Not recovered

Escalate, try an authorized alternative, or end voice safely.

This follows Phase 48's core architecture and its requirement to preserve the conversation whenever the failure permits it. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

The important separation is that detection identifies a failure, diagnosis and impact analysis explain its consequences, selection chooses a response, and the executor runs only an approved recovery strategy. These responsibilities should remain separate.

3. Record failure events from the real runtime
event.py
A failure event needs to identify the exact session and component involved. Record references to relevant telemetry instead of placing sensitive audio, transcript contents, or credentials in the event.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Mapping
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceFailureEvent:
    id: UUID
    session_id: UUID
    component: str
    event_type: str
    detected_at: datetime
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    details: Mapping[str, str]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        session_id: UUID,
        component: str,
        event_type: str,
        evidence_references: tuple[str, ...],
        details: Mapping[str, str],
    ) -> "VoiceFailureEvent":
        if not component.strip():
            raise ValueError("Failure component is required")
        if not event_type.strip():
            raise ValueError("Failure type is required")

        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            session_id=session_id,
            component=component,

Connect this model to actual error events from TTS, network clients, audio decoding, session management, storage, and synchronization. Creating an event structure alone does not detect failures; the live instrumentation must emit events when they occur.

4. Classify failures without pretending to know the cause
failure.py
Your notes define the main categories as network, provider, audio, compute, storage, session, synchronization, policy, capability, and unknown. UNKNOWN is a legitimate result, not a classification error. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from math import isfinite

from .event import VoiceFailureEvent


class FailureClass(str, Enum):
    NETWORK = "network"
    PROVIDER = "provider"
    AUDIO = "audio"
    COMPUTE = "compute"
    STORAGE = "storage"
    SESSION = "session"
    SYNCHRONIZATION = "synchronization"
    POLICY = "policy"
    CAPABILITY = "capability"
    UNKNOWN = "unknown"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Failure:
    event: VoiceFailureEvent
    failure_class: FailureClass
    confidence: float

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not isfinite(self.confidence):
            raise ValueError("Failure confidence must be finite")
        if not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError(
                "Failure confidence must be between 0 and 1"
            )

classifier.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

from .event import VoiceFailureEvent
from .failure import Failure


class FailureClassifier(Protocol):
    async def classify(
        self,
        event: VoiceFailureEvent,
        context: object,
    ) -> Failure:
        """
        Classify from real runtime evidence:
        exception/error type, provider response, network status,
        latency, queue state, decoder health and session state.
        """
        ...

The classifier must use actual telemetry. If it cannot reliably identify the failure class, it should report UNKNOWN rather than invent a cause.

5. Measure severity by user impact
severity.py
from enum import Enum


class FailureSeverity(str, Enum):
    INFORMATIONAL = "informational"
    DEGRADED = "degraded"
    MAJOR = "major"
    CRITICAL = "critical"

The severity evaluator must consider the impact on the user, not just the technical error.

For example, a background quality-analysis failure might be informational or degraded, while a complete TTS failure during an active response could be major. A session that cannot be safely recovered may be critical. These are examples from the source; actual classifications need to follow Aariya's operational policy. 
*voice TTS.txt

6. Trace failure propagation through the architecture
A component failure can affect other components through their dependencies. Phase 48 should reuse Phase 47's architecture graph instead of creating a second, potentially inconsistent dependency model.

7. Predict the impact before selecting recovery
impact.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .failure import Failure


class UserImpact(str, Enum):
    NONE_OBSERVED = "none_observed"
    DELAYED_FIRST_AUDIO = "delayed_first_audio"
    PARTIAL_RESPONSE = "partial_response"
    VOICE_QUALITY_DEGRADED = "voice_quality_degraded"
    LIP_SYNC_LOST = "lip_sync_lost"
    TURN_TAKING_FAILURE = "turn_taking_failure"
    SESSION_INTERRUPTED = "session_interrupted"
    COMPLETE_VOICE_FAILURE = "complete_voice_failure"
    UNKNOWN = "unknown"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FailureImpact:
    predicted_impact: UserImpact
    confidence: float
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]


class FailureImpactPredictor(Protocol):
    async def predict(
        self,
        failure: Failure,
        affected_components: Sequence[str],
        context: object,
    ) -> FailureImpact:
        ...

The predictor should consider which components are affected, the active session state, actual provider health, and relevant history. The existence of a failure in a component does not prove that the user experiences an interruption.

The same mechanism can eventually help detect degradation before a complete outage—for example, a pattern of rising provider latency, growing queue depth, and connection errors. 
*voice TTS.txt

8. Define recoveries that Aariya can actually execute
Potential strategies in the notes include retrying a connection, switching to a validated provider, reducing optional processing, using a validated fallback TTS path, restarting an isolated component, reconnecting a session, or preserving response text and regenerating audio.

Only strategies supported by the real infrastructure should be registered. 
*voice TTS.txt

recovery.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RecoveryStrategy:
    strategy_id: str
    name: str
    description: str
    expected_effect: str
    risk: float
    required_capabilities: tuple[str, ...]
    validation_reference: str
    implementation_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        required = (
            self.strategy_id,
            self.name,
            self.description,
            self.expected_effect,
            self.validation_reference,
            self.implementation_reference,
        )

        if not all(value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError("Recovery strategy metadata is incomplete")

        if not isfinite(self.risk) or not 0.0 <= self.risk <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Recovery risk must be between 0 and 1")

The risk field is a representation of a supplied assessment, not a universal risk model. Its meaning and calibration must be defined by the actual recovery policy.

        """
        Return only recovery paths supported by the current
        infrastructure and eligible for consideration.
        """
        ...

selector.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, Sequence

from .recovery import RecoveryStrategy


class RecoverySelector(Protocol):
    async def select(
        self,
        candidates: Sequence[RecoveryStrategy],
        context: object,
    ) -> RecoveryStrategy | None:
        """
        Select using actual availability, capability evidence,
        recent attempts, urgency, latency, cost and policy.
        """
        ...

The selector must not choose a strategy just because it has the lowest predicted latency. It must consider recovery probability, conversational continuity, voice identity, quality, and system safety. 
*voice TTS.txt

A provider that is available but unable to preserve required identity or streaming behavior is not automatically an acceptable fallback.

10. Require a safety gate and verify recovery results
Recovery itself can introduce new failures. A fallback provider may change Aariya's voice identity, lose synchronization, or increase latency.

Consequently, the source requires checks of candidate capabilities, identity, and latency before selection, followed by functionality, identity, and quality validation after recovery. 
*voice TTS.txt

validation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RecoveryValidationReport:
    functionality_passed: bool
    identity_passed: bool
    quality_passed: bool
    continuity_passed: bool
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    failures: tuple[str, ...]

    @property
    def approved(self) -> bool:
        return (
            self.functionality_passed
            and self.identity_passed
            and self.quality_passed
            and self.continuity_passed
            and bool(self.evidence_references)
            and not self.failures
        )


class RecoverySafetyGate(Protocol):
    async def approve(
        self,
        strategy: object,
        failure: object,
        context: object,
    ) -> bool:
        """
        Check policy, capability availability and identity constraints

The safety gate and validator must be backed by Aariya's existing policy and identity systems. These interfaces do not themselves measure voice identity or determine audio quality.

11. Execute recovery without infinite retries
execution.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RecoveryResult:
    strategy_id: str
    recovered: bool
    response_reference: str | None
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]
    failure_reason: str | None = None


class RecoveryExecutor(Protocol):
    async def execute(
        self,
        strategy: object,
        failure_context: object,
    ) -> RecoveryResult:
        ...

The executor should be connected to the actual provider adapter, generation cancellation, playback, and session-management systems. It must not claim success merely because the provider returned a response: the result still needs validation.

escalation.py
Some failures cannot be recovered autonomously. The system needs a clear outcome for those cases rather than retrying indefinitely. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class FailureEscalator(Protocol):
    async def escalate(
        self,
        failure: object,
        attempts: tuple[object, ...],
    ) -> object:
        """
        Resolve to an appropriate supported outcome:
        safe degradation, text fallback, clean session termination,
        user retry, unhealthy-service state, or operator escalation.
        """
        ...

Retry behavior should consider failure type, recent attempts, provider health, latency, urgency, cost, and the probability of recovery. A fixed retry count should not be applied indiscriminately to every failure type.

12. Learn from actual recovery outcomes

from typing import Protocol


class RecoveryLearningEngine(Protocol):

        failure: object,
        recovery: object,
        outcome: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Evaluate the observed outcome and preserve supporting evidence.
        """
        ...

The learning engine should update recovery knowledge using genuine execution results. One successful retry does not prove that a recovery strategy is generally reliable; repeated, properly attributed observations are necessary before a stable pattern is established.

Phase 31 can retain knowledge of failure patterns, likely causes, affected components, recovery strategies, success rates, and known risks. Phase 41 can use this evidence to predict outcomes, while Phase 42 can help choose priorities during degraded operation. 
*voice TTS.txt

14. Connect the recovery controller
controller.py
The following coordinator follows the Phase 48 controller structure while adding an explicit pre-execution safety gate and recorded validation results.

from __future__ import annotations


class VoiceReliabilityController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        classifier,
        propagation,
        impact_predictor,
        fallback_resolver,
        selector,
        safety_gate,
        executor,
        validator,
        escalator,
        learning,
        repository,
    ) -> None:
        self._classifier = classifier
        self._propagation = propagation
        self._impact = impact_predictor
        self._fallbacks = fallback_resolver
        self._selector = selector
        self._safety_gate = safety_gate
        self._executor = executor
        self._validator = validator
        self._escalator = escalator
        self._learning = learning
        self._repository = repository

    async def recover(self, failure_event, context):
        failure = await self._classifier.classify(
            failure_event,
            context,
        )
        await self._repository.save_failure(failure)

Integration notes:

The context must expose the actual Phase 47 architecture graph and relevant live session information.

The classifier, propagation analyzer, impact predictor, fallback resolver, selector, safety gate, executor, validator, escalator, learning engine, and repository must all be implemented and connected.

The controller makes one selected recovery attempt per invocation. Subsequent strategies must be governed by explicit retry, circuit-breaker, or escalation policy; they must not be executed through an uncontrolled loop.

In a production implementation, the caught exception should be recorded through structured error reporting without logging secret values. Cancellation and user interruption also need their own explicit handling.

The source's controller establishes the classification-to-recovery-to-validation-to-learning flow, and its recovery state machine prevents uncontrolled retry loops. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

15. Implement the recovery state machine
Recovery should follow explicit states rather than repeatedly calling the provider until it succeeds.

NORMAL
  ↓
DEGRADED
  ↓
FAILURE DETECTED
  ↓
CLASSIFY
  ↓
ASSESS IMPACT
  ↓
RECOVERY SELECTED
  ↓
RECOVERY EXECUTED
      ↙       ↘
   SUCCESS    FAILURE
      ↓          ↓
    VERIFY    NEXT AUTHORIZED STRATEGY
      ↓          ↓
   RESUME     ESCALATE / SAFE FALLBACK
The system also needs to handle these cases:

User interruption: cancel obsolete recovery work and prioritize the new user turn.

Audio decoder failure: restore the decoder or playback path without restarting healthy TTS unnecessarily.

Lip-sync failure: continue audio if safe, while temporarily disabling optional synchronization.

Session-state corruption: invalidate stale generation and restore authoritative state before generating another response.

Provider degradation: use a circuit breaker to isolate repeated failures, then allow controlled re-entry after health checks pass.

These cases and the associated reliability metrics are described in Phase 48. 
*voice TTS.txt

16. Measure whether recovery actually improves reliability
The subsystem should track real measurements, including:

Metric

What it tells Aariya

Failure frequency

How often components fail

Time to detect

How quickly the failure is recognized

Time to recover

How long restoration takes

Successful recovery rate

How often a strategy works

Fallback rate

How often the primary path is bypassed

Escalation rate

How often autonomous recovery is insufficient

User-visible failure rate

How often failures affect the conversation

Identity regression during recovery

Whether a fallback changes Aariya's validated voice identity

Recovery-added latency

How much delay recovery introduces

Session continuation rate

Whether the conversation continues after failure

These metrics must come from actual operational observations. They should feed the reliability learning system and Phase 31's knowledge base rather than be populated with assumed success rates. 
*voice TTS.txt

17. How Phase 48 connects to other Aariya subsystems
Subsystem

Contribution

Phase 29 — Diagnosis

Explains likely causes and supports investigation

Phase 31 — Knowledge

Stores failure, recovery, and outcome evidence

Phase 41 — Prediction

Estimates likely recovery outcomes

Phase 42 — Meta-control

Prioritizes continuity, fidelity, urgency, and safety

Phase 45 — Identity Guard

Rejects recovery paths that violate the identity envelope

Phase 46 — Capability Evolution

Identifies missing recovery capabilities from repeated failures

Phase 47 — Architecture Optimization

Supplies the component topology and dependency graph

Phase 12 — Synchronization

Helps recover playback and lip-sync faults

Phases 6 and 18 — Session safeguards

Help handle stale generation and inconsistent session state

The overall loop becomes:

FAILURE
   ↓
CLASSIFY
   ↓
TRACE PROPAGATION
   ↓
PREDICT IMPACT
   ↓
SELECT VALIDATED RECOVERY
   ↓
EXECUTE
   ↓
VERIFY REAL OUTCOME
   ↓
STORE KNOWLEDGE
   ↓
IMPROVE FUTURE RECOVERY DECISIONS
This is the goal of Phase 48: the system does not merely speak when everything works; it also has a structured way to preserve the conversation when part of the voice pipeline fails. 
*voice TTS.txt


Phase 49 — Autonomous Voice Security, Privacy & Integrity Intelligence
Phase 48 made Aariya resilient when voice components fail. Phase 49 protects the voice system, its data, its credentials, its identity models, and its autonomous improvement process.

Your TTS notes identify the core problem: Aariya now handles sensitive assets such as user audio, transcripts, user-rhythm profiles, social history, voice identity representations, experiments, research evidence, voice policies, provider configurations, and telemetry. These assets must not all have the same access, retention, or protection requirements. 
*voice TTS.txt

The governing principle is:

Every sensitive operation must be authenticated, authorized, limited to its legitimate purpose, protected against tampering, and auditable.

1. Phase 49 architecture
Voice assets and runtime decisions

Audio, transcripts, profiles, identity models, policies, credentials and research

Identity and access

Authentication, authorization and permissions

Data protection

Classification, minimization and retention

Integrity and secrets

Cryptographic verification and credential isolation

Security monitoring

Anomaly detection and auditable incidents

Containment, verification and safe recovery

Deny, isolate, revoke, invalidate, roll back or escalate as appropriate.

The source defines this security layer as a runtime-wide concern, not a separate feature added after the voice pipeline is built. 
*voice TTS.txt
