3. Define every protected asset
asset.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


class VoiceAssetType(str, Enum):
    RAW_AUDIO = "raw_audio"
    PROCESSED_AUDIO = "processed_audio"
    TRANSCRIPT = "transcript"
    VOICE_PROFILE = "voice_profile"
    IDENTITY_MODEL = "identity_model"
    USER_RHYTHM_PROFILE = "user_rhythm_profile"
    SOCIAL_PROFILE = "social_profile"
    POLICY = "policy"
    EXPERIMENT = "experiment"
    RESEARCH = "research"
    TELEMETRY = "telemetry"
    PROVIDER_CONFIGURATION = "provider_configuration"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceAsset:
    id: UUID
    asset_type: VoiceAssetType
    owner_reference: str
    storage_reference: str
    classification: str
    created_at: datetime

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        asset_type: VoiceAssetType,

The asset model gives the security system a common reference for deciding who may access an object and how it should be protected. It stores a reference to the data rather than the data itself.

4. Classify data according to its actual sensitivity
classification.py
from enum import Enum


class DataClassification(str, Enum):
    PUBLIC = "public"
    INTERNAL = "internal"
    CONFIDENTIAL = "confidential"
    HIGHLY_SENSITIVE = "highly_sensitive"

The source provides these classification levels and examples: public technical documentation, internal architecture telemetry, confidential voice policies, and highly sensitive raw user audio. The application's actual security and privacy requirements must determine the final classification rules. 
*voice TTS.txt

An example classification policy could distinguish raw audio from derived acoustic metrics. Both may warrant protection, but they do not necessarily require identical retention or access rules.

5. Define permissions and security context
permission.py
from enum import Enum


class VoicePermission(str, Enum):
    READ = "read"
    WRITE = "write"
    EXECUTE = "execute"
    DELETE = "delete"
    EXPORT = "export"
    ADMINISTER = "administer"

Permissions should be granted to specific service identities or roles, not globally.

context.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from uuid import UUID

from .permission import VoicePermission


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceSecurityContext:
    principal_id: str
    permissions: frozenset[VoicePermission]
    session_id: UUID
    request_id: str
    trust_context: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.principal_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Authenticated principal is required")
        if not self.request_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Request ID is required")
        if not self.trust_context.strip():
            raise ValueError("Trust context is required")

This context should travel through security-sensitive internal operations. It should be created from the application's actual authenticated identity—not from a principal name supplied by an untrusted request.

*voice TTS.txt

For example:

Voice-analysis service
        ↓
Requests READ access
        ↓
Raw audio asset
        ↓
Authorization service
      ↙       ↘
  ALLOWED    DENIED
     ↓          ↓

A user-specific rhythm or social profile must not become globally accessible merely because another subsystem can technically retrieve it.

7. Map the voice-data flows

Use this model to represent actual data movement:

MICROPHONE
    ↓
SPEECH-TO-TEXT
    ↓
CONVERSATION PROCESSING
    ↓
VOICE PLANNING
    ↓
TTS
    ↓

The model must also capture additional paths, such as audio moving into quality analysis and persistent storage, or research material entering the knowledge system. External boundaries should be explicit.

The data-flow inventory must be derived from the real deployment. A documented edge marked encrypted=True is not evidence that transport or storage encryption is actually configured.

8. Minimize data before giving it to another component

from __future__ import annotations

    async def filter(
        self,
        asset: object,
        consumer: object,
        purpose: str,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Return only the data the consumer is authorized to receive
        for the specified purpose.
        """
        ...

For example, an avatar lip-sync subsystem may need timing and alignment information without needing the user's full social profile.

The minimizer should be integrated with authorization and the relevant data schemas. It must not merely remove a few fields from a response while leaving the receiving component with access to the underlying unrestricted storage.

9. Apply the correct retention policy

        """
        ...

Do not hardcode arbitrary retention periods into Aariya's voice logic. Raw audio, derived measurements, experiment evidence, audit records, and security incidents may have different retention requirements. The applicable policy must come from the application's actual governance configuration. 
*voice TTS.txt

The intended principle is to distinguish raw recordings from derived measurements and to avoid retaining raw audio indefinitely by default.

10. Integrate encryption and integrity protection
The source requires use of established cryptographic libraries and the application's existing key-management infrastructure. It explicitly advises against implementing cryptography from scratch. 
*voice TTS.txt

encryption.py

        self,
        data: bytes,
        context: object,
    ) -> bytes:
        ...

    async def decrypt(
        self,
        encrypted_data: bytes,
        context: object,
    ) -> bytes:
        ...

Connect these interfaces to the actual cryptographic implementation and key-management system. The context should identify the legitimate purpose and authorized identity for decryption. The interface itself does not provide encryption, key rotation, authentication, or key revocation.

integrity.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class IntegrityService(Protocol):
    async def fingerprint(
        self,
        artifact: object,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Generate a cryptographic fingerprint of the actual artifact
        using the established integrity implementation.
        """
        ...

    async def verify(
        self,
        artifact: object,
        expected_fingerprint: str,
    ) -> bool:
        ...

Protect voice policies, experiment records, capability records, research evidence, and calibration results against silent modification.

For example:

Previously validated policy
          ↓
Integrity verification
       ↙          ↘
   MATCH          MISMATCH
     ↓                ↓
Eligible for       Reject
normal checks      + incident
The expected fingerprint must itself be protected against unauthorized modification. If an attacker can modify both the artifact and its stored fingerprint, fingerprint comparison alone cannot establish authenticity.

The production implementation must use a suitable established integrity mechanism and protect the trusted verification metadata.

Provider API credentials must not be embedded in voice policies, the knowledge graph, experiment records, logs, research memory, or client payloads. They should be retrieved through the application's established secret-management system and supplied only to authorized consumers. 
*voice TTS.txt

13. Secure the external TTS provider boundary
provider.py
The TTS adapter is a security boundary between Aariya and an external provider.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProviderResponseExpectation:
    content_type: str
    sample_rate_hz: int
    voice_configuration_reference: str
    streaming_required: bool

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.sample_rate_hz <= 0:
            raise ValueError("Sample rate must be positive")
        if not self.content_type.strip():
            raise ValueError("Content type is required")
        if not self.voice_configuration_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("Voice configuration is required")


class ProviderResponseValidator(Protocol):
    async def validate(
        self,
        response: object,
        expectation: ProviderResponseExpectation,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Validate the actual provider response and expected configuration.
        """
        ...

The real provider adapter should enforce authenticated connections, appropriate TLS transport, credential isolation, request validation, response validation, timeout and cancellation handling, rate controls, and provider identity verification. It should also reject or safely handle unexpected audio formats, sample rates, metadata, response structures, and voice configurations. 
*voice TTS.txt

The actual checks must match the provider Aariya uses. This interface does not itself establish secure transport or validate a provider response.

14. Detect security anomalies
anomaly.py

Unexpected access to raw audio.

Unusual export volume.

Unexpected provider destinations.

Repeated authorization failures.

Policy modifications outside the expected workflow.

Unexpected changes to research artifacts.

Unusual credential usage.

An unexpected component accessing sensitive profiles. 
*voice TTS.txt

Implement the detector as an interface to the real monitoring and policy system:

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol, Sequence

    async def detect(
        self,
        event_stream: Sequence[object],
    ) -> Sequence[object]:
        """
        Analyze actual security events and return evidence-backed
        anomaly findings.
        """
        ...

Do not create artificial security events or fabricate anomaly confidence. Each finding should reference the events that support it.

15. Create auditable security incidents
incident.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


class IncidentSeverity(str, Enum):
    LOW = "low"
    MEDIUM = "medium"
    HIGH = "high"
    CRITICAL = "critical"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SecurityIncident:
    id: UUID
    severity: IncidentSeverity
    category: str
    description: str
    evidence_ids: tuple[UUID, ...]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        severity: IncidentSeverity,
        category: str,
        description: str,
        evidence_ids: tuple[UUID, ...],
    ) -> "SecurityIncident":
        if not category.strip():
            raise ValueError("Incident category is required")
        if not description.strip():
            raise ValueError("Incident description is required")
        if not evidence_ids:
            raise ValueError("Incident evidence is required")

The incident record preserves evidence references instead of embedding sensitive material unnecessarily. Classification and response policies must determine the appropriate severity and action.

16. Respond to incidents without unnecessarily stopping the whole voice system
response.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class SecurityResponseController(Protocol):
    async def respond(
        self,
        incident: object,
        context: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Execute the authorized, incident-specific response.
        """
        ...

The actual response system may need to deny a request, revoke temporary access, terminate a provider connection, disable an affected capability, isolate a component, freeze policy promotion, invalidate an artifact, roll back, or escalate. The selected response must be supported by the infrastructure and appropriate to the incident. 
*voice TTS.txt

Two examples from the notes illustrate different priorities:

Unauthorized raw-audio access

Unauthorized READ request
        ↓
DENY
        ↓
AUDIT
        ↓
ANALYZE PATTERN
        ↓
INCIDENT IF WARRANTED
The unauthorized access should be denied, but normal speech processing may continue if the affected security boundary is isolated and the remaining system is safe.

Modified voice identity model

Identity model
      ↓
Integrity verification fails
      ↓
Reject compromised model
      ↓
Restore last verified version
      ↓
Record incident
A compromised identity model must not continue to be used merely to preserve availability. The system should use only a validated recovery path.

17. Record security audit events
audit.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceSecurityAuditEvent:
    actor: str
    action: str
    asset_id: str
    result: str
    timestamp: datetime
    request_id: str

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        actor: str,
        action: str,
        asset_id: str,
        result: str,
        request_id: str,
    ) -> "VoiceSecurityAuditEvent":
        required = (actor, action, asset_id, result, request_id)

        if not all(value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError("Audit event fields are required")

        return cls(
            actor=actor,
            action=action,
            asset_id=asset_id,
            result=result,
            timestamp=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
            request_id=request_id,

The source requires the security records to capture who performed the action, what happened, when it happened, which asset was affected, the permission, component, session, decision, and reason. The audit trail itself must be protected from unauthorized modification. 
*voice TTS.txt

This is a basic event model. The complete audit implementation should add the remaining context and persist it through a suitably protected audit sink.

19. Coordinate security-sensitive operations
controller.py
This scaffold demonstrates two important operations: access authorization with an audit record and integrity verification before an artifact is trusted.

from __future__ import annotations

from .asset import VoiceAsset
from .audit import VoiceSecurityAuditEvent
from .permission import VoicePermission


class VoiceSecurityController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        authorization,
        integrity,
        anomaly_detector,
        incident_responder,
        repository,
    ) -> None:
        self._authorization = authorization
        self._integrity = integrity
        self._anomaly = anomaly_detector
        self._response = incident_responder
        self._repository = repository

    async def authorize(
        self,
        principal,
        asset: VoiceAsset,
        permission: VoicePermission,
        request_id: str,
    ) -> bool:
        try:
            allowed = await self._authorization.authorize(
                principal,
                asset,
                permission,
            )
        except Exception:

Integration requirements:

Call the authorization controller before each sensitive operation, not only when initially opening a session.

Treat authorization failure as denial. The caller must not proceed when authorization returns False.

Handle audit-storage failure through an explicit security policy; do not silently continue sensitive operations if required auditing cannot be guaranteed.

When integrity verification fails, stop use of the affected artifact and route the event into the incident process.

Use established service identities and credentials. The principal object must come from verified authentication, not arbitrary caller-supplied data.

The source's security controller follows this general orchestration pattern. 
*voice TTS.txt

20. Integrate security with Aariya's autonomous improvement system
Phase 49 is especially important because Phases 46–48 introduce capability evolution, architecture optimization, and automated recovery.

Subsystem

Security requirement

Phase 45 — Identity Guard

Protect identity representations and reject unverified modifications

Phase 46 — Capability Evolution

Validate candidate sources, package integrity, dependencies, and sandbox behavior

Phase 47 — Architecture Optimization

Include privacy boundaries and security-critical dependencies in architecture impact analysis

Phase 48 — Reliability

Distinguish technical failures from security failures; do not endlessly retry invalid credentials

Phase 30 — Research

Keep private user data, credentials, and internal secrets out of arbitrary external research requests

Phase 31 — Knowledge

Separate security knowledge from voice-performance knowledge, linking only the information necessary for the purpose

These are explicit connections in the Phase 49 notes. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

For autonomous capability evolution, the guarded lifecycle becomes:

CAPABILITY GAP
      ↓
RESEARCH
      ↓
CANDIDATE
      ↓
SECURITY VALIDATION
      ↓
COMPATIBILITY CHECK
      ↓
SANDBOX
      ↓
CALIBRATION
      ↓
EXPERIMENT
      ↓
IDENTITY / QUALITY VALIDATION
      ↓
CANARY
      ↓
GOVERNED PROMOTION
A candidate that improves latency but weakens data isolation must be rejected. Research content must also remain untrusted input: it should not gain access to private audio, user profiles, credentials, or internal policy secrets.

21. Phase 49 completion criteria
Before considering this phase operational, verify the following against the actual Aariya deployment:

Protected voice assets are identified and classified.

Authentication and per-operation authorization work with real identities.

Data flows and external boundaries are mapped.

Data access is minimized according to purpose.

Actual retention policies are enforced.

Encryption and credential management are integrated.

Secrets are excluded from logs and model reasoning payloads.

Identity models, policies, and research provenance are integrity-checked.

Provider responses and access patterns are validated.

Security anomalies produce evidence-backed findings.

Incident containment and recovery have been tested.

User-specific profiles remain isolated.

Security and voice-performance knowledge remain logically separated.

Security, privacy, identity, and rollback gates are part of capability and architecture promotion.

The source lists these as Phase 49's completion goals. They are criteria to demonstrate—not functions that become operational simply by creating the files above. 
*voice TTS.txt


Phase 50 — Autonomous Voice Governance & Change Control
Phase 49 protects Aariya's voice system through security, privacy, and integrity controls. Phase 50 determines who—or which subsystem—is authorized to change it, what evidence each change requires, and how approval, deployment, and rollback are controlled. 
*voice TTS.txt

This is a critical boundary in your architecture. Aariya can have research agents, a voice optimizer, capability evolution, architecture optimization, reliability recovery, prediction, and social adaptation, but those components must not all have unrestricted authority.

For example, an optimizer might discover a voice configuration that improves naturalness while the identity guard reports unacceptable identity drift. Governance must reject that change rather than allow the optimizer to override the identity guard.

The fundamental rule is:

A subsystem may discover and propose an improvement without automatically receiving permission to activate it.

1. Complete governance architecture
Observation and diagnosis

Real system behavior, measurements, incidents and identified improvement opportunities

Change proposal

Target, reason, evidence, risk, expected effects and rollback plan

Evidence gate

Does the proposal have the required evidence?

Authority gate

Is this actor allowed to perform this action?

Risk assessment and conflict resolution

Security, privacy, identity, safety, integrity and operational requirements take precedence over optional optimization.

Approval and signed change

Required approvals, protected version history, and an auditable decision

Sandbox → canary → validation

Only an authorized deployment mechanism may proceed to activation.

Approved

Activate within the permitted scope.

Rejected or escalated

Record the reason; preserve the current validated state.

This follows the source's observation-to-proposal-to-governance-to-deployment flow. 
*voice TTS.txt

2. Create the governance subsystem
Add the directory defined in your TTS notes:

├── action.py
├── risk.py
├── policy.py
├── proposal.py
├── evidence.py
├── approval.py
├── quorum.py
├── conflict.py
├── dependency.py
├── change.py
├── version.py
├── signature.py
├── audit.py
├── escalation.py
├── rollback.py
├── lifecycle.py
├── repository.py
└── controller.py
The modules separate decision-making authority, risk and policy requirements, approval, change records, signatures, lifecycle management, and durable audit history. 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Define explicit autonomy levels
authority.py
Your notes define these authority levels. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum


class AuthorityLevel(str, Enum):
    OBSERVE = "observe"
    ANALYZE = "analyze"
    PROPOSE = "propose"
    EXPERIMENT = "experiment"
    CANARY = "canary"
    ACTIVATE = "activate"
    ADMIN = "admin"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Authority:
    principal_id: str
    level: AuthorityLevel
    scope: tuple[str, ...]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.principal_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Principal ID is required")

        if not self.scope:
            raise ValueError("Authority scope is required")

The scope is just as important as the level.

A voice optimizer might be permitted to propose prosody changes and run isolated experiments while remaining unauthorized to activate production architecture changes. An identity-boundary modification should not be accessible to the ordinary optimization workflow.

The levels represent permitted action classes, not a general guarantee that every action at a lower level is safe.

role.py
from enum import Enum


class GovernanceRole(str, Enum):
    OBSERVER = "observer"
    ANALYST = "analyst"
    RESEARCHER = "researcher"
    EXPERIMENTER = "experimenter"
    DEPLOYER = "deployer"
    SYSTEM_ADMINISTRATOR = "system_administrator"
    HUMAN_REVIEWER = "human_reviewer"

These roles come from the source notes. Map authenticated principals to roles using Aariya's established identity and access-management infrastructure. Do not allow a subsystem to assign itself a more powerful role.

4. Define which actions are governed
action.py
from enum import Enum


class GovernedAction(str, Enum):
    UPDATE_POLICY = "update_policy"
    CHANGE_CAPABILITY = "change_capability"
    CHANGE_ARCHITECTURE = "change_architecture"
    CHANGE_PROVIDER = "change_provider"
    CHANGE_CALIBRATION = "change_calibration"
    CHANGE_IDENTITY_BOUNDARY = "change_identity_boundary"
    RETIRE_COMPONENT = "retire_component"
    CHANGE_SECURITY_POLICY = "change_security_policy"

The important distinction is that changing a delivery parameter is not equivalent to changing Aariya's identity boundary, security policy, or provider credentials.

Your notes specifically identify identity-boundary and security-policy changes as especially restricted actions. 
*voice TTS.txt

5. Classify risk before permitting a change
risk.py
from enum import Enum


class ChangeRisk(str, Enum):
    LOW = "low"
    MODERATE = "moderate"
    HIGH = "high"
    CRITICAL = "critical"

The risk assessment should account for:

Blast radius and affected dependencies.

Identity, privacy, and security impact.

Latency and provider impact.

Reversibility.

Quality and strength of the supporting evidence.

These factors are specified in Phase 50. 
*voice TTS.txt

Risk must be determined by the governance risk assessor using evidence. It should not be accepted merely because the proposal creator labels a change LOW.

6. Define a centralized governance policy
policy.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Protocol

from .action import GovernedAction
from .authority import AuthorityLevel
from .risk import ChangeRisk


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GovernancePolicy:
    action: GovernedAction
    maximum_autonomous_risk: ChangeRisk
    required_evidence: tuple[str, ...]
    required_authority: AuthorityLevel
    human_review_required: bool
    reversible_required: bool

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.required_evidence:
            raise ValueError(
                "Governance policy must specify required evidence"
            )


class GovernancePolicyResolver(Protocol):
    async def resolve(
        self,
        action: GovernedAction,
        risk: ChangeRisk,
    ) -> GovernancePolicy:
        """
        Resolve the authoritative policy for this action and risk.
        """
        ...

The actual policy resolver must use a protected, versioned policy registry. The dataclass is only the contract; it does not encode your production risk thresholds or approval rules.

The source's core policy distinction is:

Change type

Governance direction

Minor validated prosody adjustment

May be eligible for autonomous handling

Production architecture change

Stronger evidence and review

Identity-boundary change

Highest governance restriction

Security policy change

Restricted authority; ordinary optimization cannot override it

*voice TTS.txt
7. Every change begins as a proposal
proposal.py
from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import datetime, timezone

from .action import GovernedAction
from .risk import ChangeRisk


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ChangeProposal:
    id: UUID

    description: str

    evidence_ids: tuple[UUID, ...]
    risk: ChangeRisk
    rollback_version: str
    created_at: datetime

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        action: GovernedAction,
        target: str,
        description: str,
        reason: str,
        evidence_ids: tuple[UUID, ...],
        risk: ChangeRisk,
        rollback_version: str,
    ) -> "ChangeProposal":
        if not target.strip():
            raise ValueError("Change target is required")
        if not description.strip():

This creates a formal boundary between:

“I think this change would help.”

“The change has been authorized and is permitted to proceed.”

The additional target and rollback_version fields make the intended target and recovery point explicit. They are implementation refinements of the source's proposal model.

8. Require evidence appropriate to the risk
evidence.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol

from .policy import GovernancePolicy
from .proposal import ChangeProposal


class GovernanceEvidenceValidator(Protocol):
    async def validate(
        self,
        proposal: ChangeProposal,
        evidence: tuple[object, ...],
        policy: GovernancePolicy,
    ) -> bool:
        """
        Verify evidence references, provenance, completeness,
        relevance and the policy's required evidence categories.
        """
        ...

For a high-impact architecture change, the required evidence might include real runtime observations, controlled experiment results, identity evaluation, security/privacy review, and a rollback plan.

The actual requirements must be resolved from the action class and risk—not hardcoded into every voice subsystem. 
*voice TTS.txt

A proposal with missing or unverifiable evidence should be rejected or escalated, not treated as approved by default.

9. Record approval decisions
approval.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ApprovalDecision:
    id: UUID
    proposal_id: UUID
    principal_id: str
    approved: bool
    reason: str
    decided_at: datetime

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        proposal_id: UUID,
        principal_id: str,
        approved: bool,
        reason: str,
    ) -> "ApprovalDecision":
        if not principal_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Approver identity is required")
        if not reason.strip():
            raise ValueError("Decision reason is required")

        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            proposal_id=proposal_id,
            principal_id=principal_id,
            approved=approved,
            reason=reason,
            decided_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc),

The approver's identity must come from an authenticated decision process. A free-form principal ID supplied by the proposal creator is not sufficient proof of an approval.

10. Implement independent approval requirements
quorum.py
Some changes require multiple independent approvals. The policy determines what is required, and the quorum evaluator verifies whether it has been satisfied.

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol
from uuid import UUID

from .approval import ApprovalDecision
from .policy import GovernancePolicy


class ApprovalQuorum(Protocol):
    async def evaluate(
        self,
        proposal_id: UUID,
        approvals: tuple[ApprovalDecision, ...],
        policy: GovernancePolicy,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Evaluate approval requirements, including independence
        and any applicable human-review requirements.
        """
        ...

The actual implementation needs to check approver identity, permission, independence, decision validity, and the requirements of the applicable governance policy.

A majority vote must not override a hard security or identity constraint. That is a separate responsibility of conflict resolution.

11. Resolve conflicting autonomous subsystems
conflict.py
Aariya's autonomous components may disagree. The optimizer might want more expressive pitch movement, social adaptation might recommend restraint, the identity guard might reject the candidate, and reliability might report that the provider is unstable.

The source defines this priority order. 
*voice TTS.txt

1
Security

2
Privacy

3
Identity

4
Safety

5
System integrity

6
Communicative requirements

7
Performance objectives

8
Optional optimization

conflict.py interface
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class GovernanceConflictResolver(Protocol):
    async def resolve(
        self,
        decisions: tuple[object, ...],
        authority_rules: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Resolve conflicts by enforcing hard constraints and
        authoritative policy—not by majority vote.
        """
        ...

The implementation should distinguish hard vetoes from ordinary preferences. A prediction that a change will improve naturalness cannot grant permission to violate identity boundaries, security rules, or privacy constraints.

12. Keep a complete change and version history

13. Protect change records with signatures
signature.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class ChangeSignatureService(Protocol):
    async def sign(self, change: object) -> object:
        ...

    async def verify(
        self,
        change: object,
        signature: object,
    ) -> bool:
        ...

Connect this to Aariya's established cryptographic infrastructure. Do not implement signing algorithms manually.

The signed material should cover a canonical representation of the change and its relevant version or provenance metadata, so that modification of the signed content can be detected. The signing key and verification trust must be managed separately from the proposal author. 
*voice TTS.txt

14. Audit decisions from proposal to outcome
audit.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GovernanceAuditEvent:
    proposal_id: str
    action: str
    principal: str
    decision: str
    timestamp: datetime

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        proposal_id: str,
        action: str,
        principal: str,
        decision: str,
    ) -> "GovernanceAuditEvent":
        required = (proposal_id, action, principal, decision)

        if not all(value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError("Audit event fields are required")

        return cls(
            proposal_id=proposal_id,
            action=action,
            principal=principal,
            decision=decision,
            timestamp=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
        )

The complete audit trail should connect the proposal, evidence, risk assessment, approvals, change, deployment, validation, outcome, and any rollback. The source emphasizes preserving this chain so you can later determine exactly why a change was made and who or what authorized it. 
*voice TTS.txt

The event model above is a starting point; the production audit system needs protected persistence and references to the full change lifecycle.

15. Abstain when governance cannot safely decide

        ...

Insufficient evidence, unresolved authority conflicts, missing required approvals, or an unclear security/identity impact should trigger rejection or escalation rather than a guessed approval.

The source explicitly prefers escalation when governance cannot safely decide.

18. Connect the governance controller
controller.py
The controller below shows the central decision boundary. It resolves the applicable policy and checks evidence before considering approval. Production activation remains the responsibility of the authorized deployment mechanism.

from __future__ import annotations

from .proposal import ChangeProposal


class VoiceGovernanceController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        policy_resolver,
        evidence_validator,
        authority_service,
        quorum,
        conflict_resolver,
        signature_service,
        repository,
        escalator,
    ) -> None:
        self._policy = policy_resolver
        self._evidence = evidence_validator
        self._authority = authority_service
        self._quorum = quorum
        self._conflicts = conflict_resolver
        self._signature = signature_service
        self._repository = repository
        self._escalator = escalator

    async def evaluate_proposal(
        self,
        proposal: ChangeProposal,
        evidence: tuple[object, ...],
        principal: object,
    ):
        await self._repository.save_proposal(proposal)

        policy = await self._policy.resolve(
            proposal.action,

Integration note: authority_service, conflict_result, and the quorum return type are integration contracts in this example; their concrete interfaces must be defined in your codebase. The controller is not a complete deployment implementation. Before issuing a deployment authorization, the real system must also verify required approvals, security and identity decisions, policy versions, signatures, change state, and rollback availability.

This follows the source's central governance controller pattern of policy resolution, evidence validation, approval evaluation, and escalation. 
*voice TTS.txt

19. What governance means for Aariya's other subsystems
Subsystem

Governance boundary

Phase 41 — Prediction

A likely benefit does not grant authority to execute a change

Phase 45 — Identity Guard

Changes to identity boundaries require exceptionally strong evidence and approval

Phase 46 — Capability Evolution

Discovery does not mean a new capability is authorized for integration

Phase 47 — Architecture Optimization

Architecture changes must pass risk assessment, approval, testing, and canary validation

Phase 48 — Reliability

A recovery decision must be authorized; a technical failure cannot justify an uncontrolled provider migration

Phase 49 — Security

Security restrictions can veto a technically beneficial proposal

These authority relationships are explicit in the Phase 50 notes. 
*voice TTS.txt

Example: low-risk prosody adjustment
If actual experiments repeatedly demonstrate that a small pause adjustment improves naturalness, remains identity-safe, is reversible, and satisfies the configured evidence requirements, governance may allow an autonomous canary and potentially automatic promotion.

That is only allowed when the applicable governance policy explicitly permits it.

Example: major architecture change
Replacing a synchronization subsystem has a much larger blast radius. Governance should require stronger evidence, an isolated test, appropriate review, canary validation, and a verified rollback path.

Example: identity or security boundary change
These should not be treated as ordinary optimization. Improving expressiveness alone is not sufficient justification to expand Aariya's identity envelope, and ordinary voice optimization must not be able to rewrite security policy.

*voice TTS.txt
20. Governance must not silently grant itself more authority
The source permits a carefully controlled progression in autonomy: a low-risk change class may become eligible for autonomous activation after repeated validated success, but expanding Aariya's authority must itself be a governed change. Aariya cannot independently grant herself new permissions. 
*voice TTS.txt

The governance self-model can describe which operations are autonomous, which are proposal-only, and which remain restricted. This makes the authority boundaries inspectable rather than implicit.

Suggested governance metrics include proposal volume, approval and rejection rates, escalation rate, rollback rate, change failure rate, unauthorized-action attempts, review time, and post-change regressions. These metrics should come from real persisted governance events. 
*voice TTS.txt

21. Phase 50 completion criteria
Before calling Phase 50 operational, verify that Aariya can:

Enforce scope-bound authority for each governed action.

Resolve a protected policy for the change type and risk.

Validate evidence before granting permission to proceed.

Require the appropriate approval and independence checks.

Resolve conflicts using hard constraints rather than majority voting.

Preserve version and signature integrity.

Record the complete proposal-to-outcome audit chain.

Reject or escalate proposals when evidence or authority is insufficient.

Run sandbox and canary stages before permitted activation.

Restore a known-good version when rollback is required.

Prevent prediction, optimization, capability evolution, and reliability recovery from overriding governance.

Keep security and identity restrictions outside the authority of ordinary performance optimizers.


Phase 51 — Autonomous Voice Observability, Causal Telemetry & Digital Twin
Phase 50 gave Aariya governance over changes. Phase 51 gives Aariya a unified operational view of the voice system, built from actual runtime telemetry.

Your notes describe the problem: valuable information is distributed across speech recognition, conversation understanding, internal state, voice strategy, policy, prosody, TTS, streaming, synchronization, identity, reliability, security, and user outcomes. Phase 51 connects those events so Aariya can reconstruct what happened during an interaction, identify possible contributors to an outcome, and track where its understanding of the system remains uncertain. 
*voice TTS.txt

The distinction is important:

Observability establishes what actually happened.

Causal analysis investigates which events may have contributed to an outcome.

The digital twin models the real architecture and observed behavior.

Simulation estimates what might happen under a proposed condition.

Phase 50 governance remains responsible for authorizing production changes.

The digital twin must never confuse a prediction with an observation or independently modify production. 
*voice TTS.txt
 
*voice TTS.txt

1. The complete observability architecture
Real voice interaction

User input, Aariya's decisions, generated audio, playback and actual outcome

Distributed traces, spans and events

Correlated using trace, session, turn and generation references

Unified timeline

Reconstruct decisions, component execution, failures, recovery and user response

Causal analysis

Candidate contributors and evidence

Anomaly detection

Unexpected runtime behavior

Evidence-backed digital twin

Architecture, current state, dependencies, historical behavior and uncertainty

Explain, predict and compare

Provide evidence to diagnosis, planning, reliability, capability evolution and governance

This is based on the source's Phase 51 architecture and unified causal timeline. 
*voice TTS.txt

3. Define a trace for each meaningful interaction
trace.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Mapping
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceTrace:
    id: UUID
    session_id: UUID
    started_at: datetime
    metadata: Mapping[str, str]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        session_id: UUID,
        metadata: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
    ) -> "VoiceTrace":
        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            session_id=session_id,
            started_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
            metadata=dict(metadata or {}),
        )

A trace represents one meaningful interaction flow. Keep metadata to non-sensitive identifiers and references; do not copy entire transcripts, raw audio, credentials, or private profiles into every trace.

context.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from uuid import UUID


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TraceContext:
    trace_id: UUID
    session_id: UUID
    turn_id: UUID | None
    generation_id: UUID | None

Pass this context through the major components involved in a response. It provides the correlation keys that connect user input to context processing, strategy resolution, TTS, playback, and recovery. 
*voice TTS.txt

4. Record component execution with spans
span.py
A span records a specific operation within a trace, such as ASR decoding, strategy resolution, prosodic composition, TTS streaming, playback startup, or identity evaluation.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from uuid import UUID


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceSpan:
    trace_id: UUID
    span_id: UUID
    component: str
    operation: str
    started_at: datetime
    finished_at: datetime | None
    status: str
    metadata: dict[str, str]
    parent_span_id: UUID | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.component.strip():
            raise ValueError("Component is required")
        if not self.operation.strip():
            raise ValueError("Operation is required")

        if self.started_at.tzinfo is None:
            raise ValueError("Start timestamp must be timezone-aware")

        if self.finished_at is not None:
            if self.finished_at.tzinfo is None:
                raise ValueError("Finish timestamp must be timezone-aware")
            if self.finished_at < self.started_at:
                raise ValueError("Span finishes before it starts")

    @property
    def duration_ms(self) -> float | None:

Correlated spans can show how much time each component took and whether it was on the path to the first audio chunk. For asynchronous operations, preserve their parent/child or other explicit trace relationships rather than assuming that all operations execute sequentially.

5. Model important runtime events
event.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceEvent:
    id: UUID
    trace_id: UUID
    event_type: str
    timestamp: datetime
    source: str
    payload_reference: str | None

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        trace_id: UUID,
        event_type: str,
        timestamp: datetime,
        source: str,
        payload_reference: str | None = None,
    ) -> "VoiceEvent":
        if timestamp.tzinfo is None:
            raise ValueError("Event timestamp must be timezone-aware")
        if not event_type.strip():
            raise ValueError("Event type is required")
        if not source.strip():
            raise ValueError("Event source is required")

        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            trace_id=trace_id,
            event_type=event_type,

Useful event types from your notes include:

USER_SPEECH_STARTED
USER_SPEECH_ENDED
ASR_FINALIZED
CONTEXT_READY
STRATEGY_SELECTED

USER_INTERRUPTED
TTS_FAILED

RESPONSE_COMPLETED

These events should be emitted by the real components at the time the corresponding action occurs, not generated later from assumptions. 
*voice TTS.txt

6. Build the unified interaction timeline
timeline.py
A unified timeline brings events and spans into one ordered view. It helps distinguish where time was spent from what happened during that time.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Sequence

from .event import VoiceEvent
from .span import VoiceSpan
from .trace import VoiceTrace


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TimelineEntry:
    timestamp: datetime
    offset_ms: float
    entry_type: str
    name: str
    source: str
    duration_ms: float | None
    status: str | None
    evidence_reference: str | None


class CausalTimelineBuilder:
    async def build(
        self,
        trace: VoiceTrace,
        spans: Sequence[VoiceSpan],
        events: Sequence[VoiceEvent],
    ) -> tuple[TimelineEntry, ...]:
        entries: list[TimelineEntry] = []

        for span in spans:
            if span.trace_id != trace.id:
                raise ValueError("Span belongs to another trace")


This implementation provides a basic ordered reconstruction. A production timeline builder should also handle clock synchronization across services, late-arriving events, missing spans, duplicate delivery, and incomplete traces.

The source's illustrative timeline includes speech start and end, ASR finalization, context readiness, strategy decisions, TTS startup, first audio, playback, interruption, and generation cancellation. The timestamps must come from actual runtime telemetry. 
*voice TTS.txt

7. Capture system snapshots at important boundaries
snapshot.py
A trace records the interaction. A snapshot records the relevant system state at a point in that interaction.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Mapping


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceSystemSnapshot:
    trace_id: str
    timestamp: datetime
    internal_state: Mapping[str, object]
    policy_version: str
    provider_configuration: Mapping[str, object]
    capability_state: Mapping[str, object]
    reliability_state: Mapping[str, object]
    identity_state: Mapping[str, object]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        trace_id: str,
        internal_state: Mapping[str, object],
        policy_version: str,
        provider_configuration: Mapping[str, object],
        capability_state: Mapping[str, object],
        reliability_state: Mapping[str, object],
        identity_state: Mapping[str, object],
    ) -> "VoiceSystemSnapshot":
        if not trace_id.strip():
            raise ValueError("Trace ID is required")
        if not policy_version.strip():
            raise ValueError("Policy version is required")

        return cls(
            trace_id=trace_id,

Snapshots should be captured at useful lifecycle boundaries—for example, when a voice strategy is selected, TTS begins, a fallback is selected, or a response completes.

Privacy constraint: the model above does not automatically redact sensitive data. Integrate Phase 49's minimization and authorization services before persisting snapshots. Prefer references and minimized representations to indiscriminately copying complete user profiles, transcripts, credentials, or raw audio. 
*voice TTS.txt

8. Record measurements with their provenance
metrics.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from math import isfinite


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TelemetryMetric:
    name: str
    value: float
    unit: str
    source: str
    timestamp: datetime
    evidence_reference: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.name.strip() or not self.unit.strip():
            raise ValueError("Metric name and unit are required")
        if not self.source.strip():
            raise ValueError("Metric source is required")
        if not isfinite(self.value):
            raise ValueError("Metric must be finite")
        if self.timestamp.tzinfo is None:
            raise ValueError("Timestamp must be timezone-aware")
        if not self.evidence_reference.strip():
            raise ValueError("Evidence reference is required")

The source lists potential measurements such as first-audio latency, TTS latency, actual pitch range, speech rate, buffer depth, queue wait, identity distance, interruption rate, and recovery time. These measurements should come from real instrumentation, and their units and collection methods must be defined consistently. 
*voice TTS.txt

9. Distinguish correlation from causation
causal_edge.py
This is one of the most important parts of Phase 51. If TTS latency increases before a user interruption, the sequence is evidence of temporal ordering—but it does not, by itself, prove that the latency caused the interruption.

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from uuid import UUID, uuid4


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CausalEdge:
    id: UUID
    source_event: UUID
    target_event: UUID
    relationship: str
    confidence: float
    evidence_references: tuple[str, ...]

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        source_event: UUID,
        target_event: UUID,
        relationship: str,
        confidence: float,
        evidence_references: tuple[str, ...],
    ) -> "CausalEdge":
        if not 0.0 <= confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Confidence must be between 0 and 1")
        if not relationship.strip():
            raise ValueError("Relationship is required")
        if not evidence_references:
            raise ValueError("Supporting evidence is required")

        return cls(
            id=uuid4(),
            source_event=source_event,
            target_event=target_event,
            relationship=relationship,

The relationship field should distinguish a temporal association, a proposed causal relationship, and a relationship supported by stronger causal evidence. The numeric confidence alone does not prove causality.

class VoiceEventCorrelator(Protocol):
    async def correlate(
        self,
        events: Sequence[object],
        metrics: Sequence[object],
        context: object,
    ) -> Sequence[object]:
        """
        Identify evidence-backed relationships between voice behavior,
        system state and user response.
        """
        ...

For example, the system might observe this sequence:

TTS latency increases
        ↓
First audio is delayed
        ↓
User interrupts
That sequence becomes a candidate explanation to investigate—not an automatic conclusion about why the user interrupted. 
*voice TTS.txt

12. Build the evidence-backed digital twin
state.py
The digital twin must distinguish observations, derived values, inferences, predictions, and unknown values. The source explicitly requires that unknown remains unknown instead of being filled with an assumption. 
*voice TTS.txt

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime
from enum import Enum


class StateOrigin(str, Enum):
    OBSERVED = "observed"
    DERIVED = "derived"
    INFERRED = "inferred"
    PREDICTED = "predicted"
    UNKNOWN = "unknown"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TwinStateValue:
    value: object
    origin: StateOrigin
    confidence: float
    last_observed_at: datetime | None
    evidence_reference: str | None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 0.0 <= self.confidence <= 1.0:
            raise ValueError("Confidence must be between 0 and 1")

        if self.origin == StateOrigin.UNKNOWN and self.value is not None:
            raise ValueError("Unknown values must not assert a value")

        if (
            self.last_observed_at is not None
            and self.last_observed_at.tzinfo is None
        ):
            raise ValueError("Observation timestamp must be timezone-aware")

last_observed_at is the last observation time, not the prediction time. Prediction timestamps and model versions should be recorded separately in the simulation and model-validation layers.

digital_twin.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping

from .state import TwinStateValue


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VoiceDigitalTwin:
    architecture_version: str
    policy_version: str
    provider_state: Mapping[str, TwinStateValue]
    capability_state: Mapping[str, TwinStateValue]
    component_state: Mapping[str, TwinStateValue]
    observed_metrics: Mapping[str, TwinStateValue]
    active_constraints: Mapping[str, TwinStateValue]
    model_version: str

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        required = (
            self.architecture_version,
            self.policy_version,
            self.model_version,
        )
        if not all(value.strip() for value in required):
            raise ValueError("Twin version references are required")

The twin represents the real architecture, its current state, dependencies, observed behavior, and historical behavior. It should make it possible to ask questions such as, “What could be affected if this component becomes unavailable?” or “What evidence explains the increase in first-audio latency?” 
*voice TTS.txt

The state model alone does not populate the twin. A real state reconciler must combine fresh telemetry, the Phase 47 topology, current policy and provider configurations, capability state, and identity/reliability evidence.

13. Keep simulation separate from real results
simulation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Protocol


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DigitalTwinSimulationResult:
    mode: str
    model_version: str
    change_reference: str
    predicted_effects: object
    uncertainty: object
    created_at: datetime

    @classmethod
    def create(
        cls,
        model_version: str,
        change_reference: str,
        predicted_effects: object,
        uncertainty: object,
    ) -> "DigitalTwinSimulationResult":
        return cls(
            mode="simulated",
            model_version=model_version,
            change_reference=change_reference,
            predicted_effects=predicted_effects,
            uncertainty=uncertainty,
            created_at=datetime.now(timezone.utc),
        )


class VoiceDigitalTwinSimulator(Protocol):
    async def simulate(

The result explicitly identifies itself as simulated. That marker must be preserved through storage, reporting, and learning.

The twin may estimate the effects of increased TTS latency or a proposed architecture change, but it cannot claim that the real system definitely behaves in that way. The actual experiment remains authoritative. 
*voice TTS.txt

validation.py
from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Protocol


class DigitalTwinValidator(Protocol):
    async def validate(
        self,
        twin: object,
        real_telemetry: object,
    ) -> object:
        """
        Compare predictions and modeled state against actual observations.
        """
        ...

Suppose the twin predicts that higher provider latency will delay first audio, but real buffering absorbs that delay. The discrepancy should become model-correction evidence; the system should not quietly alter the actual measurements to match the prediction.

The twin also needs health monitoring for prediction accuracy, state freshness, missing telemetry, stale dependencies, unverified assumptions, and model error.

The storage approach in your notes is:

Redis: short-lived runtime coordination when needed.

PostgreSQL: durable traces, events, decisions, and audit records.

Specialized telemetry infrastructure: introduce only when the real workload justifies it.

Retention should vary by data type. High-volume operational metrics, experiment evidence, governance audit records, and raw audio must follow their respective retention policies, with Phase 49 remaining authoritative for data protection.

15. Connect the observability controller
controller.py
This controller coordinates reconstruction, anomaly inspection, and twin validation.

from __future__ import annotations

from uuid import UUID


class VoiceObservabilityController:
    def __init__(
        self,
        tracer,
        timeline_builder,
        anomaly_detector,
        outcome_attributor,
        twin_provider,
        twin_validator,
        repository,
    ) -> None:
        self._tracer = tracer
        self._timeline = timeline_builder
        self._anomaly = anomaly_detector
        self._attribution = outcome_attributor
        self._twin_provider = twin_provider
        self._twin_validator = twin_validator
        self._repository = repository

    async def reconstruct(self, trace_id: UUID):
        trace = await self._tracer.get(trace_id)
        spans = await self._tracer.get_spans(trace_id)
        events = await self._tracer.get_events(trace_id)

        timeline = await self._timeline.build(
            trace,
            spans,
            events,
        )

        await self._repository.save_timeline(

The tracer, twin provider, timeline builder, anomaly detector, validator, and repository must all be real implementations. This is a coordination scaffold, not a working observability backend.

The source's controller follows this same reconstruction and anomaly-inspection model, with digital-twin validation as an additional operation. 
*voice TTS.txt

16. What the complete timeline should reveal
An actual session could eventually be reconstructed as follows:

USER SPEECH STARTED
        ↓
USER SPEECH ENDED
        ↓
ASR FINALIZED
        ↓
CONTEXT READY
        ↓
AARIYA STATE / META-DECISION
        ↓
VOICE STRATEGY SELECTED
        ↓
PROSODIC COMPOSITION
        ↓
TTS STARTED
        ↓
FIRST AUDIO DELAYED
        ↓
PROVIDER LATENCY ANOMALY
        ↓
RECOVERY STARTED
        ↓
VALIDATED ALTERNATIVE SELECTED
        ↓
PLAYBACK STARTED
        ↓
USER CONTINUES
The system can then investigate whether the delay was attributable to TTS initialization, network latency, unnecessary synchronous dependencies, buffering, or another factor. It should preserve the distinction between observed timing, candidate explanations, and confirmed causes. 
*voice TTS.txt

18. Phase 51 completion criteria
Before considering this phase operational, verify that Aariya can:

Correlate major voice subsystems through shared trace context.

Reconstruct complete interactions from real spans and events.

Store useful state snapshots without indiscriminately copying sensitive data.

Connect measurements to evidence and configuration versions.

Distinguish temporal correlation from causal hypotheses.

Map observed problems onto the Phase 47 dependency graph.

Build a digital twin grounded in real current and historical telemetry.

Preserve the distinction between observed, derived, inferred, predicted, and unknown state.

Compare simulated effects against real experiments.

Detect stale or incomplete parts of the digital twin.

Keep telemetry retention and access under Phase 49's privacy and security rules.

Require Phase 50 governance before any production change.

These are the verification targets, not claims that the scaffold has passed them. The source defines the digital twin as an evidence-backed operational model that can observe, reconstruct, correlate, diagnose, simulate, predict, and compare—but not independently change production



Phase 53 — Autonomous Voice Regression & Continuous Validation
Phase 52 gave Aariya the ability to generate and validate scenarios based on actual failures and edge cases. Phase 53 makes that validation continuous: whenever you change a voice policy, capability, architecture, provider configuration, calibration, recovery strategy, or adaptation rule, Aariya checks whether previously validated behavior still works. 
*voice TTS.txt

The core flow is:

Change proposal

Select relevant regression contracts

Execute real or controlled validation scenarios

Compare observations with validated baselines

Pass

Eligible for governed canary rollout

Fail or uncertain

Block promotion and investigate

The important difference is that Aariya must not optimize one dimension, such as latency, while silently damaging voice identity, interruption handling, prosody, synchronization, or recovery.

53.1 — Create the module structure
Add this package to your existing backend:

We’ll implement the core validation path first. The remaining files can then connect to your existing Phase 47 dependency graph, Phase 50 governance, Phase 51 telemetry, and Phase 52 scenario runner.

53.2 — Define regression contracts
A contract states what must remain true after a change. For example, the system might require that identity distance remain within an approved limit, cancellation stop active audio, or a provider failure trigger the validated fallback.

Create contract.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum
from typing import Mapping
import math


class MetricDirection(str, Enum):
    LOWER_IS_BETTER = "lower_is_better"
    HIGHER_IS_BETTER = "higher_is_better"
    WITHIN_RANGE = "within_range"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class MetricRule:
    metric: str
    direction: MetricDirection

    # Load this from approved, validated policy.
    # It is not a universal hardcoded threshold.
    max_regression: float

    # Used when the measurement system supplies standard errors.
    # The value must come from the approved evaluation policy.
    confidence_z: float

    min_value: float | None = None
    max_value: float | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.metric.strip():
            raise ValueError("Metric name cannot be empty")

        if not math.isfinite(self.max_regression):
            raise ValueError("max_regression must be finite")

Important: put measurement thresholds in the validated policy configuration, not arbitrary numbers scattered through your source code. Identity, latency, reliability, and naturalness should not all share one threshold strategy. This follows the dimension-specific rules in your Phase 53 design. 
*voice TTS.txt

53.3 — Record actual observations
Create observation.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum
from typing import Mapping
import math

from .contract import RegressionContract


class RegressionStatus(str, Enum):
    PASS = "pass"
    FAIL = "fail"
    INCONCLUSIVE = "inconclusive"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class MetricMeasurement:
    value: float
    unit: str
    sample_count: int
    standard_error: float | None = None

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not math.isfinite(self.value):
            raise ValueError("Measurement value must be finite")

        if self.sample_count < 1:
            raise ValueError("sample_count must be at least 1")

        if self.standard_error is not None:
            if (
                not math.isfinite(self.standard_error)
                or self.standard_error < 0
            ):
                raise ValueError(

The evidence_source field is deliberate. A digital-twin prediction is not an actual observation of Aariya's voice. The executor should label results from actual component execution accordingly, while keeping simulated predictions separate.

53.4 — Store a validated baseline
Create baseline.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping

from .observation import MetricMeasurement


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RegressionBaseline:
    baseline_id: str
    contract_id: str
    scenario_id: str
    version: str

    measurements: Mapping[str, MetricMeasurement]
    environment: Mapping[str, str]

    # Keep versions needed to reproduce the baseline.
    provenance: Mapping[str, str]

    # Identifier of the real validation run that established this baseline.
    validated_run_id: str

The provenance mapping should capture the relevant architecture version, policy version, capability versions, provider configuration, calibration version, scenario version, and measurement version.

Only save a baseline after its validation has passed. Do not create baselines from guessed values, unverified voice samples, or predictions from the digital twin.

53.5 — Compare the baseline with the candidate
Create comparator.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from .baseline import RegressionBaseline
from .contract import (
    MetricDirection,
    RegressionContract,
)
from .observation import (
    ContractComparison,
    MetricComparison,
    RegressionObservation,
    RegressionStatus,
)


class RegressionComparator:

    async def compare(
        self,
        contract: RegressionContract,
        baseline: RegressionBaseline,
        candidate: RegressionObservation,
    ) -> ContractComparison:

        if baseline.contract_id != contract.contract_id:
            return self._inconclusive(
                contract,
                "Baseline belongs to a different contract",
            )

        if not baseline.validated_run_id:
            return self._inconclusive(
                contract,
                "Baseline has no validated-run provenance",
            )


This comparator is a core scaffold, not a substitute for metric-specific statistical evaluation. For metrics such as naturalness or interruption behavior, use the evaluation approach established by the corresponding subsystem. A missing measurement or invalid baseline should produce an inconclusive result, not a false pass.

53.6 — Select tests based on the change
A change to the TTS adapter should trigger the TTS, streaming, cancellation, audio integrity, identity, synchronization, and relevant latency tests—not just a unit test for the changed file.

Create selector.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Mapping, Sequence

from .contract import RegressionContract


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ChangeProposal:
    change_id: str
    changed_components: frozenset[str]
    change_type: str
    known_good_version: str
    already_active: bool = False


class RegressionSelector:

    async def select(
        self,
        change: ChangeProposal,
        dependency_graph: Mapping[str, Sequence[str]],
        regression_suite: Sequence[RegressionContract],
    ) -> tuple[RegressionContract, ...]:

        all_contracts = tuple(regression_suite)

        # Safe default: unknown scope means run the complete suite.
        if not change.changed_components:
            return all_contracts

        known_components = set(dependency_graph)
        for dependencies in dependency_graph.values():
            known_components.update(dependencies)


This is the initial dependency-aware selection algorithm. Later, connect it to the authoritative Phase 47 architecture graph and improve the graph queries if your existing graph includes edge types or richer dependency metadata.

Contracts for identity and security should be configured with always_run=True so unrelated changes cannot accidentally bypass those checks. This is consistent with Phase 53's change-aware selection and dependency-aware regression design. 
*voice TTS.txt

53.7 — Connect the executor to Phase 52
Create executor.py. This module should call your existing scenario execution pathway rather than inventing fake measurements.

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Mapping, Protocol

from .contract import RegressionContract
from .observation import RegressionObservation


class ScenarioRunner(Protocol):
    async def run_scenario(
        self,
        scenario_id: str,
        environment: Mapping[str, str],
    ) -> RegressionObservation:
        """Run the scenario and return measurements from actual execution."""
        ...


class RegressionExecutor:

    def __init__(self, scenario_runner: ScenarioRunner) -> None:
        self._scenario_runner = scenario_runner

    async def execute(
        self,
        contract: RegressionContract,
        environment: Mapping[str, str],
    ) -> RegressionObservation:

        observation = await self._scenario_runner.run_scenario(
            contract.scenario_id,
            environment,
        )

        if observation.scenario_id != contract.scenario_id:
            raise ValueError(

ScenarioRunner is the integration interface; it is not an implementation of your Phase 52 scenario engine. You must connect that interface to the real runner in your codebase.

53.8 — Add the promotion gate
Create promotion.py. The gate permits eligibility for a canary, not unrestricted activation. The existing governance layer must still authorize deployment.

from __future__ import annotations

    # These must be represented by passing contracts in the selected suite.
    REQUIRED_SAFETY_DIMENSIONS = frozenset({
        "identity_consistency",
        "security",
    })

    async def evaluate(
        self,
        change_id: str,

Make sure your suite's dimension labels match the ones in this gate. If your existing system uses different labels, map them explicitly rather than silently skipping a category.

53.10 — Build the regression suite
Create suite.py:

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from .contract import RegressionContract


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RegressionSuite:
    name: str
    contracts: tuple[RegressionContract, ...]
    version: str


def validate_suite(suite: RegressionSuite) -> None:
    contract_ids = [c.contract_id for c in suite.contracts]

    if len(contract_ids) != len(set(contract_ids)):
        raise ValueError("Regression contract IDs must be unique")

    required_safety_dimensions = {
        "identity_consistency",
        "security",
    }

    always_run_dimensions = {
        dimension
        for contract in suite.contracts
        if contract.always_run
        for dimension in contract.dimensions
    }

    missing = required_safety_dimensions - always_run_dimensions

    if missing:
        raise ValueError(

Organize your actual suite by subsystem: identity, voice quality, prosody, interruption, streaming, reliability, provider compatibility, adaptation, social behavior, security, and architecture.

When constructing contracts, bind them to real scenario IDs and approved threshold rules from your configuration and policy repositories. Don't invent scenario identifiers or measurement values to make the suite appear complete.

53.11 — Connect the remaining support modules
Keep the interfaces below consistent with the source roadmap:

Module

Responsibility

dependency.py

Resolve affected contracts using Phase 47's architecture graph.

threshold.py

Load approved, metric-specific thresholds and evaluation settings.

failure.py

Persist a failed contract, metric, reason, and evidence references.

classifier.py

Distinguish genuine regressions from noise, environmental variance, unstable scenarios, test defects, and unknown failures.

triage.py

Correlate a failure with Phase 51's causal telemetry.

quarantine.py

Track unstable tests and require explicit investigation before reactivation.

rollback.py

Restore a known-good version only through the authorized Phase 50 process.

history.py

Retrieve historical pass/fail records and the versions involved.

coverage.py

Map protected behaviors to risks, components, capabilities, and known failures.

repository.py

Persist contracts, baselines, runs, measurements, failures, and audit records.

These are integration responsibilities, not completed implementations simply because the files exist. Your roadmap explicitly requires unstable tests to remain visible, regressions to be investigated causally, and rollback to respect governance. 
*voice TTS.txt

53.12 — Trigger validation on every relevant change
Wire your change events into VoiceRegressionController.validate_change().

Regression should run when any of these changes:

Voice policy, capability, architecture, or a major dependency.

Provider version or configuration, even if Aariya's own source code is unchanged.

Calibration, identity model, adaptation rules, or recovery strategy.

Security policy or a relevant governance rule.

Also schedule periodic regression runs against important validated baselines.

After a successful regression, promotion should proceed through governed canary deployment → monitoring → broader release. Passing an offline suite alone must not automatically activate a candidate.

Add these logical database entities to your existing persistence layer:

regression_contracts
regression_suites
regression_baselines
regression_runs
regression_measurements
regression_failures
regression_triage
regression_quarantine
regression_history
regression_coverage
Each run should be traceable to the relevant scenario, change proposal, architecture and policy versions, capability versions, identity version, provider configuration, and governance decision.

Keep regression-test evidence separate from production user-interaction metrics. Respect the Phase 49 data-minimization and access-control rules when saving logs, audio references, traces, or evaluation artifacts. 
*voice TTS.txt

53.14 — Validate Phase 53 before enabling promotion
Use your existing Phase 52 runner and actual system components to verify these acceptance conditions:

Test

Expected result

All required contracts pass

Candidate becomes eligible for a governed canary.

Identity contract fails

Promotion is blocked.

Security contract fails or is missing

Promotion is blocked.

A required measurement is missing

The result is inconclusive and promotion is blocked.

The baseline is missing or unvalidated

Promotion is blocked.

A dependency changes

Relevant dependent contracts are selected.

The provider changes without a code change

Regression validation is triggered.

A test becomes unstable

It is tracked for investigation, not silently disabled.

Regression history cannot be persisted

The controller fails closed and blocks promotion.

A production release regresses

Investigate and perform rollback only through the authorized governance process.

One implementation detail to preserve: the current controller blocks promotion on inconclusive results, which is appropriate for required contracts. Its history-persistence error also blocks promotion, but you should add a protected operational alert for that failure because the unavailable repository cannot record its own persistence failure.


Phase 54 — Autonomous Voice Quality Benchmarking & Longitudinal Evaluation
Phase 53 protects Aariya's previously validated behavior when something changes. Phase 54 measures whether Aariya is genuinely improving over weeks and months.

It evaluates the evolution of naturalness, conversationality, expressiveness, emotional appropriateness, identity consistency, responsiveness, interruption handling, social appropriateness, reliability, latency, and synchronization. It must also identify when one quality dimension improves at the expense of another. 
*voice TTS.txt

The fundamental difference:

Phase

Main question

53 — Regression

What did this change break?

54 — Longitudinal evaluation

Is Aariya actually getting better over time?
